# Queue

- **The render host keeps a waiting page's answers inline** (2026-10-31, LLP 1048.007 D10): the JavaScript render server names a waiting request page's answers past 1 KB (`/.exact/checkpoint/<hash>.json`) and keeps them; `host/render` (`page.rs`) still writes them into the page. Mirror it there, its server answering the path, with the document-render test run for both renderers.
- **A short page under `scroll document` shows the canvas, not the app's background, below its content** (2026-10-31, the Pokédex agent runs): with `<html data-scrolldocument>`, `#exact-root` is as tall as its content, so a root `view background-color=…` stops at the content's end and the canvas (white, or dark) shows below; `min-height="100%"` resolves against an auto height. The agent tried `100%`, then `100vh`, and searched the docs for a page background. CSS carries `body`'s background to the canvas (CSS Backgrounds 3 §2.11.2); the app's first root is `body`'s analogue, so both renderers and the runtime could carry its background to `<html>` while the page is the document's scroller.
- **A `table` takes no `border-collapse`** (2026-10-31, the Pokédex agent runs): `border-spacing` is a row since (one length; HTML's 2px unsaid; `border-spacing=0` with cells' `border-bottom` rules a table flush), but `border-collapse: collapse`, which merges neighbouring cells' borders by CSS 2.1 §17.6.2's conflict rules, is not: the web would take it as written, and the kernel's table grid would need the merge.
- **`host/web-js/sheet-leave.test.mjs` fails to compile since 05c258c192** (2026-10-31): both sources put `navigationPresentation` on a column with no `navigationKey`, which `lower-route-place` now refuses. Make each a route of a navigation root, or decide that the runtime's sheet-leave path need not serve a routeless column and test it through a route.
- **The render host does not write `decoding="async"` on an image** (2026-10-31), which the JS build writes on every `img` (LLP 1145 D3); its pages adopt, decoding on the page's thread.
- **The fragment after LLP 1153 D5: five edges the last review left** (2026-10-30, from the delta review of ca3c18eecf). (1) The JS target's settlement (`host/web-js/router.js`) counts held.js's `Counting`, which keeps an ordinary asynchronous ticket after its resource replaced or forgot it (rt.js changes `r.ticket`; held.js drops the count only when the request finishes), so a navigation to settled content can wait on an obsolete request where the runner removes forgotten tickets from `pending`: count only current, owned tickets, or retire a count when the replacing commit lands. (2) A new navigation does not end a native hold: Linux (`host/linux/src/presenter.rs`) arms the new activation without clearing `fragment_anchor`, and `hold_fragment()` checks only the person-scroll count and the node; iOS (`Fragment.swift`) can add a second `ElementScrolls` request without dropping the first, and replacing `fragmentHeld` loses the first request's name: retire the previous hold when navigation supersedes it, a navigation that asks no fragment scroll included. (3) The deferred traversal restoration in `host/web/fragment.js` (two frames after Back, around Chrome's own scroll) checks only `personScrolls`: neither `cancel()` nor a later `arm()` invalidates it and it does not check the current history entry, so an app's `scrollIntoView` before it runs is overwritten; bind it to a cancellable activation and history entry. (4) Activation numbers restart with each `RouterContext`, but Linux's reload path and Apple's presenter resets keep `fragment_seen`/`fragmentSeen`, so after a runner replacement activations below the old watermark are not armed and run without the person-scroll check: reset the watermark with the runner, or put a runner generation in the activation's identity. (5) The JS target's `scrollBy` calls `cancel()` with its default `pending = true`, marking an armed fragment superseded, where Linux clears only the existing hold and the runner cancels a pending activation only for `scrollIntoView`: `cancel(false)` for the held-only meaning, with a pending-data test.
- **Tables after LLP 1153 D7: five edges the last review left** (2026-10-30). (1) `relative()` in kernel/src/layout/table.rs drops percentage and percentage-bearing `calc()` offsets and always prefers `left`, while the engine resolves them and prefers `right` in right-to-left: `tr left="10%"` moves the row without its cells, and under RTL `left=10 right=20` moves the row left and its cells right; propagate the engine's resolved displacement with CSS 2.2 §9.4.3's precedence. (2) Every authored `display` on a table gets one anonymous table, `flex` included, where the browser makes `thead` and `tbody` separate flex items (CSS Flexbox §4, blockification before anonymous table generation); repair by the actual formatting context. (3) An existing anonymous table keeps a stale `direction` when only the table's direction changes (kernel/src/layout/table.rs, the reuse branch neither updates the node nor marks the table stale). (4) A caption's percentage `margin-bottom` resolves against the grid area inside the padding, not the table's width, as the three other sides now do (kernel/src/style/table.rs). (5) An empty `table display="block"` gets an anonymous grid with 2 px top and bottom padding, 4 px of height, where CSS 2.2 §17.2.1 generates one only for a misparented table child. (6) `make_table` (kernel/src/layout/table.rs, the branch with no engine node yet, and the `new_leaf` failure after it) removes `formed`, rewrites `placed` and returns without putting the table back in `stale`, leaving an anonymous grid already in `before.anon` allocated and forgotten; reinsert `formed`, keep or remove that node, and mark the table stale again. Each from the two delta reviews of 93bf5b8775; none reaches an ordinary table.
- **A fragment's scroll is driven on the JS target, by the router sweep's fragment case, and on Linux by its presenter's tests, only** (2026-10-30, the LLP 1153 D5 lane). The wasm web host (`fragment.js` from glue.js's `scrollToFragment`) and Apple's `Fragment.swift` were compiled, not driven: `bun scripts/smoke.mjs web` runs the sweep's case on the wasm host (not run here: the pinned nightly's `libLLVM.dylib` was missing), and on Apple open `/item/7#moves` and read the scroller. Two gaps remain on Apple: no host holds the target while pictures load there (the web and Linux re-align it until they land), and the Mac moves VoiceOver to the target but its key-view loop has no starting point to move. On the web, while the document still loads, Chrome holds its own fragment's element in view after Back or Forward, over the place `fragment.js` puts back.
- **Native table accessibility stops at roles** (2026-10-30, LLP 1153 D7). The Apple, Linux and Windows hosts expose a table, its rows and its cells by role (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/TableAccessibility.swift`, `host/linux/src/presenter/access.rs` `structure_role`, `host/windows/src/accessibility.rs`), not where each cell stands: AppKit's `AXTable` answers its rows, columns and header elements, UIKit's `.dataTable` container `UIAccessibilityContainerDataTable` (each cell's row and column ranges), AccessKit a row's and a cell's indices and spans, and Android's reader `CollectionInfo` and `CollectionItemInfo`, which `Access.kt` does not set for the new roles (it draws them, and a description list's, as groups). The kernel's placement holds every cell's row, column and spans (`kernel/src/layout/table.rs`): expose it on `Kernel` and answer each platform's table protocol from it, before the Mac's table becomes an `NSTableView` (LLP 1115.002).
- **LLP 1154 steps 2 and 3: Snapback publishes `snapback4-exact`, then exact2 deletes `snapback4/`** (2026-10-31). Step 1 is on main: the native slot's admitted and reserved storage, a package's prebuilt library fetched by hash and embedded on Apple and Linux, its web assets, and `packages/fixtures/vault` proving M5 on the Linux host, the web, macOS and the iOS simulator. What remains is Snapback's (§4: publish the package with D2's `exact.json`, dynamic libraries for Harmony's five targets, the module's call over the C ABI, `init --exact`, the guide) and then exact2's step 3 in one change (§3 Deletes; Harmony depends on the package; M1 to M6). Nothing in exact2 changes until Snapback publishes.

- **A fragment's scroll is driven on the JS target, and on Linux by its presenter's tests, only** (2026-10-30, the LLP 1153 D5 lane). The wasm web host (`fragment.js` from glue.js's `scrollToFragment`) and Apple's `Fragment.swift` were compiled, not driven: open `/item/7#moves` on each and read the scroller. Two gaps remain on Apple: no host holds the target while pictures load there (the web and Linux re-align it until they land), and the Mac moves VoiceOver to the target but its key-view loop has no starting point to move.
- **Reduced motion on the web** (2026-10-31, found with LLP 1083.000 D4's exit ghosts). An app follows Reduce Motion by computing its motion from `exactViewport().prefersReducedMotion` (LLP 1061 D5, `docs/recipes/motion.md`), and the web host updates that fact from `prefers-reduced-motion`. Two parts remain on the web. (1) Every motion row an app may gate on it should take a computed value on the JS target, as on Apple and the wasm web host. `-exact-exit-animation` does not: "a dynamic `exit_animation` (Animations) is not one declaration; not in the JS target" (`host/web-js/src/style.rs` `style_row`), so a web exit animation is one literal, the same with or without Reduce Motion. A computed `animation`, `transition` and `-exact-layout-transition` already build (`host/web-js/conformance/rowsmore.contract`; `host/web-js/src/rows.rs`, GitHub #809). Presence reads `--exact-exit-animation` from the element's inline style when its region removes it (`host/web-js/rt.js`), so writing it as `-exact-layout-transition` is written, from the values its expression can take (`style.rs` `layout_transitions`), would serve; compiling conditions on `prefersReducedMotion` to `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` rules instead would need a non-inherited custom property and presence reading the computed value. Check the other motion rows for the same refusal. (2) The motion the web host starts itself should follow Reduce Motion as the platform's does, as a shared-element flight lands at once on Apple (LLP 1013 D8). Some of it already follows `prefers-reduced-motion`: a view transition (`host/web-js/shared.js`), a switch and a modal's presentation (`host/web/neutral.css`), and a reorder group (`host/web/group-glue.js`'s `reduced`). Check the rest, such as navigation's pushes and pops, sheets, popovers, a reorder's glide and smooth scrolls.

  origin/main.
- **A box over a navigation root sits under the iOS status bar** (2026-10-30, bake-off r34 gradebook-ios). With a native stack the session's viewport is the whole view and `env()` is 0 (`ExactViewIOS.swift` `fit`, `wantsWholeView`), so a child of the navigation root that is no route, an overlay at `position="absolute" inset=0`, is laid out from the screen's top: its first row is under the status bar, where iOS gives it no touches, and the author has no inset to pad. On the web, the layout oracle, the same box starts below the browser's chrome. The compiler now refuses the case the bake-off hit (`navigationPresentation` off a route); lay such a box out in the safe area, or give it `env()`'s insets, for the rest.
- **Contract cannot label a day a date input picked** (2026-10-30, bake-off r34 gradebook-ios and tasks). `input type="date"` answers `2026-10-16`, `formatDate` and `formatDay` take epoch milliseconds, and nothing converts one to the other, so a due date showed as written; the dates recipe now routes it through the data module (`Date.UTC`). A day key accepted by `formatDate` (runner, web JS target and the Lean semantics alike) would make that unnecessary.
- **Caltrain, Expose and Typetour swap screens by state** (2026-10-30, `lower-screen-by-state` now walks routes and pages named by one state). `apps/caltrain` chooses its stations picker and deck fixture with `screen`, and `apps/expose` and `apps/typetour` their lock, home, settings and messages screens, so each build warns and on the web none of those screens has a URL. Make them routes (`contract vocab screen`), or delete a screen nobody drives.
- **`serve`'s rendering and `render.mjs --serve`, three gaps** (2026-10-30, LLP 1153 D3's delta review). (1) `sitePages` (`host/web/serve.mjs`) renders from the generation `servedFile` picked, but between a swap's two renames the picked generation is in neither `dist` nor `dist.gen/<id>`, so it answers the shell, and an exception during a swap (the renderer's construction, a file read) is a 500 instead of a retry: wait as `generationFile` does, bounded, and retry. (2) `render.mjs --serve` builds `renderer(dist)` before any request, so a release site with only client and build routes (no `.gen/server.js`) fails at start instead of serving the shell and its pages: make the renderer lazy. (3) `render.mjs --serve` resolves a build route's directory URL (`/about/`) through `matchPattern`, which refuses a trailing slash, and renders `notfound` where `serve` serves `about/index.html`: share `serve`'s file resolution. LLP 1153.001 D1 makes the two one server, which closes (2) and (3) by construction. (4) `foreignBeside` runs once before a `--out` build; a sibling (`<out>.previous`, a non-build entry of `<out>.gen`) made during the build is still deleted by `replaceBuild` or reaped: check again just before each delete and leave the tree in place when it fails.
- **Two edges of `var(--name)` (LLP 1153 D6)** (2026-10-30). The loader writes a `var()` naming a token as the name before lowering (`contract/lower/src/var_tokens.rs`), so (1) a row it then refuses quotes the name, not what the author wrote: `color="var(--space.row)"` is refused as `` `color="space.row"`: `space.row` is a length token ``; and (2) a component argument is written only when it is one `var()` (it may be text), so `Card(edge="1px solid var(--line)")`, `line` declared, reaches its row unwritten and is refused there (`lower-css-shorthand`: `` `var(--line)` is not an admitted `border` width, style or color ``). Keep each literal's spelling beside the rewrite for the refusal's words, and write an argument where its prop reaches a style row (`params::reached` walks that path).
- **`serve` answers a page's `head status=404` with 200** (2026-10-30, LLP 1153 D8's `site` pattern, `/planet/99`). LLP 1048.000 D11 makes a settled page's head status (404, 410, 503) its HTTP status, as `host/render`'s `Rendered::status` does; Bun's renderer answers 404 only for the `notfound` route (`host/web-js/render.mjs` `finish`), its server bundle's `__render` does not return `Head.headStatus`, and a browser's navigation gets its head, and so its 200, before the render settles (`pageServer`). Return the head's status from `__render`, use it in `finish`, and hold a navigation's head until the status is known for a route whose page can declare one.
- **Glyph atlas upload nits** (2026-10-31, LLP 1149.001 M10, cut 7; Claude findings 3 and 7). Split a shelf-wrap dirty rectangle if measured worthwhile: the current union copies about 16.9 KiB per fling copy for a few KiB of masks. Packing dirty rows then copying them into staging costs two copies; Claude cites 29.8 μs for cold staging allocation/copy and a few μs per fling batch. A `(shadow, stride, rect)` device input could remove the intermediate copy. Neither optimization is needed for parity; measure before widening the device seam.

Cut 7's atlas batch lowers main's carrier from 253.124 to 215.080 ms (qualified n=10/9; nine matched pairs save 32.862 ms median), without the other startup cuts. Session 1 after is Ink 200.874, GPUI 209.308 and egui 222.489 ms; session 2 is 201.766, 197.920 and 203.609 ms (n=10/10/7). All three margin predictions miss. - **The Rust render host keeps no answers across renders** (2026-10-30, LLP 1153.001 D7). Bun's renderer (`host/web-js/render.mjs`) puts every render's fetches through one RFC 9111 shared cache (`host/web-js/http-cache.mjs`); `host/render` starts each render with no answer in hand, so an API-backed page waits on the API every time (Pokédex home 190 to 550 ms a render there, against 5 to 12 ms warm under Bun). Give its fetch executor the same cache, under the same rules, before delivery serves such pages from it.
- **`check` on the Windows host** (2026-10-31, bake-off Windows lane). The painted presenter answers no `check` observation (`scripts/agent-check.mjs` takes web and iOS), so `verify windows` captures each screen unjudged: no contrast, target-size, overflow or largest-text findings. Answer it from the presenter as the Apple agent's `check` does, then let `verify windows` judge light and dark as iOS's does.
- **A Windows build's receipt names no app source** (2026-10-31). `windows-<target>.build.json`'s inputs hold the app's Cargo files but not app.contract, app.ts or the crate, so `agent.mjs windows` does not refuse a stale build; `agent-build.mjs` `windowsBuild` compares the app's files with the executable's time instead. Record the bake's inputs in the receipt and drop that comparison.
- **M2 rest trace on the caret workload** (LLP 1149.001 M2b). The G5m caret now presents twice per second but still uses 27.148 process / 24.661 main-thread ms/s. Its `every(250, tick)` and `every(1000, second)` fire five times per second, and the display link starts and stops twice. Attribute the service passes, link start/stop and probe on the retained workload before predicting another CPU saving; this is not M2's timer-free rest case. See `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, “G5m, after M2b”.

- **The SVG expansion-limit test exceeds its five-second deadline after a passing full run** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149.001 Stage 0 review fixes). `exact-svg-file::tests::a_file_that_expands_past_a_limit_draws_nothing_and_names_it` fails at 8.218781 s in the root suite and 8.294572 s in isolation, both with `RUST_TEST_THREADS=2`. - **The field adapter is what unblocks Excord's tests on the harness** (2026-10-30, LLP 1151 lane F). `clock until` is delivered (one `land`, then the `expect state` matcher), and every one of Excord's 93 blocks that reach it waits on `gateway.state`, a field path the matcher cannot read: the observation prints a record positionally, with no field names (D1's reason for leaving fields out of stage 1). Resolving fields and indices on both observations through the plan's shapes, the adapter D1 names for stage 2, is therefore the next delivery step worth its cost: it turns 93 partial blocks into candidates for whole, and it is the same adapter `expect state a.b` and a property over a field need. `semantics/difftest/src/settle.rs` stops the block at `clock until (field path)` today and the receipt counts them.
- **A Mac atom host counts hidden siblings** (2026-10-31, #1180's review). `NodeView.atomHost` (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/FieldAtoms.swift`, `holdsMoreThanOneNode`) counts every child node, `display: none` and `aria-hidden` ones included, which VoiceOver does not see. A scrolling wrapper holding the field and one hidden sibling becomes the host, so the atoms go into a scroll area of several items that VoiceOver enters only on interaction (LLP 1045.000 §5.2). The T3 composer's placeholder is there only while the text is empty, so it does not meet this. Count only nodes VoiceOver lists.
- **Metal archive identity across reboots** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149.001 Stage 0b, Claude review 11). Check that the same GPU’s `registryID` and archive path survive a reboot, then require `MTLPipelineOptionFailOnBinaryArchiveMiss` to load that retained archive on the same OS and device. If the ID changes, choose a stable device key before claiming retained-cache startup across reboots.

- **The G3c aggregation no longer accepts its retained driver records** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149.001 Stage 0 landing). `bun bench/heavy-list/gpui/aggregate.mjs target/g3c --verify` fails because G3c predates the driver's `emptyCallbacks` field: summing that absent field produces a nonfinite metric. The same command fails with origin/main `fc321f962e`'s unchanged reducer. Keep the absent measurement distinct from zero when accepting the older series. Diagnostics: `target/s0/landing/aggregate-{diagnostic,origin-main}.log`.
- **The Apple upload-stream burst test flakes in complete suites** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149.001 Stage 0). `a_burst_of_photo_uploads_goes_three_at_a_time_and_leaves_other_streams_their_places` timed out waiting five seconds for an outcome in a full run and in the asynchronous-core sub-suite of a serial root run; it passed in isolation. After rebasing onto `fc321f962e`, the complete root check passes with `RUST_TEST_THREADS=2`, including the asynchronous-core sub-suite. No assertion, timeout or executor policy changed. It recurs in the review-fix continuation's third full root run after two passing complete runs: `target/s0/review-fixes/root-final-round3/test.log` records the asynchronous-core sub-suite's five-second timeout. Retries stop at the lane's three-round limit. Investigate delivery and the fixture's outcome handling. Failures: `target/s0/checks-attempt/{test,test-serial}.log`; passing full rerun: `target/s0/landing/checks/test.log`.
- **Windows device-loss recovery does not reliably restore Mica within three presents** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149.001 Stage 0). The native fixture passed an isolated retry and the next complete suite, then failed with desktop composition disabled through all three retries; an earlier failure restored Mica on retry two where the fixture requires retry one. Separate the host's recovery obligation from compositor availability before changing that assertion. The Stage 0 review-fix continuation passes forced device loss in the NucBox's interactive desktop: the recovered/fresh Mica band has mean 0 and no pixels over 32. That machine needs no GO file. G6d's retry code was already in the earlier failing tip; no recovery policy changed, and the earlier intermittency remains unexplained. Failures: `target/s0/windows/native-attempt/d3d11-device_loss.log` and `target/s0/windows/native-period/d3d11-device_loss.log`; passing recheck: `target/s0/review-fixes/windows/native/d3d11-device_loss.log`. See the Stage 0 section of `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`.
- **A seeded Apple target's registry crates keep the seeding target's dep-info paths** (2026-10-30, the textarea-resize lane). A new app's first `--ios --no-install` and `--tvos --no-install` builds (`host/apple/build.mjs`, `EXACT_APP_DIR` set) failed with `no matching rustc unit dep-info for zeroize` (then `typenum`): the units `host/apple/crates.mjs` copied into `target/aarch64-apple-ios-sim/host-dev/deps` keep `.d` files whose paths name the target that compiled them (`~/.cache/exact-lanes/t3-lane-a/…`), which `scripts/cargo-dep-info.mjs` `unitDepInfo` does not accept. Rewriting those paths to the new target let both builds finish. Rewrite them when the crates are copied in, or match the unit by its file name alone.
- **A focused Mac textarea's focus ring shows a 1 px line at its right and bottom edges** (2026-10-30, the textarea-resize lane; not caused by `resize`). Seen in a key window after a drag made the agent's window key: its pixels show the ring coming through the box's fractional last pixel. The ring's mask is `TextAreaScroll.drawFocusRingMask` (`Mac/NativeFieldsMac.swift`). A mask on the pixel grid was tried and not verified by running; look at it in a key window with a fractional-height textarea.
- **An Apple Markdown reader without atoms reads its source to assistive technology** (2026-10-30, fw-atom-name). A `text markup="markdown"` with no `atoms` is one paragraph element whose label and value are `props["text"]`, the Markdown source with its syntax (`NodeView.visibleParagraphText`, `TextInteraction.swift`), where the web's reader is its rendered words. With atoms it reads the plain text, each atom by its accessible name (LLP 1045.000 D8); the same plain text (`exact_markup_plain`) would serve every Markdown reader.
- **MIDIMacTests.testAFollowedClockIsBatchedAndStartGoesAtOnce failed once under load, not reproduced** (2026-10-30, iosfix2). One full parallel Mac run at load 49 (2026-10-29 19:28Z) failed `ticks alone no more often than 50 ms` (0.028 s, not above 0.04). Not reproduced since: 5 runs of the class alone beside a cargo build (load 14-18), 5 full parallel Mac suites (load 6-22), and 3 alone, all 0 failures. If it recurs, the interval it measures is wall time between delivered ticks, which a stalled process compresses: measure it on the deliveries' own timestamps.
- **IframeWindowsTests' download case, a red in rt1's 10-29 baseline, not reproduced** (2026-10-30, iosfix2). 3 runs of the class alone at load 12 and 8 full parallel iOS runs (2026-10-29/30, load 15-235) were all clean. It has flaked under load before; if it recurs, keep its log.
- **The native executor's `Proxy` wrapper reads an explicit new target's `prototype`** (2026-10-30, rt1's crossing skip; theme2's 06:26 ruling). js/src/binary.js puts the global `Proxy` and `Proxy.revocable` behind Proxies of the engine's own whose traps record every proxy made, so `keptReply` never keeps a value holding one. Hermes, constructing a callable proxy, runs `ordinaryCreateFromConstructor` on the new target before the `construct` trap, so `Reflect.construct(Proxy, args, C)` (or a bound `Proxy`) reads `C.prototype`, which the engine's `Proxy` doesn't (a getter there runs; js/tests/it/kept_json.rs pins today's one read). Fix in the engine: skip that creation when a `construct` trap is present (the spec's order), or give the executor a native Proxy predicate so the wrapper can go.
- **On the Mac a surface inside a card is darker than the card in dark mode** (2026-10-30, the Mac surface roles; theme2's 04:06 ruling). The Mac's card roles (secondary-background, secondary-grouped-background) are AppKit's underPageBackgroundColor (#f6f6f6 / #282828); tertiary-background and tertiary-grouped-background, a surface inside a card, stay controlBackgroundColor (#ffffff / #1e1e1e), white inside the grey card in light but #1e1e1e inside #282828 in dark, where iOS goes lighter (#2c2c2e inside #1c1c1e). AppKit has no solid between them (unemphasizedSelectedContentBackgroundColor #dcdcdc / #464646 is too strong in light). Revisit if AppKit gains one, or name a dark-only literal pair.
- **Windows: G6e restores cadence; its CPU gate remains open** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149 G6e). The G6d cadence regression is done: restoring the full-period wait and removing the clean-frame reservation skip gives five quiet flings with zero late intervals, p99 16.85 [16.77–16.93] ms and 58.15 [58.07–58.53] displays per gesture second. The full gate is not done: process CPU is 84.08 [74.70–93.39] ms/s against G6c’s 73.11–84.05 range. All 1,247 snapshots match successful frames. No third pacing policy was tried. The PresentMon trace qualifies cadence but has no CPU stacks to attribute the remaining range miss. Evidence: `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, “G6e”; `target/g6e/final/` and `skip-trial/`.
- **Windows: resolve G6c’s remaining startup shift** (2026-10-30, LLP 1149 G6c). The waitable reservation fixes the D3D11 fling, but startup neutrality is not established: ten quiet final launches give DWM 182.25 [171.22–211.84] ms against G6’s 175.98 [169.40–190.49]. A quiet alternating ten-pair control gives before/after medians 184.29/188.43 ms and paired increase +3.28 [−10.72–37.23] ms. Compare the startup phase timestamps and waitable admission before deciding whether this is launch variation or remaining scheduling cost. G6c stopped after three performance revisions. Evidence: `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, “G6c”; `target/g6c/final/warm-startup/` and `target/g6c/paired-waitable/`.
- **Egui heavy-list intermittently fails readiness** (2026-10-29, LLP 1149 §4.1 G4; owed to `bench/heavy-list/egui/`, not Exact). One of thirteen timed launches, `target/g4b/series/cold/egui-8.log`, timed out at 30 s with its window frontmost and key. G4a's first functional launch took 14.7 s; four G4b readiness probes did not reproduce the delay. The scoped system logs name no cause. Capture the frame, image-worker and drawable timeline on a failing launch before choosing a fix; the twelve successful launches do not close this. See `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, “G4”.
- **The s15b2 gate's web-wasm image-raster rule covers whatever is drawn over a full-bleed image** (2026-10-30, theme2's s15b2 sign-off, a follow-up). video-devi's Image box is the whole screen (a full-bleed poster), so the 4-level noise rule (scripts/stage12-named.mjs `WASM_IMAGE_NOISE`, inside Image boxes laid out alike on both sides) also covers the overlays drawn above it. Before the next gate leans on it, count a pixel as image noise only where the Image is the topmost node there (from the whole layout's order), so an overlay's change still differs.
- **The wasm web host's scaled images vary by up to 4 levels from run to run** (2026-10-29, the s15b2 gate; theme2's 22:01 ruling). On web-wasm the package Avatar's images (42 and 52 pt, scaled) draw a few hundred pixels 1 to 4 levels apart between two drives of one build: the Bluesky clone's light chat 286 px at most 2 levels between two drives of the same side, its dark chat 288 px at most 4 against the gate's run, new-chat 243 px at most 4; another pair of drives was identical. web-js, iOS, the Mac and Linux never differ there. The s15b2 gate counts it as image-raster noise (scripts/stage12-named.mjs `WASM_IMAGE_NOISE`, inside Image boxes laid out alike on both sides). Find why the wasm host's image draw is not deterministic (its scaling path, a decode or a GPU raster), then drop the rule.
- **A game's logic edit still takes 11-12 s in the dev loop after its app wasm stopped rebuilding** (2026-10-29, rt1, measured on Beacons under `game/dev.mjs`, `rust: rebuilt in` 11.1-11.4 s at load 5-6; the app wasm's 34-58 s `web`-profile rebuild was the table asset's identical rewrite moving `assets/`'s mtime, fixed in `game/app/tables.mjs`). What is left, sampled from the build's processes at load 5 (12.4 s in all): `game/app/shells.mjs --check` with its clippy and check of the logic crate (~1.3 s), the GPU module's `gpu-dev` build (~1.4 s), the app wasm's cargo build (fresh, ~0.5 s), `wasm-opt -Oz` over that unchanged app wasm (~3.8 s, `host/web/build.mjs`), then the instantiate and plan extract, the GPU module's `wasm-bindgen` (its own wasm, which a logic edit can change) and the build's tail (~4 s together), and the restart's `contract sources` and `-dev` bin (~2 s). Cuts to measure: skip `wasm-opt` when the app wasm's bytes are the last build's, and find which of the ~4 s after it is the instantiate, the bindgen or the tail.
- **A module's availability source could answer from the app's grant** (2026-10-29, rt1; theme2's 21:47 ruling). Without `device.calls` the runner still substitutes `@exact/calls`' sends and sources: `startCall` lists an in-app call and `callsStatus()` answers `available: true` (probe beside `contract/cli/tests/it/voip_calls.rs`), so an app can't hide a feature from the module's own answer. Harmony doesn't need it: its test call's grants are a development build's only (LLP 1069.008, as built 2026-10-29) and the row reads Harmony's own `grants` (`testCallBuild`). For the next app that does: a module's availability source answers its unavailable form, and its grant-gated sends refuse, when the app lacks the module's grant (runner/src/runner/modules/admit.rs), calls as the first example.
- **A wasm worker realm still encodes an answer for a module with no overlay** (2026-10-29, the overlay cut's round 3, theme2's 15:05 ruling). The page realm and the native and JS executors decide `exact.overlay` once at load (LLP 1054) and skip the encode without one, but a worker realm cannot answer `has-overlay` synchronously, so `host/web/module-glue.js` answers it `true` and `js/web/src/lib.rs` encodes the answer and writes before the worker's `{tag:4, worker:true}`. Carry the load-time decision in the worker's init handshake (`module-worker.js`), cached on the realm, and drop LLP 1054's worker qualifier.
- **ResetGate's known losses** (2026-10-29, rt1; triaged with theme2, by probe). `ResetGate.knownLoss` (host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/ResetGate.swift) lists 16 held Runtime calls whose content a restart's reset would lose: `storeChanged`, an incoming `share`, `picked`, an incoming `navigate`/`hostGo`, a document's paths through `change`, `controlTextChanged`, `deliverySync` (re-asked only after `applyTogether`), `canvasImage`, `canvasDisplay`, `textReady`, `midi`, `activities`, `preferExtension`, `designRows`, `setModes`. Most come from the main queue, which the hold never drains: ResetHoldTests (iOS and Mac) shows nothing queued before a restart (main queue, common-modes timer and perform) runs inside it, with a sheet open or two windows restarted together. So none is reachable today; they stay listed for the scan. Reopen if ResetHoldTests fails (the teardown turning the run loop in a common mode, or draining the main queue): then keep such an arrival at the session while held and deliver it after `replayHeld`.
- **PARKED: an import's accent offered as the packages' brand fills** (2026-10-29, dtool3; theme2's 07:21 stop rule, after six review rounds). Design: an import that carries an accent (shadcn's primary, Astryx's accent) offers it as `actions.follow-fill` and `cards.empty-button`, each with its ink token set per scheme to white or black, whichever reaches 4.5:1 (the two ratios multiply to 21 on an opaque fill, so one always does), in a `review.brands` list of its own (a tamber/1.18 line), off until ticked, left out of a bare accept below 4.5:1, on an import's first proposal only. Built and tested on branch `dt3/import-brand-parked` (8d20b67c53, not merged). The rounds found, in turn: inks gone stale through every re-hold (partial accept, edit-held, recompute: aliases, `when` lines, design-held values), designs that check only with their packages (a line-stripping measure hid accent branches), dial orphans across role and brand lines, older tools taking brand items on recomputed proposals, and, last, an offer that invalidates its own measurement (the accent's `when` branch reading the very ink token the offer rewrites). Revisit when the importer can fold the FINAL design (the import and its offers' own operations applied, with the app's packages joined) and measure every tuple there; until then an app sets the brand tokens by full name, which loses nothing but the convenience.
- **LLP 1149 S1, what G3c leaves open** (2026-10-29; `bench/heavy-list/gpui/RESULTS.md`, G3c). (1) The carrier's fling peak footprint, 592 MiB against GPUI's 409 and the native host's 229: about 340 MiB of owned-unmapped graphics memory during the fling that `vmmap` and `malloc_history` cannot attribute (S1.4). First drop decoded CPU pixels after their GPU upload and re-decode when the texture is evicted; then attribute what remains with Metal allocation profiling at launch. (2) Cold start to the first complete frame is 344 ms for ink, 312 for GPUI and 370 for the native host, all over D15's 100 ms budget; the 100 ms budget phase by phase, as S2 owes on Windows. (3) No content-cadence instrument exists for GPUI, which re-presents unchanged scenes for a second after input: the presented-drawable cadence row compares content cadence (ink) with tick cadence (GPUI) outside a gesture. (4) G3c does not rerun D15's vello-with-rows or wgpu control arms on the Mac. (5) The drawable ring's wait duplicates the per-present completion wait: use one mechanism or let the worker pipeline frames. (6) A per-frame staging arena would remove the two-slot serialization and the double copy.
- **Two `-exact-press-*` paints duplicate the `:active` state part** (2026-10-28, LLP 1035.005.002 §3's `-exact-` inventory). `-exact-press-background-color` and `-exact-press-color` say what `:active`'s `background-color` and `color` rows say (LLP 1121 D8, `contract/lower/src/states.rs` `ACTIVE_ROWS`), and `lower-state-press-paint` already refuses writing both. `-exact-press-opacity` is not a duplicate: it is a share of the node's opacity where `:active`'s `opacity` lowers to the absolute `-exact-press-opacity-to`. `-exact-press-scale` is also an `:active` row, under its own name, so it stays. Under LLP 1081 D6 the part stays and the two paint names go to `renamed`; no app in the repo writes them today, so the `contract migrate` rule is for apps outside the repo.
- **An SVG id that n instances repeat costs n² to resolve** (2026-10-29). `Kernel::resolve_id` (`kernel/src/svg/refs.rs`) builds, for every reference, the ancestor chain of each live node the id names and each step's place among its siblings, so a component's gradient or clip id used n times costs n² chains. In a `sample` profile of the T3 Code clone's JS build (261,364 nodes) it is 8 of 41 s, from `exact_web::host::element::css_style_of` under the JS target's projection (`style::project`). Each candidate's place in tree order, found once per id, would answer a reference in time proportional to its depth.
- **A bound length row's token map is named without its inheritance** (2026-10-29, `measures::map_name`). `width` and `letter-spacing` both take `$tl`, but `measures::map` leaves a varying token out of an inherited row's map, so the row the JS target writes first decides both: with `state w = "space.row"` (a token that varies by an app axis), `width=w` before `letter-spacing=w` maps the token to its property on both, and the other order on neither, so `width` writes the token's name. Name the map by what `map` reads, `id.inherited()` included.
- **The stub engine's `exact-js` tests do not compile** (2026-10-28). `EXACT_JS_ENGINE=stub cargo check -p exact-js --tests` fails: under the stub, `js/build.rs` leaves `exact_js_engine` unset, so `js/tests/it/storage.rs` compiles out, and `js/tests/it/background.rs`, which imports `super::storage`, lacks the `#![cfg(exact_js_engine)]` its neighbours carry.
- **`host/web-js/gpu-cache.test.mjs`'s lock test asserts an exclusion `withLock` does not promise** (2026-10-29). "waiters take the lock one at a time, wait for a live holder of either kind, and take it from one killed" failed in 4 of 10 runs of the file by itself on `6da2c9ef9b`, with an iOS test suite running on the same Mac. When the flock holder is killed, the old checkout's file lock (the test's `oldLock`) can remove the dead holder's file just as the flock waiter takes it; both then hold the lock, and the second's `inside` mark (`:176`) fails with `EEXIST`, reported at `:213`. `host/web/build-lock.mjs`'s header lists this race as left open between the two kinds of lock, so the decision is to narrow the test's step or close the race.
- **`host/web-js/shared-reorder.test.mjs`'s scaffold serves a file from a path that does not exist** (2026-10-29, `bun test --isolate host/web-js` on `6da2c9ef9b`, and the file alone, in three runs of three). "a deferred drop keeps its own result through an unrelated refusal" fails grouped and not: its page server maps a path under `/web/` to `../web` plus the whole path, so it serves `/web/timer-observations.js` from `host/web/web/`.
- **An app's abort listener can keep a web stream open** (2026-10-30, the web JS target's fetch deadlines). `host/web-js/ts-stream.js` `open` ends a stream when its caller's signal aborts through a listener on that signal, so an app listener added first that calls `stopImmediatePropagation()` keeps the stream open; a browser's fetch, and the file work in `ts-fetch.js` and `ts-body-from.js`, hear the abort through a signal of their own (`ownSignal`), which no app listener reaches. Hear it there too.
- **The painted presenter does not pad a popover of the author's content** (2026-10-29). The web's neutral look pads one by the metric table's `popover-padding` (1rem) where its author wrote no padding; the Linux presenter (`host/linux/src/presenter/`, which paints Linux, Windows and Android's reader) paints the kernel's layout, whose padding there is 0, so by its code it draws that content flush against the popover's edges (not seen running). Its look reads neutral-column lengths through `host/linux/src/paint/look.rs` (`metrics::length_in`).
- **Safari leaves its top-edge blur over the page after a resume, on the iPhone Duo's cover display** (2026-10-30, iOS 27.2, Safari, the Duo closed; seen on Unsaid's first screen). Brought back from the background, Safari blurs the band where a phone's status bar would be, over an unscrolled page's title, though on the cover display its status items sit in its side column; switching tabs clears it. Exact's page draws no blur there and changes nothing across the resume, and the iOS 27.1 Duo simulator does not show it, so it reads as Safari's: report it to Apple (Feedback Assistant) with a photo, and do not pad every page's top to dodge it.
- **The web's touch press under a person's scroll, not yet seen in Firefox for Android** (2026-10-29). `input-glue.js` counts a scroll as a person's when it follows a touch or pen `pointercancel` or a wheel, moves a press under any other scroll, and swallows the click of a touch lifted outside the target it began on. iOS Safari 27.2 on an iPhone passed a pan, a caught glide (Safari keeps that touch: no pointer or touch events reach the page) and a chat that keeps itself at the bottom, by a diagnostics page over the bundled glue. Firefox for Android has run it with synthetic events only.
- **LLP 1071.001 part B: the feed's rows built cheaper** (2026-10-30, re-measured by x2-perf4; the stage 1 line it replaces is done). Part A landed in 1c7b918556 (A1-A3, A5-A8). A4, snapshots compared by revision, is CLOSED: on main 1b2e291a69 (`bun bench/web-shootout/scrollprof.mjs bench/dioxus/web/dist/bounded <out> {6000,24000} 3`, load 16-19, two runs each) `JSON.stringify` has no sampled self time and `snapshot()` is 0.5-2.3 ms inclusive per 3 s, at most 1% of a 255-388 ms task. The scroll's time is now the browser's style, layout and paint of the rows just built, 60-75% of the task: collection-glue's clamped offset read carries 46-94 ms of the forced pass over the new rows. Only part B moves that: B1 (a row is one template clone) and B5 (enumerated style bindings as classes; the feed makes 5,629 `setProperty` calls per 3 s). It waits on Charlie's LLP 1071.001 §8 answers for part B (D5, its measure: a Krausest entry, which needs LLP 1152 D3, or Conduit's feed page), then a measured plan (~/x2theme/x2p4-webfeed-measure.md).
- **`bun exact.mjs verify`'s verdict is no longer its last line** (2026-10-28, LLP 1151 D8 as `b4055482d9` built it). `contract verify`'s whole report (its PART/NOTE lines, a page of suggested properties, the receipt) follows `verify: clean in …` on stdout, so an agent reading the tail sees the receipt and not the verdict, and the new-app report's nine-line budget no longer holds. Fold a summary of it into verify's report ahead of the verdict, or end on a combined verdict.
- **An author's bar beside a whole-view tab container is not its routes' bottom inset** (2026-10-28, bake-off r33's photos). The select bar, an in-flow row that replaces the hidden tablist, is laid out below the panels, but the `UITabBarController` and its routes take the whole view, so the grid's bottom inset falls from the tab bar's 83 to the home indicator's 34 and its last row ends 12 pt under the bar. Entering select mode then moves the grid down 44 pt: kept at its end as the inset shrinks, which UIKit does too (a table at its end whose toolbar hides moves down by the difference). The rest is the app's: its bar is 46 pt where Photos' toolbar takes the tab bar's height. Count an in-flow sibling below the panels in the routes' safe area (`additionalSafeAreaInsets.bottom`) while the tab bar is hidden.
- **Speech, what is owed** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.012 §13's as-built notes). (1) On Android's reader an extension's `preferred` call lands on the main thread after the drive's next request has moved the clock, so an agent path that stamps what a drive prefers with `now()` sees it late: drive 6 of `packages/speech-recognition/test/drives.mjs android` reads 29 of 30 finals. The carrier needs a signal that the call has landed, as `polls` is for a jump's asks. (2) On the reader, `clock settle` cannot wait for an extension's answer, which only the main thread gives. (3) Synthesis has run on no Android app, and neither package's real Android engine has run; the Android 14 emulator's Google services would serve a fixture that binds them. (4) D12 item 6 and `microphone=true` on Apple wait on Charlie's decision (§13).
- **The web shootout, stage 0** (2026-10-28, LLP 1152 r2 §10). `bench/web-shootout/`: the streaming proxy that injects one probe into every arm and applies the leg's latency once, the local Conduit API server, the drives, `report.mjs`, the three baseline arms (static HTML, vanilla JS, Exact's render-served Conduit) on Mac Chrome, the pilot that fixes repetition counts and the tie rule, and the phone feasibility spike (a touch route that exists on a physical iPhone, the `PerformanceObserver` entry-type matrix per pinned engine). Stage 0 depends on none of §12's decisions; the field and the ladder wait on D1 to D8.
- **@exact/clipboard: what stage 1 still owes** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.006 §9 as built). (1) Item 5, `storage.fs.compressImage`'s amendments: `maxDimension` omitted with `type: "image/png"` keeps the size and `maxBytes` refuses (`unfit`) rather than shrinks; `maxPixels` refuses a source over it before decoding; SVG input, on Apple through the hosts' SVG rasterizer (`host/svg-file`) and on the web through an `Image` (which a worker realm has none of), sized as an `img` is; and frame 0 of an APNG, which ImageIO does not give (`packages/clipboard/apple/Clipboard.swift` `firstFrame` does it for the clipboard). Across `js/src/binary.js`, `data/host/src/native.rs`, `data/src/image.rs` and `image/apple.rs`, and `host/web/storage-image.js`. Then the fixture's JPEG, SVG with no size and 16,000 × 1,000 buttons. (2) `ClipboardItem.supports` answers from the declaration on every target, true where the module is absent (Android, Linux, Windows): the binding is one for all targets and data code has no synchronous way to ask whether a module is linked. (3) The Rust binding has no bytes form for a keyed shape's key. (4) A person's steps: `packages/clipboard/test/checklist.md` (Preview, Messages, Safari's and Firefox's activation, D3).
- **The local listener, what is owed** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.005 §10's as-built notes). The T3 Code clone's adoption drive is the module's acceptance (`desktop-activation.ts` on `exactListen`, its broker in TypeScript, `codex-setup-ops.ts` on `openAuthSession`, then `t3 app <dir>` from Terminal with D2's three routing differences and a real ChatGPT sign-in with a cancel and a port in use), on `daehyeon/t3-code-main`. `focus()` from Terminal against T3 Code's own activation (D10), and whether it brings the app in front of the browser after a loopback sign-in, need a person at a Mac.
- **Ink region copies** (LLP 1149 D2.5; C3f review Muse 7). Bound blend and backdrop destination copies to the affected region; they still copy the viewport. Overflow clips now flatten coverage without copying the destination.

- **Ink retained coverage on direct Metal** (LLP 1149 D9). Compare the `ink/rows/tests.rs` scene at origin (0, 0) as a kept row and a fresh walk: the cropped path coverage differs by up to 19/255 in a channel. Resolve the discrepancy before expanding the direct-device parity claim.

- **Purchases' owed pieces** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.015 §12's as-built notes). The `SKTestSession` conformance suite (stage 1) needs a development-signed hosted test (`packages/purchases/test/Weatherlight.storekit` is its configuration); the drives on the iOS simulator and the Android emulator; the merged-manifest check of D16 item 6; tier 2's Apple names, which wait for an implementation's own names (LLP 1133.001 D2); `storefront` on Apple (an alpha-3 to alpha-2 table); D21's Developer ID check; stage 3's seam tests. An app with TypeScript keeps every module source's answer under LLP 1027 D4, `unfinishedPurchases` included, which D4 says is not kept. Drive 13 waits for the accounts.
- **LLP 1133.007, what is still open** (2026-10-30, §10, §14, §17). Not built: the secret field (stage 2) and Windows (stage 3); Linux's `fork` path has not run on Linux ("Desktop processes, after stage 1" below). Every desktop app links the supervision (§14's declared difference): linking it by use needs a capability the TypeScript bake sets from the app's imports, which the Apple and Linux entries read as `host/apple/src/link.rs` reads the grammars. D13's headless origin does not exist (its declared difference), and a process request is held to its enqueuing commit's origin, not `data_origin()` (its known difference).
- **Natively, an answer's timer cannot cut its own fetch short** (2026-10-28; kept as a declared difference by Daehyeon Mun's ruling; LLP 1027.000 D1's amendment record, *Fetches*). An answer waits on one thing at a time, so on a native host `Promise.race([fetch, sleep])`, or a `setTimeout` that calls `abort()`, runs after the fetch lands; the web races them. `js/tests/it/timers.rs` `natively_a_timer_raced_against_its_own_fetch_waits_for_the_fetch` pins today's order and its journal line. The structural fix lets an answer resume while its own fetch is still in flight, so its timer runs and can abort the request: that relaxes the runner's one request per target (LLP 1016 D5) for an answer's continuation beside its own fetch. Until then, a hung fetch's deadline is `AbortSignal.timeout` or `exactTimeout`, kept by the host.
- **macOS: a `mousedown` `preventDefault` that keeps the focus where it is** (2026-10-30, named for a later ruling by theme2 when it approved the pointerdown order, LLP 1005's macOS pointer line). Chrome lets a `mousedown` handler's `preventDefault` stop a press from moving the focus. On the Mac, a press in a text area or an input now reports `pointerdown` before NSWindow focuses the editor (`Presenter.pressEditorFirst`), but nothing can stop the focus move: that needs a hook between the window monitor's early down and `NSWindow.sendEvent`, plus a ruling on which event carries it.
- **The hatch's undeclared-recognizer check reports a hatched frame's own web view** (2026-10-28, seen driving `native-fixture`'s `frame` hatch on macOS and iOS). `ElementHatches.call` snapshots only the node's own recognizers before the call, and `HatchRegions.undeclared` walks every subview after it, so a frame's `WKWebView` (and on iOS its scroll view's) recognizers are journaled as the hatch's at each `built` or `changed` that has the view: 14 lines on macOS, 30 on iOS, for a hatch that added nothing. Snapshot the subtree, or leave the platform object's own views out of the walk.
- **A missing-oracle divergence can print `<end>` on both sides** (2026-10-28, LLP 1148 A2): Harmony's second default walk and Shelf's swipe-delete test diverge at boot, but `verify::locate` drops the reason comments that `compare` used. Show the missing oracle answer and the runner's refusal reason when the first difference has no configuration line; the raw observations already retain both.

- **Default `contract verify` is slow on Design Tool** (2026-10-28, LLP 1148 A2): its runner half took 515 s for 142 search scripts, including 20 walks of 40 events and 71 stored replays. A sample shows timed-task commits rebuilding the document during clock advances. Measure the cost of the 60-second advances and the search policy before making this a routine author loop; retain the coverage and receipt denominators.


- **Shelf: module effects are outside the differential oracle** (2026-10-30, LLP 1151 lane G). `contract verify apps/shelf/app.contract --walks 20 --steps 40 --walk-seed 0xeed4df11b739e972 --confirm 2` still disagrees at `clock +1`. The runner consumes `setContentIndex` and `setAppShortcuts` as module effects (`runner/src/runner/modules/declared.rs:132`, `runner/src/runner/commit.rs:682,747`); Lean retains them as host commands (`semantics/Contract/Runtime.lean:824,877`). This is not repeated-command deduplication. The one-event case is kept in `semantics/pending/shelf-module-effects.contract`; reproduce with `cargo run -p contract-difftest -- corpus semantics/pending/shelf-module-effects.contract`. Modelling these effects needs their module declarations and observation boundary; do not remove commands by name just to make comparison pass.

- **Excord: login-item sends bypass the differential oracle** (2026-10-30, LLP 1151 lane G; pre-existing host-operation boundary in §5). The login switch commits on the runner; Lean refuses because its transcript has no `registerLoginItem` answer. The runner routes module sends internally (`runner/src/runner/modules.rs:355,368`; substitute: `runner/src/runner/modules/declared/desktop.rs:105,116`); Lean asks the data oracle (`semantics/Contract/Runtime.lean:851,206`). The isolated case is `semantics/pending/excord-login-item.contract`: `cargo run -p contract-difftest -- corpus semantics/pending/excord-login-item.contract`. App receipt: `contract verify apps/excord/app.contract --walks 20 --steps 40 --walk-seed 0x986bb432cbe497dd --confirm 2`. Keep module replies and their later resource changes in scope together when extending the oracle.

- **Photo Editor: camera and photo-library sends are outside the differential oracle** (2026-10-30, LLP 1151 lane G; pre-existing, §5). `getUserMedia` and `copyPhotoAsset` commit through the runner's module route (`runner/src/runner/modules.rs:355,368`; caller: `runner/src/runner/queue.rs:397`), while Lean asks the data transcript and refuses its missing answers (`semantics/Contract/Runtime.lean:851,206`). `semantics/pending/photo-editor-host-operations.contract` keeps one input per operation; reproduce with `cargo run -p contract-difftest -- corpus semantics/pending/photo-editor-host-operations.contract`. App receipt: `contract verify apps/photo-editor/app.contract --walks 20 --steps 40 --walk-seed 0x98a7091cbe8d1f60 --confirm 2`.

- **Harmony: Lean omits the Delete button generated by `row delete=`** (2026-10-30, LLP 1151 lane G). The receipt reaches Custom Emoji and exposes a semantics-embedding gap. Lowering inserts the button and its suffixed testId (`contract/lower/src/grouped.rs:664`, `contract/lower/src/swipe_rows.rs:146`); the Lean emitter traverses only authored children (`contract/cli/src/lean.rs:884`), and the semantics renders those (`semantics/Contract/Runtime.lean:390`). `semantics/pending/harmony-delete-row.contract` differs at boot with zero events: `cargo run -p contract-difftest -- corpus semantics/pending/harmony-delete-row.contract`. App reproduce: `contract verify apps/harmony/app/app.contract --walks 20 --steps 40 --walk-seed 0xd2dc0447e77b7cec --confirm 2`. Share the admitted list-row expansion with the embedding, respecting grouped/cell context and disabled semantics; the existing private lowerer and the CLI emitter span two crates, outside this lane's one-crate fix limit.
- **Eight apps' explore runs diverge at their first host operation outside the oracle** (2026-10-28, pre-existing: on `8233879705` they diverged at boot; after LLP 1148 stage 1 captures the boot's host facts they diverge at `registerLoginItem`, `getUserMedia`, `getCurrentPosition`, `selectContacts`, `addGeofence`, `cancelSpeech`, `exactTime` and a live time). The harness has no answer for a host operation; LLP 1148 D12's inventory is where they get one, and until then `verify` on such an app says `OUTSIDE` for nothing and diverges instead.
- **A timer cannot say it waits for a frame, a UI update or the end of a scroll** (2026-10-27, from Lexy's iPhone Duo traces; `issues/20261027-timers-cannot-wait-for-a-frame.md`; LLP 1147 drafts the change; `experiments/timer-wait-points/` measures the platform behavior it relies on). Precise timing stays the default; an explicit choice on `every` and `after` says what a timer may wait for. The issue has where Exact's clock fires today on Apple and the web, the device evidence, and the platform facts: the web's scheduling APIs in 2026, `CADisplayLink`, and `UIUpdateLink`'s phases and deadline.
- **What can still lose a request kept past its answer** (2026-10-28, LLP 1146 §8). LLP 1146 D2 keeps a let-go request with effects running until it lands: a macOS quit, the Apple driver's exit and the Linux host's orderly exit (an agent's quit, a headless run's end) wait for it within their bounds, and iOS holds a background task while one is in flight (`FinishHold`). A reload or an update ends the engine with the request in flight, and closing a window on Windows drains only the store (`host/windows/src/window.rs`): each loses its reply. Nothing protects the request on Android once the app is stopped, as the reader drains only the store at `onStop` (`host/android/canvas/src/main/java/dev/exact/canvas/ExactActivity.kt`), nor on iOS once the background time the system grants runs out: the app may then be suspended, and a process the system ends before the reply lands loses it. A rotating refresh token spent by a request whose reply is lost signs the person out at the next refresh; Lexy refreshes Lexus's rotating token about every half hour, and each refresh is exposed while it is in flight. The Windows close can wait as the Linux headless exit does (`exact_linux::teardown::finish`), driven on the NucBox; the Android reader can keep its process running while `must_finish` holds; a reload and an update need the request handed to the engine that follows.
- **Under the agent, Apple runs no app timer, platform timing included** (2026-10-27, measured for LLP 1147: `experiments/timer-wait-points/compiled/README.md`). `SessionClock.scheduleClock` arms no wake and `fireClock` and `advanceIfDue` return under `ExactEnv.agentMode`, so `scripts/fling.mjs` (LLP 1144 D1) never sees a timer's batch: Messages' `every(250, poll)` stood still across a real fling. Platform timing should run the app's timers on the wall's clock, as it already lands `then`s there.
- **A held-out OAuth case fails now and then on the Linux host under heavy load** (2026-10-27, LLP 1146 D7). Twice, a case that passes on the web failed once on Linux at a load average of 700 to 900 and passed on rerun; 64 runs of every profile's cases at 90 to 830 all passed, and no failing output survives. No known cause fits the two failures. Since 3b1a98297b, which landed after both, the Linux host writes the store on a thread drained at exit within a bound, and `reload` kills the app 2 s after closing its input (`scripts/agent-stdio.mjs` `close`), which under load could cut a write short and fail a case anew. Ruled out: `clock data`'s 20 s bound (the same on the web), and a kept connection the fixture closed, as it keeps idle connections open (`idleTimeout: 0`, `packages/oauth/test/fixture.ts`) and the Linux transport now sends a request again when a kept connection closes unanswered (expo/ibex#3, vendored). The held-out cases are withheld from the package's authors (`~/.cache/exact-oauth-heldout`): render them with `packages/oauth/test/cases.mjs` `casesFor`, never read them.
- **The local network's owed pieces** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.036 §14 as built). Apple's probe (D8) is not built, so a reach's allowance becomes evidence only through a Bonjour browse or resolve; the bake does not refuse a literal `browseServices(type=…)` no `net.dns-sd` line lists (admission refuses it, `SecurityError`); stage 3's conformance fixture in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, stage 4's seam tests, and the Avahi and windns browsers are not written. Every real-network drive needs a person: macOS's Local Network alert for the bundle, and drive 9 with a WLED light on an iPhone, an Android phone and Chrome (`packages/local-network/test/checklist.md`).
- **LLP 1133.021: Android's hosted views take no touches, and there is no headless session** (2026-10-27, §9 items 4 and 6). `exact-map` on Android does not pan or zoom by touch, and `MapView` hears only create, start, resume, pause and stop (not `onSaveInstanceState` or `onLowMemory`); a geofence crossing on Android waits for the next launch. map-demo has no Android crate. Each is owed by the RFC's stage 3.
- **LLP 1133.021 on Apple: drive 1 and drive 3's wheel need an unlocked Mac** (2026-10-27). With the console session locked, MapKit places no annotation view (its frame stays at the origin) and draws no marker, so `tap map/ferry` has nothing to hit; the window server gives no capture either. Run `bun packages/maps/test/map-demo.mjs macos` on an unlocked Mac. `tree` on Apple lists no package view's parts, which the web lists.
- **LLP 1133.021: what is not yet built or measured** (2026-10-27). Stage 4 (each package packed into an `exact new` app, and a third party's region module reaching `context.regions`); the simulator's background relaunch for a region under `--bind geofencing`, with its boot time and `backgroundTimeRemaining`; D21's region-carrier launch cost and the time to the map on an iPhone; Caltrain's arrival notice, which waits for `@exact/notifications` (§15 question 5).
- **@exact/nfc's Apple and Android stages: injected tags, their tests and the drives** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.035 §12 stages 2 and 3). The registries (`packages/nfc/apple/NfcRegistry.swift`, `packages/nfc/android/Nfc.kt`) are written but take no injected tag source in a test build, so the XCTests and JVM tests over claims, `busy`, `once`, writes, commands with their leases and two sessions sharing the registry do not exist. Not run: Shelf's drives on the iOS simulator and the Android emulator, drive 0 on macOS (and that its bake links no Core NFC), `--tvos --no-install` with no NFC key, the fixture's Linux drive (`packages/nfc/test/linux.mjs`), and the bound fixture on the simulator and emulator reading `available` false. The runner's permission continuation (`permits.rs`) does not call `arrival_claimed` when a write held on its permission becomes a value ticket, so a write allowed after another is held replaces nothing; the web runtime does.
- **The share extension on a device** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.024 stage 2). A device build skips it and says so until the team registers the App Group `group.<app id>` and the App ID `<app id>.share`; `entryProfiles` (`host/apple/devices.mjs`) then has to take the share extension's id beside the widgets'. A Mac build under an Apple Development identity packages it with the team's group, which no drive has run: the drives use ad-hoc builds and a scratch inbox.
- **Android's share target, what is owed** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.024 stage 3). Sharing text and a photo to Fieldnotes from another app's share sheet on an Android phone, and the web member's `share_target` in an installed Chromium, both by hand. The copy runs on exact2's thread with no five-second bound (D3), so a large warm share holds frames while it copies: move it off that thread, setting the token from there. The `shareIntent` hatch (D6) waits for a consumer.
- **Compose, what is owed** (2026-10-28, LLP 1133.024 stage 5). The Calendar example has no Android crate, so `@exact/compose`'s Android cell is compiled and not driven, and the iOS simulator drive was not run (CoreSimulator hung on `simctl list`); give it one (its contacts and calendar packages have Android cells) and run `examples/calendar/tests/compose.mjs android`. `share(files=)` on Android still carries text only: wire it through the reader's file provider (`dev.exact.canvas.Files`, `ExtensionContext.contentUri`) and drive a file share on the emulator. The hand steps of `packages/compose/test/checklist.md`.
- **LLP 1133.024 stages 6 to 8 are not built** (2026-10-28). Stage 6: the Rust implementation slot for the painted hosts (D22 item 4) and Windows's browser and compose cells, driven on the NucBox. Stage 7: print (the kernel's `print_layout`, the cut over pages, `@exact/print` and its cells, the Chrome oracle). Stage 8 waits on LLP 1115.004 S3.
- **The in-app browser, what is owed** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.024 stage 4). (1) The hand steps of `packages/in-app-browser/test/checklist.md`: Safari's view and Done on the iOS simulator and an iPhone, a Custom Tab and its close on an Android device with Chrome and on one without a Custom Tabs browser, the Mac's default browser, a new tab on the web. (2) What `androidx.browser` adds to Excord's release APK after R8, measured against a build without the module (its AAR is 229,576 bytes). (3) A module's `context.log` on Android goes to logcat, not the agent's journal, so `state`/`logs` under the agent never show it there; the drive reads logcat.
- **Payments: what is owed before stages 1 to 3 are done** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.034 §12's as-built notes). (1) The Apple conformance suite with `--bind payments` on the simulator beyond the wallet's status (the request from the method data, the sheet over the session's window and refused over an inactive scene, a dismissal answered `AbortError`), on the model of `packages/camera/test/apple.mjs`; and the cost probe. (2) A call with no `onmerchantvalidation` and an Apple Pay method on the web is refused `payment-merchant-validation` when made; D10 has the bake refuse it. (3) The web send is `"gesture": true`, not D16 item 7's adoption by `exact-payment-button` that the declaration's `adopts` names; Safari's call-stack rule may refuse the gesture path, and no engine here can show which (Playwright's WebKit has no `PaymentRequest`). (4) Drive 11 on an emulator with a Google Play image (none on this Mac), and the async lane's second tier building the fixture's APK (`bun packages/payments/test/android.mjs`). (5) Drive 6's third part (leave during a retry, then a second Order a Print: `busy` on the web, queued natively) is held by the runner's tests, not driven.
- **On the web, a modal presented right after another was dismissed slides in on the agent's clock, but `clock settle` does not wait for the slide** (2026-10-27, found driving Photo Editor's Order a Print). A `tap` on a control in it then fails "outside the viewport" until the clock moves on; `packages/payments/test/drives.mjs` `settled` steps the clock by 500 ms up to four times.
- **`exact-linux`'s `image::sampled_pixels_keep_natural_auto_axes_and_min_max_layout` fails** (2026-10-27, found running its tests for LLP 1133.034; it fails with that change to `host/linux` reverted too): `width=100` lays the 4000×2000 image out 100×0, not 100×50. The other image tests pass.
- **On the Mac, Caltrain's large home title is missing after returning from "Change station" in dark mode** (2026-10-27, found checking c9085fbcfc on main; it is there before that commit too). With the sky on, choose Dark, open "Change station", go back: "Mountain View" under "Next stop" is not drawn (the line below it and the cards are).
- **The web list's held scroll correction, reverted with the Photos commits** (2026-10-27). 10259a7646 and f076f25383 were committed from an older tree and undid 17cc8cac13 ("A jump that meets a fling waits for it to end, then lands"), so `issues/20261022-web-list-stops-short-of-its-end-after-a-touch-drag.md` is open again. A restore with three fix rounds is on branch `restore/fling` in Charlie's MacBook checkout (4cec3bd544): it drops a held correction when its request is cancelled, publishes the final aligned destination on completion, and cancels a request whose key left the items. Two findings stand (Astra): cancelling restores the anchor from the cancelled seek's unlanded destination, which can emit a correction that lands at once (300 → 900 when the last row is deleted); and a completed request's held correction is not revoked when its key is removed during the 100 ms rest.
- **The desktop modules, what is owed** (2026-10-27, LLP 1133.025 §14's as-built notes). (1) **macOS:** the XCTests beside `MenuBarMacTests` (a status item's title, image, menu, dimming and removal; the Dock menu's order; `focus()` showing a hidden window; the unpresented first frame); the cost probe (D13) and the time from launch to the status item's placement; §12.4's timer title and its App Nap drive; the sample host's two sessions reading one chord; a document app's status item moving to its next session. (2) **A hot key's press on macOS has the device's origin:** the Carbon handler is the package's (`packages/global-shortcuts/apple/GlobalShortcuts.swift`) and a press is its `emit`; D11 items 2 and 3 want an ExactKit `hotkeys` service that raises it as a person's activation, as `exact_linux::desktop` does for the Rust hosts. (3) **Q9:** whether `SMAppService.mainApp`'s login launch carries `keyAELaunchedAsLogInItem` needs a logout and login on a Mac (`packages/dock/test/checklist.md`, Mac 6). (4) **Windows, stage 2's rest:** the notification-area icon and its flyout, `TaskbarCreated`, `keepRunning`, the launch with no window, one instance and the activation record (Windows App SDK), the startup task, the pair's `WH_KEYBOARD_LL` hook, and the session running while its window is hidden (D11 item 8); then the NucBox drives. (5) §12.1, the T3 Code clone dropping its Carbon registration, is on `daehyeon/t3-code-main`.
- **The iOS simulator's WebKit download tests fail, one of them always** (2026-10-27, found landing the bake-off on main). `IframeWindowsTests.testADownloadNeverReplacesAFolder` failed every run on the iOS 27 simulator at main before and after the landing (`2d1b719713`, `e9421ad0f5`; two runs each); `IframeAutomationTests.testDownloadsGoToTheFolder`, `testPromptsOfAnAgentControlledFrame` and `IframeWindowsTests.testAFailedDownloadKeepsTheFileItWouldReplace` fail on some runs. In each, WebKit's download events never arrive. They test the web arm (`host/apple/webarm`), which the landing did not touch.
- **The painted presenter's focus ring keeps the last input's modality per node, where Chrome keeps it per document** (2026-10-27, LLP 1115.004 D9.2; `brush.ringless` in `surface_controls.rs` `focus_for_press`, `presenter/events.rs` `set_focus`, `presenter/arrive.rs`, `presenter/typing.rs` `key_shows_ring`). A press leaves its focus ringless, a key shows the ring, and a focus the app moves inherits from the one it leaves. Three review rounds closed the common paths; what remains follows from `ringless` naming a node instead of recording pointer-or-keyboard modality, as Chromium's `HadKeyboardEvent` does: a press on nothing focusable, or on a text field, forgets the pointer, so a later `focus()` rings; a finger on a canvas while a field keeps focus names the canvas; a key handler that navigates within the press window arrives as the press did; a canvas's focus fallback and a heading's focus handler that remounts can leave the wrong node named; on Android a key does not end touch mode for an arrival with no press; a pointer Back to a field's origin hides its `:focus-visible` look. A modality flag beside the per-focus decision (Chrome's model) would close them together. On Windows (`brush.winui_focus`, `keyless`) WinUI's model is that flag too: its programmatic focus shows the visual when the last input device was the keyboard, so a mouse moved after a key should leave a script's focus ringless (round 3 of the 2026-10-27 review: Astra, citing `focusmgr.cpp`). A bare modifier now counts on Windows.
- **Restore input focus when a blur handler opens another authored dialog.** On Windows, `a_blur_that_opens_another_dialog_keeps_the_newer_surface` fails in both `authored_dialog` integration suites: the second dialog stays open, but focus is on its container rather than its input. The fixtures contain no autofocus candidates. Excord's quick switcher and emoji picker pass native opening, typing and focus restoration separately. See `autofocus-integrated-linux-tests.log` under `target/windows-chat-comparison/`.
- **The painted split's list column is a clip the runner does not know** (2026-10-26, LLP 1115.004 D16). The list route keeps the window's width and is narrowed by a cover, and paint, hit testing and accessibility clip it to the column, but `frame()`, `elementFromPoint` and the intersection observer see the whole route: content overflowing the column (Shelf's filter row at 320) answers a point in the empty detail pane. A runner placement that clips without a host block, or the list as a block of its own with `beside_a_pane` taught to keep it live, would close it. Smaller: a list element destroyed and remade while empty loses its retained split, since `retained` holds its old `ViewId`; and a pane's text takes partial damage only where native scrollbars are off, so on Windows (`refresh_scrollbar_preferences` turns them on) the painter still repaints the window, as it did before the split.
- **A layer node on the Android reader can lose its drawing the way a slot did** (2026-10-28, found reviewing `host/android/canvas/.../Slots.kt`). HWUI discards the display list of a node that leaves its tree, and exact2 sends a layer's drawing (op 30) only when it changes. A layer whose drawing rows were all freed before the row taking it over was drawn (a waiting row, or one made on the worker) would draw empty, with any slots inside it. Slots now keep their drawings and are recorded again (`Slots.refill`, also when a layer's drawing is recorded); `Layers` could keep each layer's ops the same way and record a layer again as a row drawing it is drawn. Not seen in Excord.
- **Gradle's file watching missed edits to the Android reader's Kotlin** (2026-10-28, `bun scripts/agent-android.mjs build excord --apk` run over ssh on an M5 MacBook Pro). After `git apply` changed `ExactView.kt`, `:canvas:compileReleaseKotlin` was reported up to date, deleting `canvas/build` did not change that, and the APK carried the old classes; an earlier build's `libexactcanvas.so` likewise stayed stale until the merged native-lib intermediates were deleted. `GRADLE_OPTS=-Dorg.gradle.vfs.watch=false` made it rebuild. Not reproduced on another machine; if it recurs, `agent-android.mjs` could pass `--no-watch-fs` to its `gradlew` (a cold check of the reader's few hundred inputs).
- **Android presents no dialog or popover its author lays out** (2026-10-26, LLP 1115.001 D10's Draft paragraph). Linux and Windows now paint one in the window (LLP 1115.004 D10.7, `host/linux/src/presenter/alert.rs`); Android still refuses one ("not yet in Android's windows"), so Excord's switcher, profile, member list and pickers do not open there. The Draft names the five pieces owed, each its own work: the subtree's op stream in the surface's window (a modal bottom sheet in a compact window, a basic dialog wider, a `PopupWindow` for a popover), the page's contact path in that window, a hosted `EditText` per window, TalkBack through D13's provider, and the framework's motion.
- **Linux and Windows: what an authored dialog or popover still lacks** (2026-10-26, LLP 1115.004 D10.7; the last review round of `presenter/alert.rs`). (1) A field in one has no edit menu (right click, the Menu key, Shift+F10): `edit_menu.rs` refuses while any surface is open, and the menu is itself a surface, of which one is open at a time; the edit menu would have to stack above the box. (2) The page's scrollbars are painted after the box and its scrim (`paint.rs`, `paint_scrollbars` after `paint_alert`), so where one crosses them it shows on top; presses on it stay inert. (3) A `focus` handler that closes the box while it opens queues `open>closed` and then `closed>open` for the same toggle, so the `toggle` event reports it open (`queue_toggle` keeps the last state). (4) Desktop Linux presents no menu popover yet, and its refusal still says "Linux does not support popover presentation" (`navigation.rs`, pinned by `tests/pinned/host.rs`); the agent's `contextmenu` op needs a shell that makes windows.
- **Windows: an authored dialog and popover wear the neutral look until D12's Fluent column** (2026-10-26, LLP 1115.004 D10.7). The kernel's starts for them are the neutral column's (`kernel/src/arena/alert.rs`, contexts 13 to 15); WinUI's ContentDialog and Flyout metrics (an 8 px corner, a 1 px stroke, 24 px padding, 320 to 548 px wide; a Flyout's 16, 15, 16, 17 padding on Acrylic) are listed in D10.7 for that column. The popover is painted in the app's window, so it is clipped by it where a Flyout is not; and D10.7 asks Charlie whether an unsaid popover opens above its invoker, as a Flyout does.
- **A borderless image-only button on the Mac has no focus ring and a glyph-sized target** (2026-10-26, Excord's composer). Excord's + and the reply bar's × are the platform's default button, a push button on the Mac and an outlined box on the web, where Discord and Messages draw an icon. Making them `buttonStyle="borderless"` (tried, reviewed, dropped) shrank the Mac's to the glyph's fitting size, 15×13 pt, which is also its hit area, and with Keyboard Navigation on, Tab reached them with no ring: `drawFocusRingMask` on the image-only borderless cell paints an empty mask (Grok). AppKit's own borderless image buttons ring the image; the host could do the same and give the box a minimum target.
- **A box outside every route cannot clear the iPhone's status bar** (2026-10-26, Excord's image viewer, since made a full-screen route). An overlay drawn from the root, padded with `env(safe-area-inset-top)`, sits beside the Dynamic Island: under `viewport-fit="auto"` with a navigation stack `env()` is 0 (docs/agent-pitfalls.md, "A banner at the root of a tabbed app"). LLP 1123 D9's `var(--exact-safe-area-inset-top)` (stage 2, accepted) is the fix; until it is built such an overlay has no inset to read.
- **A scroller beside the primary one cannot pad the home indicator** (2026-10-27, Excord's server rail). The rail now reaches the iPhone's bottom edge, laid over the channel list's padding so the list spans the route and takes its clearance (LLP 1123 D3); UIKit gives the rail no bottom inset, so with enough servers to scroll the last one can rest under the home indicator. LLP 1123 D9's `var(--exact-safe-area-inset-bottom)` on the rail's padding is the fix.
- **Excord's Mac transcript drops frames while it scrolls on a real account** (2026-10-27, Charlie, by hand, on his account; first seen as a jerk past the end). A ⌥⌘T trace from his MacBook (120 Hz, 83 s of late frames) settles where it is not: the stretch past the end is free, since its hundreds of scroll events commit nothing. Of 82 missed frames, 8 followed the lists re-asking on every change elsewhere on the account, which the lists' own versions now stop (`live.ts`). The other 67 followed scroll events that moved the content: each took about 10 ms of the main thread (about 4 ms runtime, 4 ms apply, the rest unattributed) across about four kernel transactions, all with no tree change. An agent drive cannot stand in for it: under the agent nearly every batch the app applies is the agent's own (its clock steps and position reports; `docs/agent-pitfalls.md`), and no frame is presented, so a `sample` of one says little about a real scroll.
