A fixed-position editor's residual overlap shortens the innermost owner whose block is its fixed containing block, once, only under effective `resizes-content`; under `overlays-content` it stays covered, as on the web. Ruled (Charlie, 2026-10-23): an undocked, floating or split keyboard gets nothing beyond the editor's own caret scrolling (`followsUndockedKeyboard` is false by default, and no UIKit app moves content sideways); the both-axis reveal this bullet promises is withdrawn, to be revisited only if a real iPad shows a caret covered that a hand-built app would reveal.
- **The canvas through a trusted tunnel** (2026-10-19, stage 10 B2; theme2's Q3 ruling). `--trust-tunnel <port>` carries the design wire only: C's canvas gateway is TLS-only in stage 10, so a developer working through a tunnel edits the design without the canvas (LLP 1122 D12 allows it). Wanted, if asked for: a second tunnel port for the gateway (`--trust-tunnel <port>[,<canvas port>]`), with the gateway's grant and cookie rules unchanged.
- **Boxes between a placed block's parent and the view that holds it still clip it in point reads** (2026-10-14, LLP 1123 stage 1 review). A placement can be in an ancestor beyond the root's parent (`Scrolled::place`'s `within`: on iOS a tab's route is placed in the routes' owner, since UIKit's tab container holds the route beside its panel), and the runner shows the root in that ancestor directly (`shown_in`), so `frame` and the intersection observer leave the boxes between out, but `elementFromPoint`'s walk still descends through them, so a tab panel with `overflow` other than `visible` cuts its route where UIKit shows it whole (the part under a floating tab bar). Wanted: the walk enters such a root from the ancestor that holds it, past the clips of the boxes between, with a runner test of a clipping panel.

- **SVG lengths, what the viewport-unit change leaves** (2026-10-13, SVG viewport review; main already has these differences). (a) Static math and environment geometry: `host/web/src/element.rs:767` omits dimensions other than points, percent and the four admitted viewport units. A nested SVG's `height="calc(10vh + 10px)"` or `height="env(viewport-segment-height 1 0, 25%)"` uses the initial viewport on the web, including its document, while native resolves the length. `min()`/`max()`/`clamp()`, safe-area `env()` and `sv`/`lv`/`dv` units have the same gap unless the kernel simplifies them to points or percent. Dynamic JS geometry keeps main's CSS path (`host/web-js/svg-transform.js:13`): a nested SVG's CSS `height` does not size its viewport, while a `foreignObject` takes it; browser support for segment `env()` fallbacks also differs. (b) Paint-server templates: omitting an undefined dimension (`host/web/src/element.rs:767`) exposes the `href` template's value. A radial gradient with `href="#base"` and `cx="env(viewport-segment-width 1 0)"`, where `base` has `cx="20%"`, uses 20% on the web when segment (1, 0) is absent; the kernel selects the authored row and uses its initial 50% (`kernel/src/svg/server.rs:136`, `kernel/src/svg/server.rs:176`). (d) Static font-relative geometry and hairlines: the kernel converts `em`/`rem` and `-exact-hairline` to points before `host/web/src/element.rs:748`; main emits these numbers rather than omitting them. The JS template (`host/web/src/template.rs:56`) keeps the build's pixels: a nested `svg height="2em"` stays 32 when its font size changes, and a pattern's static hairline stays 1 at 2x, while native and the wasm host recompute them. Dynamic JS hairlines use main's CSS resolution steps, so 1.25x uses 1 CSS px instead of native's 0.8.

- **Chrome retains SVG viewport attribute geometry after window resize** (2026-10-13, SVG viewport verification; browser limitation reproduced without Exact). In an 800x600 window, a fixed 400x400 SVG containing `<svg width="10vw" height="10vh"><rect width="100%" height="100%"/></svg>` draws at 80x60. After resizing the window to 200x400, Chrome's length API reports 20x40 but its drawn box remains 80x60; WebKit and Firefox update it. The literal viewport attributes written by `host/web/src/element.rs:754` and `host/web-js/svg-transform.js:12` inherit this limitation. Main omitted static viewport attributes and wrote dynamic geometry as CSS, so this is a limitation of the newly admitted form, not a regression of a working main case. Native scenes resolve against the resized window.

- **Refuse dynamic SVG attribute-only lengths at runtime** (2026-10-17; LLP 1055.000 D4). Contract refuses deferred units and function text in literals, template text and conditional literals on line/linearGradient `x1`/`y1`/`x2`/`y2`, radialGradient `fx`/`fy`/`fr`, marker `markerWidth`/`markerHeight`/`refX`/`refY`, and SVG text/tspan `x`/`y`/`dx`/`dy`. Bound values, incomplete template or concatenation tokens, and expressions beyond the static check’s 4,096-visit budget can still send relative lengths to the web that native `Length::parse` ignores. The SVG scene consumers have no warning path; add validation at a shared runtime boundary with a named diagnostic. CSS geometry, `offset`, `orient` and filter primitive attributes are separate.

- **Resolved: EX3 native unbound-handle logout — one-round waiver (2026-10-12).** The coordinator acting for the repo owner granted exactly one additional round after the RULES.md three-round limit, with a stop-if-NOT-READY instruction. Logout now retires matching origin/principal partitions even when the caller has no held partition. The real-server regression covers private cached reads, authenticated and manual-headers siblings, same-account relogin, and old-handle closure. Full live suite: 24 passed / 285 assertions; dev and serve scratch browser tests passed. Both fresh confirmation reviews are READY: [Codex](llp/reviews/1086-wave8-ex3-waiver.codex.md), [Muse](llp/reviews/1086-wave8-ex3-waiver.muse.md). The P1 is closed; no further round taken. This resolved record is retained at the coordinator's explicit request. Original finding: [round 3](llp/reviews/1086-wave8-ex3-r3.codex.md).

Add an explicit online mode once the journal wire/identity contract is available to all Exact executors; it needs its own design and offline semantics, rather than silently bypassing the device. The app still builds the ~2.3 MB wasm meanwhile.
- **Wave 8 E9: grouped-list columns on day-two lane 006.** Deferred from EX3: reproduce on the same native presenter and app before changing cell projection; this workstream ran on Linux/Chrome and did not have the required Apple launch. The password helpers do not resolve inherited persona-specific tests; authors must update those tests when switching identity modes.

- **No runnable Exact+Snapback app in `apps/`** (2026-10-11, the sb4 farm's wave 2: 088; 051 found `select` driving only in the guestbook). The snapback4 README's guestbook builds and passes both its tests as written (extracted into a scratch app against 0.4.17, with `Snapback.switcher`, a `missing` and a positional testId), and `scripts/snapback-handshake.test.mjs` now extracts and rechecks both complete guestbooks against the pinned public-registry package (EX8, 2026-10-16). There is still no in-repo app: an `apps/` copy needs a backend, the device's wasm built from this checkout and a running `snapback4 dev`, which neither the gate nor the async lane has, and a README-extraction test would need Chrome as well. - **Remaining Snapback media work (EX8, 2026-10-16).** Durable upload-and-place now uses the device outbox and sync round, including lost replies and reopen. - **A tap on a file `input` holds no picker under the agent** (2026-10-12, sb4 farm wave 7: 006, 3 minutes; E6). Only `showPicker("<id>")` from an action holds one, so `tap "<the input>"` then `pick` answers `held: none`; the snapback4 README now says to tap the button whose action calls `showPicker`. Holding a picker for a tapped file input (the web glue, as a person's click opens the system's) would make the obvious test work; 040 also had to join four references for a PDF picker (grammar, the file-pickers corpus, Documents, test pick syntax), which one worked example would cover.
- **A named agent store reported busy after an interrupted drive** (2026-10-11, wave 4: 053, 089; 3 minutes). Not reproduced here: an interrupted drive could not be staged (a drive's virtual clock ends it in seconds). The lock is a live page's Web Lock (`host/web/storage-fs.js`), so the likely holder is a Chrome from the earlier drive still running on that store's profile; a drive could refuse up front naming that Chrome's pid, from the profile's `SingletonLock`.
- **A shell's wrong directory still costs a build a minute** (2026-10-11, wave 2: 019 048 061 093 094 096 099). `exact.mjs` resolves a named file from either directory, but the slips left are shell redirects (`cat > app/app.contract` from inside the app): bash refuses them, and the next `contract build` checks the untouched file and prints `[]`. A stderr line naming the file checked and how long ago it changed (`app.contract, changed 14 min ago`) would contradict a write the agent believes it made; not done, as it adds a line to every call.

- **The Mac's split, what is owed** (2026-10-13, LLP 1115.002 D4, stage 6b's as-built; `runner/src/runner/split.rs`, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/Mac/SplitMac.swift`, `SidebarViewsMac.swift`; records in `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/mac-split/`). (1) **The rule cannot tell a push from a Back or a sheet** when a row's `press` is an action: the plan holds an action's write set and no more, so a list whose rows only open sheets, or go back, splits with a pane that stays empty until its author says `navigationSplit="none"`. The route table also lacks an unmounted destination's presentation, and the recursive outer-press rule has no logical row-accessory boundary. Evaluate static modal destinations and nested row accessories across all hosts before changing this shared rule. The compiler knows which verb an action calls; the table's arrow key wants the same fact (below). (2) **Shelf now splits, but selecting another book pushes its route over the previous detail.** Its library is a `list` (LLP 1115.000 D16) and its unchanged test file passes 12 of 12 in both Mac chromes, including swipe deletion. Its `follow` still uses `go`; changing the selected book should use the list-detail pattern's `replace`. (3) **The page's view is the list's column**, so `vw`, and a width an app reads, are the column's while a stack splits (LLP 1115.002 D8.2 item 7 says the window's): the kernel's viewport is the page's view, and telling it the window's needs the root's block to be its pane. Chrome an app lays out around its stack inside the navigation root (a bar under every route) stays in the list's column for the same reason. (5) **The selected row** (a table's, or a painted list's drawn by `SplitHost.mark`). (a) A route is a row's detail by a `link` or by the row's press: one that comes any other way while the rows' press is an action (a link that arrives, Excord's ⌘K switcher) has no row selected. (b) Whether a press writes the router is its action's write set, not what the press did: a row whose action navigates only sometimes takes the shown detail when it does not. The runner knows what an activation wrote. (c) The host names the row by its press's view, so a windowed list's row that unmounts and mounts again comes back unselected; LLP 1127's association, by plan site and instance path, would keep it. (d) A click on a painted row leaves the keyboard where it was (LLP 1115.000 D7.1), so after a field had it the selection is grey, where a table takes first responder. (e) A painted selection does not slide with its swipe row. (f) A person has not compared the painted selection in a key window, or inside a grouped list's card, with a hand-built screen. (6) **Dark Mode's grounds differ in a split:** AppKit tints the window's own ground with the desktop (`#2c2a29` on this Mac) and a box painted in the window's background role is flat (`#1e1e1e`), so a detail that is a grouped form is darker than the list beside it. The same was found in a sheet (LLP 1115.002 D7.4); a painted role cannot be the tinted ground. (7) **The driver:** a plain `screenshot` is the page's view, the list's column; `check`, `sample`, `hover` and `tree --ax` were not run across the two panes; the pane's routes are the pane's accessibility children, not the page's. (8) **The other reading of Q10** is `SplitHost.listOwnsWindow`: run once on a scratch app with the constant turned (pictures in the records), held by no test. (9) Needs a person at a Mac: a drag of the divider and of the window's edge, the selected row's emphasis in a key window, the bar's items moving as a detail comes, a real Command-click to take the selection off, full screen, a 2x display, VoiceOver across the panes. (10) Not built: the iPad's half (the same rule, `UISplitViewController`). The web's stage 6d move and its test boundaries are recorded in LLP 1115.002 D4; integrated browser receipts live under `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/web-split-prep/`.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/b3, 47c581d58d, 006e16796d, 6bf174382c): the agent reads both panes (screenshot, sample, hover, AX) in the page's coordinate space; implicit split grounds are AppKit's. The rest of the list remains.

(UIKit's eight HDR/image failures are fixed by 6f1d9020a.) macOS reports border/selected-text colour assertions and `PressFeedbackMacTests` (the focused run expected black at 0.16 alpha but received white at 0.18). Compare against a hand-built control in the same appearance before changing expectations. - **Two things red on main that the list-rows lane met and left** (2026-10-13, on `40c6595df` with LLP 1115.000 D16 over it; none is in what that lane changed). (1) `bun scripts/smoke.mjs ios` on a freshly made iPhone 17 Pro simulator (iOS 27.0) reports seven failures, the same seven with the lane's press-path hooks taken out: Enter's key hint at the field, the canvas 1.25% beyond its reference's band, a tap under the keyboard, and four of the retained editor's focus. Not looked into; a new simulator's keyboard setting is the first thing to rule out. - **A list's rows as cells, what is owed** (2026-10-13, LLP 1115.000 D16's as-built row; `contract/lower/src/cells.rs`, `kernel/src/rows.rs`, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/RowCellsIOS.swift`, `host/web/neutral.css`, `host/linux/src/paint/neutral.rs`; records in `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/d16/`). (1) **The real UIKit-cell gate failed; ordinary lists remain painted.** The fixture implementation stays unlanded on `no-tells/ios-cells-gate-2026-10-13` at `f6f754da6`: the old collection survives the unchanged five-second release condition after session destruction and queued work, both in the combined 19-test run and alone with the identical binary. The feed releases; identify the collection's retaining owner before reopening the gate. Three lifetime repair rounds and seven extra are spent. **Ruled 2026-10-16** (Charlie asked for a decision; GPT-6 Astra, Grok 4.7 and Fable each recommended it): drawn rows stay, and the iOS tells on every screen go first (half-pixel edges, the status bar, the keyboard over a bar, type). Then a teardown-only investigation of three rounds names the retaining owner, starting from the grouped list's collection scroller (#502), before any adoption; drawn rows do not grow editing or multiple selection meanwhile, and VoiceOver list traits are worth a small slice if cheap. An earlier native-cell empty-editor hold also omitted AutoFill where the painted fixture and UIKit reference showed it. Latest-source delivered-touch reorder, paired release performance and the full landing matrix remain unqualified. Any later adoption must also qualify the bar/large title, `scrollIntoView`, restoration, keyboard avoidance, pull to refresh and `reachend` against the replacement scroll owner. (2) **iOS, what the drawn row does not do.** A separator starts at the row's first text; a row whose first text is not its title starts there all the same. Seen with a real touch on 2026-10-23: the pressed row's ground, the row selected under the screen it pushed, and its fade following a back swipe's finger. Not measured, so not matched: what a real touch's highlight does before it is a tap, a cancelled pop, an iPad's wider margin, 120 Hz, Increase Contrast, right to left. The driver's own `tap` presses without a touch, so it selects no row; a real touch (`--touch platform`) goes through the path that does, and its selected row was not pictured. (3) **Android.** The reader draws no divider, which is a Material 3 list item's default, read from Material's specification and not measured on a phone. A press shows nothing there, on a row as on any pressable (LLP 1115.001 D9), and a row keeps its author's height where Material's is 56, 72 or 88 dp. (4) **The web.** A box whose author wrote a top or bottom border carries `data-exact-said` whether or not it is a row, since an element's attributes are written from the node alone; telling it from what holds it would take the parent at the three places attributes are made (`document.rs`, `template.rs`, the live host). A row's line is under its children, so a child with an opaque ground at the row's bottom covers it. A child of a list that is out of the flow is still a row to the sheet and to the kernel. Not seen in Safari itself or on a phone's browser. (5) **The Linux desktop.** A row that is a link has no focus ring, as a link has none anywhere on that host. Not seen on a real display or with a real mouse; Windows compiled, never run. (6) **The rule.** A `display` or `flex-direction` that is no literal leaves a list unmarked. A list of `section`s is unmarked on every host (the grouped form's `listStyle="plain"` is the sectioned plain list). A plain list has no header or footer that is no row by the rule: Shelf's count and placeholders write an edge `none`, which is the author's "no separator here" and which only iOS needs; `header` and `footer` children of a `list` could be that. The terminal host draws nothing for the mark. (7) **Apps not converted.** Only Shelf's two were a `scroll` of hand-built rows turned into a `list`; other apps whose rows are hand-built in a `scroll` (look at `messages`, `fieldnotes`, `realworld`, `todo`) were not looked at one by one.

Separators were already row-owned on main (`891fd7749c`). The new host drag test lifts a ghost, moves it, repaints separators during displacement, drops and lands it; the line stays at the row’s window-space bottom. The lifetime test boots the grouped-list fixture in a scene-hosted ExactView and destroys its session with an animated collectio
- **The Mac's table, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D5, stage 3b's as-built; `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/Mac/TableHostMac.swift`, `TableFeedMac.swift`, `TableInkMac.swift`; records in `~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/mac-table/`). (1) **The frame budget on an idle Mac, and on a 120 Hz one.** The condition was met in thirteen runs of thirteen (the table's 95th percentile 8.9 to 11.9 ms against 13.33 at a 12,000 pt/s fling, release kernel), all in the quietest stretch a shared Mac gave (load 3.5 to 7.0), and an earlier cut went over in runs made while the load climbed to 9 and 20, where today's list did not; `sh ~/audits/exact2-no-tells-2026-10-11/mac-table/scripts/rerun.sh <checkout>` measures it again. Against a 120 Hz frame (8.33 ms) neither the table nor today's list holds its 95th percentile at that fling. (2) **What the compiler must say:** whether a row's `press` action writes the router. An arrow key that reaches a navigation row presses it (SwiftUI's list of navigation links, measured); the host knows a `link` to a route and nothing else, so a row whose action pushes is only selected. `Lowerer::unsplit` already works it out for the split, and stage 6b needs the same fact on the node. (3) **What the runner must still give the table:** a row's text when it is not mounted, for type-select (a stock table finds any row, this one mounted rows). The table now uses the runner's existing item index by key so an unmounted selection follows its item while the table does not have the keyboard; disappearance ends the selection. (4) **Kept painted, each said once in the log:** a list whose rows are in `section`s (AppKit's group rows), a list inside another list's row, a list with `reorderdrop` (the lift of a reordered row should be the table's own drag, D9), and a list that is no scroller of its own was written for and never run. (5) Items coming and going reload the table (`reloadData`): its row views are made again and a row's exit animation is cut, where `insertRows` and `removeRows` would keep them. (6) A row's chevron is not drawn but its box is still laid out, so a row's text ends that much earlier than a hand-built list's. (7) Not built: an inline run's own context menu in a row, `magnify` and a drop over a row (the table is what AppKit finds under the pointer), a native control's look in a selected row, an `svg`'s role paints in a selected row, and a swipe as a Mac user expects it (Delete, or the context menu) and multiple selection, which are vocabulary (LLP 1115.002 section 4). (8) Under load the table's tail has less room than a painted list's: its row views are made in the slice that builds rows, and the same travel back up at 5,000 pt/s was over the frame in two runs of six at the default quality of service (14.2 ms at worst).

Guard those behind `#if os(iOS)`/`canImport(AppKit)` or give tvOS its own branch; SVG Gallery builds and signs for tvOS already.
- **A phone's captures lack the keyboard, and `agent check` does not model sticky boxes** (2026-10-12, found verifying LLP 1116.008 D8). (1) `withKeyboard` (host/apple/devices.mjs) puts the keyboard into a simulator's window capture; a phone's (`--device`) has none, since the phone driver captures the app's window. Xcode 27.1's `devicectl device capture` captures the device's screen, keyboard included. (2) `AgentCheckIOS.swift` reads `style["position"]` for sticky and fixed, but the key is `position_type` (which has no `fixed`), so that test never matches. Reading `position_type` instead is not the fix: whether a covered control can be scrolled clear depends on how the covering box and the covered one each move under the scroller being judged. A box declared sticky may never stick (its natural and limit boxes are equal, its insets are `auto`, or it has an inset only on the other axis); a header with 10 points of sticky travel stops sticking before what it covers scrolls out; two controls in one stuck bar, or two bars stuck at the same edge, move together; a constraint can belong to an inner scroller while the outer one is judged; and sticking clears no sideways overflow. An approach that holds: over the room the scroller has in the clearing direction, move each box inside that scroller's content by the content's offset plus the change in `StickyConstraint.offset` of the box itself and of each sticky ancestor, counting only constraints whose scroller is that one (a box outside it stays put), and count the control reachable if at some offset its middle is inside the scroller's visible port and under no cover. A stuck row is not drawn over the form when the keyboard comes up (iPhone SE simulator, iOS 27.0, platform chrome and timing, 2026-10-16): a `position="sticky" bottom=0` row at the end of a long `scroll` sits just above the keys while a field above it has the focus.
- **RealWorld's app.js: hunt for link-by-use cuts** (2026-10-12, Charlie raised its budget 38 → 45 KiB with this line): 43.45 KiB brotli-11 at fe5b26798. Find runtime code shipped to every app that only some apps use (as the file-body path and Caltrain's grant/media/event families were moved), and lower the budget when a cut lands.
- **A subtree's containing block, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.002 D8.2, stage 6a's proof). (1) A change inside a block is laid out from the block's root: the replay at a clipping box (`LayoutTree::prepare_boundaries`) is taken under the page's root only, because the page's pass sees every change first and a box under another engine root is none of its boundaries. The same has always held for a second arena root. A long list scrolling in a split's pane will want it. (2) The Mac reads the `block` op through the adapter path (`BatchOp.Kind.unknown`, `BlockHost.applied`), so no shared Swift moved; the iPad's half adds the kind to `Batch.swift` and takes `Runtime.blocks` out of `Mac/BlockHostMac.swift`, with the iOS tests and a tvOS build. (3) `env(safe-area-inset-*)` is one environment, the window's: an iPad sheet or column has a safe area of its own, and no block carries one. (4) A subtree in a container's view has left the page's key-view loop, its accessibility parent is the container's view, and the driver's `tap` and coordinates know one window (read, not run; the document's "As built"). Since stage 6b a split's pane is in the page's Tab order and the driver taps in it; its routes' accessibility parent is still the pane. (5) `browser_host_block.tsv` was measured by a script that is not in the repository, since an agent adds none: modelled on `fixtures/browser_paint_order.mjs`, it also reproduces `browser_containing_block.tsv`'s 155 cases. Beside the fixture it would let the cases be measured again when the rule changes. (6) A content region and a block are refused together; an exclusion shapes no text across a block's edge.
- **The Mac's text and scrolling, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000 D7.7, D7.9, D8.2, D8.5). (1) Selected text's ink, the rest: an underline under a part-transparent role colour (a label tier) stays part transparent when selected, where its glyphs are solid (the recolouring keeps the line's own alpha); and a content region's role colours are its pair's, resolved in Rust, where a paragraph's are AppKit's own (before this too). (2) AppKit's own selected-text menu, the rest (`TextMenuMac.swift`): the text view it is asked of lays the selection out itself, so Look Up's highlight is on the page's glyphs only for a selection inside one paragraph (its first line exactly, later lines by the paragraph's pitch); across paragraphs, or in a giant paragraph, it is near them. Nobody has opened the menu by hand, chosen Look Up, Translate, Share or a Service from it, or seen its Writing Tools items act on read-only text. (3) The localised standard menu titles are not built, and wait on Charlie's choice (LLP 1115.000 D7.9; measured 2026-10-12 with probe bundles run in Japanese for the process only, macOS 27.0.1). AppKit localises no title an app supplies, by action or otherwise: Xcode's own Mac template is a nib with English titles (`MainMenuApp.xib`) that its developer translates. What AppKit does give in the person's language is what it builds itself: the items it adds (Writing Tools, AutoFill, Start Dictation, Emoji & Symbols, Close All) and the menus it hands out (`NSTextView.defaultMenu`: Cut, Copy, Paste, Paste and Match Style, and the Spelling and Grammar, Substitutions, Transformations and Speech submenus whole; `NSFontManager`'s Font menu), which leaves the application menu, Undo, Redo, Delete, Select All, Find, File, View, Go, Window and Help in English. SwiftUI's `App` does build every standard title localised (About, Settings…, Services, Hide, Hide Others, Show All, Quit, the whole Edit menu with `TextEditingCommands`, Minimize, Zoom, Bring All to Front, Help), so the one supported way to all of them is to let SwiftUI own the main menu: the Mac executable's entry becomes an `App` and Exact's File, Go, shortcut and Develop items become its `Commands`, which is a rewrite of `DevMenuMac` and `ShortcutsMac`'s menu half and belongs with LLP 1115.002. The other ways are the two ruled out: ship translations, or read AppKit's and SwiftUI's tables by key (`MenuCommands` has 25 of 48). Beneath all of them: an Exact app's bundle declares its localisations only when it grants a device (`usageKeys`), so every other app runs in English whatever the person's language, and then AppKit's own strings are English too (its alerts, panels, the text menu). A bundle that declares the app's `lang` and its strings tables' tags is the first step of any of these. (4) AppKit's focus ring has never been seen around an Exact field: it is drawn only in a key window of an active app, and neither the driver's picture, `cacheDisplay` nor the window's layer tree holds it (probes outside the repository, macOS 27.0.1; a screen capture needs a permission the lanes do not have). So three things rest on AppKit's contract and want one look by hand in a key window: a bare field with a border its author wrote shows AppKit's exterior ring around its box and the border under it (LLP 1115.000 D7.9, built); a bare field with no written border shows Exact's two-point ring on its box and, if AppKit draws too, a second ring outside it (the audit's MAC-31 infers it; LLP 1104 D6's note that "the masks alone never drew" was from the driver's picture, which shows no AppKit ring on a native field either); and a native textarea, whose stand-in ring may double its scroll view's. If AppKit draws them, Exact's rings go. A textarea with a written border draws Exact's ring outside its box in AppKit's colour, since its text view's own ring is cut off by its clip view, which is also inferred. (5) Under the agent a wheel is applied at once, with no momentum, and a window has no opening animation; a live gesture test passed 22 or 23 of 27 runs and is kept outside the suite. (6) The page hit test from #327 costs about 10 ms a pointer move on a 400-paragraph page in an unoptimized build. (7) `CTRunDraw` draws no underline, so a paragraph with per-run shadows or a hidden run loses its underlines. (8) By hand on a Mac, none done: a trackpad scroll's momentum and bounce in Shelf's list and a mouse wheel's step; the I-beam over prose, the hand over links, the arrow over buttons; clicking a row, a card and a button in a background window; hover in a background window and after a drag; the window's opening animation and no Show Tab Bar; spelling marks in a plain field; keys in a key window, with a custom `DefaultKeyBinding.dict`.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/b3, 75951c1102, b6f99c5171, be21561641, 31b12ee4ca): selected text keeps underlines and decorations on shadowed runs, propagated decorations at full ink, its measured fragments over exclusions, and flow truncation in source offsets. The rest of the list remains.

The kernel supplies header defaults below stored values and authored declarations, with live ancestry/prop invalidation. Other platform columns are unchanged. At `7d0be3297`, the tab-geometry slice passes the six checks, affected Linux/web-JS suites and Neutral CSS tests, ordinary Caltrain on Linux/Mac/iOS and Shelf in both Mac chromes and iOS. The four Linux fixture drives cover both widths and schemes, root-font changes and retained membership. Apple app drives use normal builds; iOS ran on an owned simulator that was deleted. (4) **A button whose author wrote a width narrower than its padded title** wraps the title in the padded room while it is measured, where the page drops the padding: the measure cannot tell a written width from a stretched one (the painter can, and the box is the written one). (5) The accent is the table's blue: a browser draws its own hue at the table's lightness (item 4 above), and these hosts have none to fit. A selection's highlight in a field is still the painter's own blue pair. (6) A file field always says "No file chosen": the Linux picker hands the files to the app and keeps no name. A date field draws its ISO value in the kernel's box for Chrome's date control, with no picker's icon. A `type="search"` field is as wide as any field (Chrome's is 150 px where the others are 176), and a select is as wide as its widest option in the host's font. (1) `markdown`, `markdown-stress` and `exact-live` gained `role="heading"` with `font-size="1em" font-weight=400` on their heading blocks to keep their look, and none was run on a host. (2) A pale blob inside a sheet where the page's checked radio sits is the driver's `screenshot … window` capture (the app's own drawing), not the screen: on 2026-10-23 the display's pixels (`screenshot` without `window`) showed none under the same sheet. The capture is what is owed. (3) Not run: VoiceOver's speech, a tap on a sheet's dimmed page, a hardware Escape, an iPad, the calendar opening from an empty date. (4) tvOS has been compiled with these changes and never run: this Mac has no Apple TV simulator.
- **Conformance after the Shelf bisect, what is owed** (2026-10-12). The lines Shelf and `synthetic-failed` gave are fixed, and were three things: the wasm page had no `showModal(id)` from an action (since `b968df0d8`), a tap the page refused left its reveal's scroll on the one page that was asked (since Shelf's first commit, `cf7196c0d`; the two pages never differed), and the JS target lost a resource's failure when a commit that had cleared it was refused (since `9ad0230e5`, #380, in the lane's own set). LLP 1116.005's "0 failures across 9 targets" named neither Shelf nor the synthetic plans: both were failing on that tree (`53205e43b`). Left: (1) The per-commit lane never drives Shelf: `scripts/async.mjs` `WEB_APPS` does not name it, so its lines stood for two days until two lanes ran it by hand. Adding it is a check, so it waits for a human's word, and Shelf is not ready for one: Discover fetches openlibrary.org for real, and in 2 of 9 runs the boot compare fell between the two pages' answers (21 lines at `boot`, `state resources.works…`, none after). (2) `conform.mjs` taps the JS page only when the wasm page took the tap, so whatever a tap the wasm page refuses leaves behind shows as a difference between pages that do not differ. The cause found is fixed in `reveal` (host/web/navigation.js); a target behind a modal `dialog`, inert with no `inert` attribute, is still scrolled to before its tap is refused as covered (read, not run). Tapping both pages and comparing the refusals would end the class. (3) On the dev Mac under load, runs of the lane's set give lines the next run does not: the wasm page's video in `video-player` has no duration where the JS page's has (37 lines, `state slots.duration: wasm 0 js 10`; here, and in a lane's run the day before); `harness: Chrome <pid> did not exit` after a drive that was equal at every step (ten targets in one run, at a load average of 90); and `synthetic-resize boot`, the wasm page's first size report in before the compare and the JS page's not. (4) The first run in a fresh checkout answers `linux-open: linux build is stale` for canvas-gallery, duo-lab and messages: the JS build writes `app.contract.d.ts` into the app's folder after `--build` made the Linux binary, whose dep-info names that folder (seen twice; a second run is clean). (5) The driver's rule counts `apps/<app>/android` as an input of the app's web build (`scripts/agent-launch.mjs` `webInputs` leaves out `apple`, `ios`, `macos` and `linux` only), so a change to Shelf's Android crate makes its web dist stale for a drive and for conformance. (6) On the web Shelf's swipe action is a 16 px `symbol:delete` drawn in the accent, though its button is `destructive`: a swipe moves the row 16 px aside, on both targets. (7) `scrollBy` is still the JS target's alone on the web, as `docs/contract-grammar.md` says; nothing holds either web target's commands to `HOST_COMMANDS`, which is how `showModal` was missed.
- **The focus follows the route on the painted hosts, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000 D4.2 and ruling 5, `host/linux/src/presenter/arrive.rs`). (1) A heading or a route that takes the focus after a key's press shows no ring: the painted hosts ring controls and fields only, where the web shows `:focus-visible`'s outline there. (2) The presenter is Windows's and Android's reader's too, and the rule was seen on neither: on Android a finger's press leaves no ring by the rule (LLP 1115.001 D7.6) and TalkBack's focus is the reader's own. (3) A pointer's press gives up its control's focus before its handler runs (`dispatch_press`), so that control hears no `blur` when the route's heading then takes the focus; the web fires one. (4) A commit's `focus()` command runs after its commit here, so the heading holds the focus for a moment first; where it ends is the web's.
- **The web's routes as pages, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.000 D4 part A). (1) A server-rendered page's `<title>` is the app's name until the runtime starts and sets the route's. (2) `tree --ax` lists a sheet's ancestors as "outside modal". (3) A menu does not take the focus when it opens; its arrows work once it has it. (4) A route that arrives without a press (a timer, Forward in the wide split) takes the focus only when nothing has it. (5) Arrows on a tab bar move the focus and do not press, since a tab's press can fetch; a tablist is one Tab stop. (6) Not run: Safari, Firefox and WebKit (`conform-firefox`, `conform-webkit` included), a screen reader's speech, a real finger; the focus ring's behaviour in Firefox and Safari is from HTML's rule for `:focus-visible`, not a run.
- **The filesystem helper's callers go one at a time, and a cold sixth check has two hundred of them** (2026-10-12, found repairing the wait they shared, LLP 1030.002 D3). Every Bun process that uses the helper runs its own `cargo build` of it under the build claim (`scripts/filesystem.mjs`), a no-op once it is built. With every app build script due and `exact-bake`'s tests beside it, 228 callers held the claim in turn: for 32 s at a load of 20 (median 0.13 s each) and for 68 s at a load of 165 to 205 (median 0.23 s). The cold build was 1.8 and 3.6 s of those, so the line, not the cold build, is the likelier place the old 60 s went (the failing gates, at loads of 300 to 800, were not measured). Nothing fails now, however long the line: a caller waits while the claim's owner runs. But the line is the check's time under load. A caller that saw another take the claim after it began waiting could use that caller's capture without building again: the sources would still have been checked during its own life.
- **Linux's authored accent, what the painting scope does not reach** (2026-10-14, after `roles::authored_accent`, the painter's per-node scope for `AccentColor`, `LinkText` and Exact's tint roles). Not covered, each found by review and none a few lines: (1) an SVG descendant that authors its own `accent-color` paints under its `svg`'s accent (`svg_item` opens no scope); (2) a retained content region's replayed SVG and gradient stops resolve with no scope (`ink`, `Gradient::resolved` at replay), so under a region-local accent they are the app's tint; (3) a native button's face parts (title, subtitle, symbol) resolve under the button's accent, not a child's own; (4) a theme alias to a tint role (`CanvasText = "LinkText"`) with a theme `AccentColor` resolves to the theme's colour before the scope is read (`painted_override`); (5) `accent-color="AccentColor"` keeps the accent around only inside the walk, so a hosted field or a region capture under it, with a nearer accent above, reads the tint; (6) colour motion targets (`kernel/src/motion.rs` `color_targets`) still resolve with no scope, so a transition into `AccentColor` under an authored accent eases to the tint and snaps at its end (a fix must keep Apple's `@tint` set, not the web's); (7) a recorded row (Android's recording backend) under `accent-color="AccentColor"` keeps an ancestor accent that changes after it was recorded: invalidation stops at the authored row (`txn.rs`) and a recording's validity does not compare its effective accent (`paint/rows.rs`).
- **iOS after wave 2's keyboard and bars, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.000 D9 and D11, seen on an iPhone 17 Pro simulator, iOS 27.0). (1) A pushed route's large title arrives expanded now (`9b3e3c110`; LLP 1115.000's row for D11.1 says what UIKit needs). What is left of it: a single stack installed partway through the first batch, where a cover cannot be reported, still gets its first screen's header cover after UIKit's first layout; UIKit opens a stack's first title all the same (in UIKit alone on iOS 27, a root's scroller moved up to its controller's top a turn after the first layout), but it is the cause the pushed title had. A header's cover held back by a list's slice in flight (`Session.whenIdle`) would reach layout after the push had begun, and that push's title would arrive collapsed: read in the code, not reproduced. Under the driver's own drag (no `--touch platform`) a scroller cannot be pulled past its top, so a collapsed title does not open there; a real touch opens it. A selected tab tapped again over content at its top under a collapsed title opens it (2026-10-20); but a scroller that never saw its title open holds only the collapsed inset in `scrollOrigin`, and an inset kept from another scroller goes stale when a hatch turns large titles off or a search field leaves the bar. (2) `user-select="text"` on iOS is built for a selection inside one paragraph (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.000's row "D11.6, `user-select`", `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/TextSelectionIOS.swift`). What it leaves: **(a) a selection across paragraphs.** UIKit's `UITextInteraction` takes a touch only when its view is the touch's hit view (measured: on a container whose text is in child views it never begins), so one selection over several paragraphs needs one view that takes every touch on their text and hands a tap on to the paragraph's own press and links; the document it would answer from is there (the Mac's order of paragraphs in `TextSelectionMac.swift`, the runner's `list_text` for a virtualized list's rows). The Markdown reader selects one block at a time until then. **(b) VoiceOver's text selection rotor**, which a `UITextView` offers because it is its own text input: the paragraph is a static text element. **(c) The highlight is over the glyphs**, a text view's under them: the container below the text is asked of the display interaction's delegate, which `UITextInteraction` owns (the reference's input was not asked for one). **(d) A triple tap** selects a paragraph in a text view; not measured here (the touch runner has no triple tap) and not seen. **(e) Linux, Windows, Android and tvOS take `text` as `auto`**, said in LLP 1001 and the guide but not by the host: one line in the Linux driver's log, as `font-stack-fallback` has, would say it where it happens. Selecting text there is the Mac's whole selection again (an anchor and a focus over the painted lines, the fill and the selected ink in the painter, a pointer drag, the clipboard), and `host/linux` has none of it. **(f) The driver's plain `tap` on text with a selection up** presses, where a finger's tap clears the selection and presses nothing; a real double tap needs the touch runner to make one (`XCUICoordinate.doubleTap`), and `tap <id> dblclick` is the recognized selection until then. **(g) Before iOS 18** nothing asks a text input whether it is editable; an empty input view is there to keep the keyboard away, on no simulator this Mac has. Not seen on a phone: the loupe under a finger, the haptic, a handle dragged, VoiceOver. (3) The page's own scroller (the viewport) takes the keyboard down with a drag as D9.1's default does (2026-10-22); no prop reaches it. (5) Under the driver's frozen clock, `tap <grouped list> drag 0 -300` on a list under a large title collapses the title and leaves the list at its top (the collection view puts its offset back over the bar's change of inset, as `KeyboardAvoidanceIOS.swift` `reveal` already works around); a real touch scrolls it. (6) `exact new`, the patterns and most recipes still write `navigationScroll` on a route with one scroller, which iOS no longer needs. D11.4 (a borderless field in a grouped row) landed as LLP 1116.005 D2 while this lane built its own, which was dropped; one thing that one had and D2 has not: `appearance="auto"` written on a field in a row does not bring the bordered field back. (7) Not tried: a real finger pulling the keyboard down in a `resizes-content` page (the driver refuses a drag that leaves the viewport, which ends at the keys), VoiceOver speaking a link, the ⌘ overlay on an iPad with a hardware keyboard, pull-to-refresh on a grouped list by a finger.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/g, 621d77563e, d699532ea3, 2d0eb37023, a47ee8f985, 5fa9ad1d85): title scroll positions kept and projected search fields focused; prose selected across mounted paragraphs with one text input (atomic `user-select: all` takes the outer element; a shared text touch reaches its paragraph's handlers); page drags track an interactive keyboard dismissal. Virtual-row retirement during selection remains.

- **Android's accessibility, what is owed** (2026-10-12, LLP 1115.001 D13, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md), "Accessibility"; seen on a Pixel 10 Pro XL with Shelf and `apps/unsaid`). (1) **TalkBack itself has not read a screen.** The reader answers accessibility services and the framework serves its nodes (checked from outside the app with `uiautomator`); TalkBack found them and focused the first node of two screens. But a touch or key from `adb shell input` goes straight to the app on that phone and never reaches TalkBack, and its speech is not logged, so its swipe through a screen, its double tap, its scroll action, and its focus in an alert and a menu are unseen. It needs a person with the phone for ten minutes, or the owner's word on a virtual input device (`adb shell uinput`: a keyboard for TalkBack's Alt keys, gone when its process ends), which is not among the things a lane may do to a shared phone. Until then D13's gate is not passed. (2) TalkBack asks for the notification permission each time it is turned on there and the prompt does not close on Back: a lane that turns TalkBack on leaves that prompt in a background task unless someone answers it, which is the owner's to do. (3) `Tab` and `link` are English role descriptions: the reader has no strings by language. (4) With a service on, a scroll states the whole tree every frame; coalesce it to the scroll's end if TalkBack's own scroll stutters. (5) A swipe row's trailing action has no accessible action of its own (the row's menu has it). (6) A polite live region that arrives with a new route is spoken once where the app has no top app bar. (7) The reader's click, long click, scroll and set-progress actions were not reached on a phone (only accessibility focus was); a hosted web view or video under the provider was not tried.
- **Shelf's test file is 11 of 12 on the Android reader about one run in six, and the test that fails is not Discover** (2026-10-12; `bun scripts/agent.mjs android --reader --app shelf --test apps/shelf/app.test.contract`, two Pixel 10 Pro XLs). This entry said "discover shows an error, then tries again" passed on a reader built from `a0600e637` and failed on one from `79d468eaf`. Run again the same day it failed on neither, nor on `c48f4e0ea`: 26 runs of the whole file over the three builds (five with `79d468eaf`'s own driver) and 15 of that test alone or by hand, none failing. After `pass fetch` that test's retry is a live fetch of openlibrary.org from the phone (236 to 676 ms that day), so a network that stalls leaves `discover-error` in the tree as the entry described; "Android's bars" (8) below saw one such fetch time out. No commit broke it. What does fail, on all three builds (4 of the 26; 2 of 16 run alone), is "a book: progress clamps and moves the status, rating, notes" at its last step: `tap "detail-status-want"` straight after `type "notes" "Statues"` answers "view 316 is covered or not hit at its projected center". The keyboard that `type` raises is told to exact2 within about a second, between the driver's requests: the viewport goes from 443 by 896 to 443 by 548.5 and the page scrolls 137 points to keep Notes in view, so the control goes from y 212 to y 75. Of 16 drives that read the control's place and then tapped it, 12 pressed it where it was, 3 where it went, and 1 was refused. A person sees a correct screen throughout. Not found: which of the reader's turns the refused tap lands in (the window told, its paint, the scroll `reveal_owed` makes after that paint, that scroll's paint). `Presenter::boxes` does not repaint for an attached display, and the Canvas host's agent (`host/linux/src/canvas/agent.rs`) takes one display turn after a request and none before it. Find the turn, then either `type` answers only once the keyboard it raised has been told and shown, or the agent takes display turns until none is owed before it reads a request. A `CanvasHost` has no test in `exact-linux` (its boot sets the process's environment), so the fix wants a test binary of its own.

- **Android's text fields, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.001 D8, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md), seen on a Pixel 10 Pro XL with `apps/unsaid`'s form). (1) Composing text has not been seen on a phone: Gboard there, typed on by tapping its own keys, sent `commitText` a letter and never `setComposingText` (`x_log_fields=1` logs the calls), in this host's fields; whether it composes in another app's field on that phone was not tried, and no one has composed Japanese, Chinese or Korean. What a composition does across an app's commit is held only at the Rust boundary (`presenter/hosted_tests.rs`). A value the app writes while a field composes is held until the composition ends since 2026-10-12 (LLP 1115.000 §5, ruling 6: `Native.fieldComposing`, `presenter/hosted.rs`), built without a phone: that `Fields.flush` sees a composition begin and end (the composing span on the field's text, read after each of the keyboard's calls), that nothing restarts the keyboard inside a word, and that the action key commits the word before the app's value replaces it, are unseen on a device. (2) Every field's view is made as its page appears, 0.6 to 1 ms a field on the main thread: the frame that showed the form's twenty-one fields took 26 ms where painted fields took 5 to 8 (`dumpsys gfxinfo`). Make a view only once its field is near its port, or ahead of need while the thread is idle. (3) A swipe row, a reorder grip or a `pan` under a field never gets a touch that lands on the field. (4) A field inside a layer the reader animates is drawn moving while its view waits for the next paint. (5) A font declared by its bytes sets the field in the device's default family. (6) The reader has no test of its own (no Gradle test task is built anywhere): `Fields.kt`'s rules for a told value, the caret after a rewrite and the action key are seen only by driving the phone. (7) Not seen on a phone: the light scheme, a font scale other than 1, a hardware keyboard, autofill, a field under a bar or a sheet, `setSelectionRange`, a disabled or read-only field, TalkBack.
- **Android's colours and channel, what is owed** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.001 D4 and D5, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md), seen on a Pixel 10 Pro XL with `apps/unsaid`). (1) The channel carries no file picker yet (`showPicker`, `saveFile`, the document pickers are refused, and say so), and `@exact/notifications` has no Android implementation (LLP 1133.004 stage 3); a date or time control's picker is LLP 1115.001.000's. (2) Done 2026-10-14: the painter resolves `AccentColor`, `LinkText` and Exact's tint roles by the nearest authored `accent-color` (`roles::authored_accent`). (3) A `link` left unsaid is drawn in the text colour with no underline, where the web's is `LinkText`, underlined. (4) The painter's own colours that are not roles are still Chrome's and iOS's on Android: a field's fill and border, the bordered button, a switch's off track, a field's selection highlight (D9). (5) The palette's derivation for Android 12 and 13 ran only forced, on Android 16; and a wallpaper change reaching a running app was not tried on a device. (6) The Sharesheet's `dismissed` is inferred half a second after the activity is in front again; a device that tells its choice later than that journals `dismissed` for a share.
- **The Android reader is in the tree; the benchmark still builds its own copy** (2026-10-11, LLP 1115.001 D2, [`host/android`](host/android/README.md)). The Canvas host's Kotlin side came in from the benchmark's copy at `c30c683` (`expo-build-1000:~/bench/lists/exact-v11/android/canvas`; its history is not imported), with the benchmark's driver cut to a seam (`ExactActivity.drive`) and the package renamed to `dev.exact.canvas`. `bun scripts/agent-android.mjs build caltrain --apk` builds Caltrain on it (drawn on a Pixel 10 Pro XL that day). Owed: port every commit the benchmark's copy takes after `c30c683` (`git log c30c683.. -- android/canvas` there); move the benchmark to consuming `host/android`'s `:canvas`, its `Driver` attached at the seam, because `canvas_jni!` now defines `Java_dev_exact_canvas_Native_*` and the benchmark's own reader (`dev.exact.bench.exactcanvas.Native`) finds none of its methods in a library built from this commit on. What the import does not do is in the README's last section.
- **`agent ios` wheel on a grouped list stops short of its end** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.003): `Agent.scroll` clamps to the collection view's `contentSize`, which a self-sizing grouped list only estimates until its cells come in, so one `tap <list> wheel 0 20000` landed rows short (row 29 of 30 under the tab bar, row 30 unseen) where drags reach the end. Clamp again until the size settles, as `TabEdgeIOSTests.scrollToEnd` does.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/a2): not reproduced in three drive rounds on an iPhone 17e; `Agent.place` already reclamps after layout (at most four times). A characterization test covers one wheel reaching the measured end (TabEdgeIOSTests); when the short wheel reproduces, reclamp until contentSize and the last index path stop changing.
- **A grouped list's cell kept across a batch that changed the list's height shows empty on iOS** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.003 D2.1): the keyboard batch puts every carried row back (`prepare`), then `mount` carries rows only into `visibleCells`, judged before the new layout; a cell left out but kept (not reused) comes back into the port empty, its row hidden beneath the list, until a batch touches the list. The keyboard's reveal carries rows after it scrolls (`carryRows`, KeyboardAvoidanceIOS.swift); a finger's scroll after the keys come up does not, nor the driver's: after Return moved focus and a submit added a row, `tap` on the next exercise's field answered "… covers its middle" (on f6769a64e too). Likely the same family as round 4's blank Finish row below. Checked 2026-10-22: the stated mechanism does not occur in a bare `UICollectionView` on iOS 27.0. `visibleCells` keeps its set until UIKit lays out (14 cells before and after the frame shrank, 4 after layout), and every cell that leaves gets `didEndDisplaying` and then `willDisplay` when it returns, which carries its row. A scratch list of field rows, with the keys up, a press that changed every row and drags both ways, showed no blank cell. The cause is still open.
- **The first `contract` call on a cold checkout builds the compiler for minutes** (2026-10-11, from closing `issues/closed/20261009-agent-port-cost-vs-swiftui.md`): `scripts/exact.mjs` builds `contract` on first use, so a new app's first `contract types`/`build` waits on a cold cargo build. `host/web-js/module.mjs --prebuild` warms only the web builder; a cached or prebuilt compiler would remove the wait from an agent's first minutes.
- **iOS scrolling's leftover suggestions** (2026-10-11, from closing `issues/closed/20261002-scroll-efficiency.md` §7–§8, whose three changes landed in 7098e9537, edaaa7c90, 1ff7407af, a1cd643d2): a 120 Hz device trace first; then per-frame checks driven by what is actually owed, the macOS PressClock on Core Animation's clock, and a per-frame budget for a fling.
- **Windows app-storage removal: the progress guard and a real batch test** (2026-10-11, review of the storage-results fix): `vendor/ibex/crates/ibex2/src/stdlib/windows_fs.rs`'s recursive `remove` drains `names_batch` until it is empty or a call errors, with no `require_progress` as Unix has (`app_fs_unix.rs`), so a file held open with `FILE_SHARE_DELETE` (delete pending, still listed) fails `fs.rm` though the pass made progress. `windows_fs_tests.rs`'s `recursive_remove_drains_more_than_one_directory_batch` uses 300 short names, under the 16 MiB listing cap, so it passes without the batching. Port the guard, make the test exceed the cap, and run both on the fleet's Windows machine.
- **Surface-control overflow-child multitouch and cancellation** (2026-10-11, review of #421): two fingers on the overflow child are not tracked because the child lacks `isMultipleTouchEnabled`; a wrapper disabled mid-press can lose cancellation; a focus callback moving the label before the touch id is stored can strand a held control; a link and a blank spot lifted in one `touchesEnded` can skip the link. The round-3 per-touch child route was descoped; design and test a UIKit-delivered route before restoring it.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/k): parked after three rounds; no change.
- **Shared Elements web zoom can lose its Close button in captures** (2026-10-23, the Shared Elements repaint): drive the grid viewer and close it, open and close a card, finish a task, then open a Zoom photo, settling each flight and switching light/dark preferences between screens. The Close control remains tappable but can disappear from screenshots, and `check` omits its text. Reproduces with the original literal colours as well as roles; investigate the web presentation separately.
- **This repository's apps earn `lower-colour-one-scheme`** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.002 D1): rebuilding the listed graphical entries on 2026-10-24 reports 148 warning sites outside Caltrain: examples/calendar 108, weatherlight 1, messages 33, video-player 1, carousel 2, sparkline 1, llp 1, exact-live 1. Caltrain's graphical entry uses roles and a light/dark accent; its one remaining warning is the fixed sky tint that dims the artwork on TV. The sky's fallback colour also stays fixed. Its glass cards change fill with the scheme; web checks and screenshots no longer show white text on light cards. GPU material rendering still needs a machine with a graphics adapter. Video-player retains pure black for its video letterbox; its chrome uses dark-scheme roles. Weatherlight retains one literal for its blue sky fallback artwork; the overlay uses dark-scheme roles. Carousel retains two white card labels on fixed content hues; its chrome uses roles. Sparkline retains black avatar letters on fixed data hues for contrast; its chrome uses roles and its status pills use light/dark pairs. Its measured baseline was nine warning sites, not ten (SVG paint is exempt); eight were removed.
- **Realworld's and Expose's dark accent fills take Apple's white `AccentColorText`** (2026-10-23, the Fieldnotes repaint's review). `AccentColorText` is white on iOS and the Mac in both schemes (`kernel/tables/schema.json`, its row: `whiteColor`, `alternateSelectedControlTextColor`); only the web picks black or white by the fill's lightness. Realworld's root accent is `light-dark(#238136, #5fcf70)` and its filled buttons, toggles and favourite chips write `AccentColorText` on it (`apps/realworld/app.contract:95`, 439, 930, 1057): white on `#5fcf70` is 1.97:1 in dark mode on Apple. Expose's `signal` fill (`light-dark(#b83d00, #ff6a2b)`) under `AccentColorText` (home badge `apps/expose/parts.contract:167`, `app.contract:414`, 434, 558) is 2.86:1 there. Computed from the table, not seen on a device; the web is fine. Fieldnotes names its ink per scheme on its green fills (`light-dark(#ffffff, #000000)`); the same would do here, or a darker dark accent where its text use allows.
- **`-exact-fit` is not fitted inside a content region** (2026-10-11, LLP 1116.001 D1.4): the kernel's region layout (`kernel/src/region`) measures text by its own path, which `layout/fit.rs` does not reach, so a fitted text in a region keeps its size. Fit there when a consumer puts a readout in one.

Check with VoiceOver on a device that UIKit offers them as the cell's custom actions, as a table's one-row projection relied on.

- **CSS values builders reach for and Contract refuses** (2026-10-10, bake-off round 1; focus-timer, recipe-finder): font-size calculations remain refused, including `clamp(14px, 16px, 20px)` as well as viewport-relative values; qualifying text sizing needs an Apple host. A unitless line-height template (``line-height=`1.5` ``) compiles without the quoted-literal diagnostic. - **Snapback 4 client bugs from the bake-off** (2026-10-10, LLP 1116 §9; the bake-off moved to a plain REST server, so these are their own track): one partition per native device against the answer model (two resources on one partition refreshed in one action → "native call outside an answer", "pending on nothing"); a cold first sync of 176–650 ms over the 100 ms source budget (fresh launches sit on "Connecting…" until the poll); on iOS every successful write hung until relaunch while the 4 s refresh stopped (team-kanban); on the web reads stayed stale after the app's own writes (event-rsvp); the README's "Synced" and "Refused: CODE" lines were copied as UI text in 4 of 5 apps. Reproductions: `~/bakeoff/rounds/r1/analysis/batch-c.md` (9 bugs), apps `~/bakeoff/rounds/r1/apps/{team-kanban,chat-rooms,event-rsvp,household-list,polls}-exact2`.
- **A prerendered web page shows a persisted state's declared value until its module boots** (2026-10-10, LLP 1116 D5). The JS target reads `localStorage` as a persisted slot is made, so a page the build renders no document for (the dev loop, an `exact new` app) is right on its first paint; one rendered at build (`--render rust|js`) paints the declared value, then the module adopts the page and shows the kept one (a structural difference abandons adoption and builds afresh, journaled), and an `activate="interaction"` page waits for a tap. Either hide the root before first paint when an `exact.state.` key exists (the capture script) and show it at boot, or render no document for an app that persists a state. Neither is done: no app that persists renders one yet.
- **The checks' workspace `cargo test` skips exact-linux** (2026-10-10, the agent-tap review): `host/linux` is a workspace member but not in `default-members`, so `cargo test --lib --bins --tests` in the gate never runs its lib or pinned tests; a stale pinned test (`host/linux/tests/pinned/host.rs`) kept passing the gate while it failed under `cargo test -p exact-linux`. Proposal for Charlie: add exact-linux to the gate as host/web and host/apple were (or to `default-members`). Cost: its build and tests add minutes to every check run (measure before deciding).
- **A handed-off module turn's outcome is not reserved** (2026-10-09, LLP 1041 §8.4 amendment, Astra's review). `next_job` charges a handed-off turn nothing more when it starts, and `complete` adds what its outcome retains without checking the ordered lane's 512 MiB, so several large outcomes can pass it. Bounded today only by the sixteen (a turn is an effect until drained). Reserve its ceiling at hand-off or backpressure the owner.
- **The re-ask herd** (2026-10-09, LLP 1041 §8.4 Q6). Each delivery re-asks every waiting answer: twenty rows awaiting one shared load take 62 pumps, 300 take 430 (`js/tests/it/admission.rs`). Ask again only the waiters whose promise chain moved (the prelude can tell after a drain), with tests for completed calls, unclaimed fetches, storage-head changes and the final "pending on nothing" check.
- **Storage reads at the sixteen** (2026-10-09, LLP 1041 §8.4 Q2). A TypeScript module's storage step is an opaque effect, so many answers each awaiting storage before a shared load (the Bluesky clone's `hiddenFirst`) still meet the sixteen. Classifying needs the seam to mark an identified read-only operation or batch.
- **`new Date()` works in a data module under bun tests but fails the iOS bake** (2026-10-08, the clone's group 7: muted words' expiry): the bun test environment does not match the data-module runtime. The shared prelude refuses ambient time on purpose (`js/src/prelude.js`), while a `Date` built from an explicit value works, so it is the bun tests that should refuse `new Date()` with no argument, as the bake does. The clone passes the wall time in (`noteWall`).
- **Two message rows drew on top of each other while a sheet was up over the conversation after the keyboard went down** (2026-10-08, the clone's conversation view): cleared when the sheet closed.
- **No way to read the host's root font size** (2026-10-08, the clone's font-size setting): `setRootFontSize("87.5%")`/`("125%")` now scale Dynamic Type instead of replacing it (LLP 1121 D6), so Smaller/Larger need no read; the size itself still can't be read back, if an app needs it.
- **Cross-browser conformance drives Chrome once per engine; a recorded Chrome drive could serve both** (2026-10-09). `conform.mjs --browser firefox|webkit` opens Chrome and the other engine for every target and drives them step by step together, so each async-lane commit drives Chrome three times per target: in the main run (wasm against JS) and in each cross-browser run. The main run's Chrome cannot stand in as it is: it pins only the media clock, the cross-browser runs also pin `line-height: 1.2` (`parityScript`), and it is compared against the wasm page, not recorded. The change: drive the JS page in Chrome once with the cross-browser parity, record each step's state, tree and layout, and have Firefox and WebKit each drive alone against that record. It takes Chrome out of both cross-browser runs, about half their work (Firefox 282 s and WebKit 176 s pooled). First read `drive()` for any step that depends on what Chrome shows mid-drive (an aim, a wait on its state). A smaller step: run Firefox and WebKit in one invocation sharing one Chrome per target, a third of the cross-browser drives.
- **The drift line can misname packages when two sources share a name and version** (2026-10-08, LLP 1086.000.000 §7; Astra's round-4 code review, left open by Charlie's ruling at the fix-loop cap). `lockDrift` (`game/app/shells.mjs`) matches a lock's dependency strings to packages not quite as Cargo does: an unqualified edge to a name and version both a path and a registry package hold follows both (Cargo prefers the path package), and a git-qualified edge may carry no commit fragment where the package's source does (Grok read Cargo the other way). Only the names in "exact2 moved … (added: …; updated: …)" are affected; whether the line appears, what `lock` writes and every build are not. Match Cargo's `encode.rs` lookup and add both regressions.
- **Scrolling a list to a row needs the list's id as a literal** (2026-10-08, the clone's group 3): `type-scroll-into-view` takes no computed id, so N pinned Home pages are named `p0`…`p11` and the logo's scroll-to-top is a chain of literal calls; a 13th page has none.
- **An indicator measured from a row's tabs goes stale when the tabs change and the row's box does not** (2026-10-08, the clone's Home tab row): `resize` follows the row's own box, as `ResizeObserver` does, so new labels of the same total width give no event; an app measuring its children needs to re-measure on the change itself (the clone measures again when its tabs change). A pitfalls line, if anything.
- **`agent ios` cannot tap a hidden tab button** (2026-10-08, the clone): `tap tab-me` answers "native segment is unavailable"; a drive must use the visible bottom-bar buttons (compare the Native Fixture segment entry below).
- **One SF symbol drew at two sizes across renders of one sheet** (2026-10-08, the clone's interaction-settings sheet): the same `symbol:sf/circle` with the same `width`/`height` measured differently in two captures.
- **Excord's window crosses the data seam whole on every page** (2026-10-29, measured on an iOS 27 simulator, provisional: host-dev Rust, debug Swift; ring deferred by theme2 12:00). Every page that lands refreshes `win`, so loadWindow's whole window crosses again: the prelude stringifies it and the runner decodes it. Medians of 33 pages (fixture `--history-delay 600`, #general flung to the 300-row cap): the crossing (stringify + decode) is 0.75 ms at ~50 rows held (a 57 KB reply), 1.8 ms at ~150 (153 KB), 3.5 ms at ~250 (283 KB) and 4.7 ms at ~299 (379 KB), about 12 µs a row; the page's whole fulfil is 11-20 ms, and at the cap 10 of 18 pages land in a native-scroll hitch of 19 ms (median; 27 ms max), so the crossing is about a quarter of the late frame. Against the lead's gate (≥3 ms a page or ≥1/3 of the late frame) it passes the first only at 250+ rows. The deferred design is reload1's six-chunk ring (~/x2theme/reload1-excord-window-plan.md: `win` a descriptor, six chunk resources, a page re-asking one chunk), which would take the crossing to ~0.75 ms at any size; the measurement is ~/x2theme/x2p4-excord-crossing-measure.md. Revisit it if, after the cheaper levers (loadWindow's flat 3.5-4.8 ms call, the ~8 ms of re-evaluation at the cap, a page landing outside the scroll callback), the 300-row cap still shows late frames on a device.
- **A list's data update is O(rows) in the size index** (2026-10-03, Expose's heavy list on Android; measured again 2026-10-29, parked by theme2). `SizeIndex::splice_keys` copies every row, rebuilds the `SumTree` and shifts every key's position, and `rekey_shared` walks the whole span between the shared prefix and suffix. On main 2b9447b370 (release, the live-tick heavy list, medians of 40): at 10,000 rows a splice costs 70-100 µs on an M-series Mac and 330-400 µs on an arm64 emulator (provisional; no phone was available), flat in the change's size (a 1-row top insert, a 50-row page and an append alike), about 40% of a structural commit (a top insert plus a reaction: 0.245 ms on the Mac, 1.2 ms on the emulator); at Excord's 300-row window it is 2-6 µs (23-43 µs for a 50-row page); a row's data change under the same key never splices. What else stays O(N) at 10,000 rows: `keys.splice` with the `string_keys` scan, 30-50 µs, and `compare::shared`'s prefix and suffix scan, 16-25 µs. The approved design (~/x2theme/x2p4-sizeindex-plan.md; scratch benches in ~/x2scratch/x2-perf4-si-scratch): a counted B+ tree of rows with per-node count, sum, min epoch and zeros, positions by leaf rank (a key maps to its leaf), a splice per changed run in O((changes + 64) · log rows); anchors keep today's old-neighbour fallback through a splice log kept only while an anchor is outstanding, instead of a snapshot of the whole order; `string_keys` becomes a count. Take it up when an app shows 10,000+ rows on a phone, or a phone tick measures over about 2 ms.
- **Pictures a GPU painter holds need no CPU copy** (2026-10-03, Expose on Android). A cached decoded picture is CPU pixels (`image/bitmap.rs`) and, once a GPU painter shows it, a texture too. Android's hardware bitmaps keep only the texture, so `VmRSS` (the list benchmark's peak RSS) leaves them out: Views' 164 MB of photos are graphics memory, Expose's 113 MB cache is native heap. The Canvas host's pictures already decode into `AHardwareBuffer`s its reader draws (`image::hardware_pictures`); a GPU painter could take the same buffers as textures (an `EGLImage`, a Vulkan import) instead of uploading CPU pixels.
- **Apple/Linux executor: a root swapped for a symlink between its check and its first open is pinned** (2026-10-08, review of the lazy app roots). `body::open` refuses a root that is a symbolic link, then `create_dir_all` and `AppDirectories::new` follow one put there in between (the same window existed when the roots opened at boot; storage's own `Session::configure` follows too). Closing it needs a no-follow root open (`O_DIRECTORY | O_NOFOLLOW`) in ibex2's `AppDirectories`, used by both. The first open is also outside the request's deadline and abort (three `create_dir_all` calls in the app's container).
- **Apple: images linked by use** (2026-10-08, LLP 1047.001; measured, not built). Only an `image` node loads a bitmap (its `imageSource`), so the use rule is one line (a plan with an image node uses `images`; the web links nothing for it). But the move is all or nothing: Swift in the core calls the `exact_raster_*` exports (`RasterLoader`), and the core holds image results itself (`NativeRasterLease`/`RasterImage` on every node; `RasterGeometry`'s drawing in the modal, flights and box layer; `AnimatedRasters` in the session, video, agent and box layers). Worth it for image-free apps and embeds only: ~54 KB of `exact_raster` and ~45 KB of Swift by symbol gaps, perhaps 50 KB real (speed). Split `RasterGeometry` and a lease protocol into the core, move the rest to an `ExactImages` module, and emit `raster_exports!` by use.
- **Apple: list swipe actions in every app** (2026-10-08): `SwipeActionsHost` (~34 KB by symbol gaps) is in hello, which has no list. Find its use rule (a list row's swipe actions) and move it out as `ExactDrag` was.
  2026-10-23 (lane q1022/ab): Parked: measured; no source change. `bun host/apple/build.mjs --ios hello-apple --sim "$EXACT_SIM"` passed. Hello's development executable retains 54,856 bytes (53.57 KiB) across 186 unique `SwipeActionsHost` text-symbol addresses, measured by gaps to the next text symbol. This is not a stripped binary size delta. A separate linked capability requires plan-u
- **Apple: the host's Rust drag and height sync run on every commit** (2026-10-08): `reconcile_height_handles`, `sync_height_owner`, `sync_height_transitions` and the transform drags are called from the commit path whether or not the plan has a drag; behind a const link (Drag) they would leave image-free, drag-free apps.
  2026-10-23 (lane q1022/aa): Parked. Commit `dbc0c3bc93`. Temporary runner-backed Rust probe: all four empty sync calls, 100,000 iterations, debug build: 0.361 us/commit, or 0.350 us with height targets forced dirty. Whole unseen-state commit: 30.598 us/commit, 1,000 iterations. 1 probe passed in 0.13 s; source restored afterward. The empty-path cost does not justify a const-link split 
- **Standard-library sorts copied per call site** (2026-10-08, the duplicate audit): `quicksort`/`driftsort` are instantiated ~38 times in hello (~80 KB by symbol gaps), one per `sort_by`/`sort_by_key` closure and element type. A few shared non-generic helpers (sort by a key of one type) would fold most of them.
- **Web writes lost when a tab closes mid-write, redesign** (2026-10-08; **Charlie, 2026-10-08: leave it documented for now, no redesign; the bench grader stays as is** — the pitfall covers it). A storage.fs write still in its IndexedDB transaction when the tab closes is lost (4/4 on an immediate close; bench t5 on the Android round). The first fix (da6848b8f..29efdaad6, a per-tab localStorage journal replayed at next launch) was reverted after a fresh blind review (Astra: revert; Grok: keep with fixes): a replay can write a closed tab's older value over a newer one another tab committed, a page restored from the back-forward cache was treated as gone for good, a non-permanent replay error dropped every dead tab's journal, and a failed localStorage flush could let a committed write age out of the applied set and replay. A redesign needs: a replay only if the target is still the version the write was admitted against (a durable generation per path), pageshow clearing the gone state, per-entry failure handling, and stale-snapshot removal on a failed flush; its own LLP 1097 amendment and review before landing.
- **Opening a select's menu raised the software keyboard on an EAS iOS 26.5 Simulator** (2026-10-08, seen while verifying app farm 008): a real touch (agent-device) on a `select` with options opened its menu and the system keyboard, the first time with its "slide to type" card, on main `57eeaa18` and with the fix alike. `KeyboardFocusIOSTests.testNativeSelectMenuPreservesShortcutFocusAndAllowsTextEntry` passed on that commit (with the fix, which leaves focus alone), so its zero-height `inputView` is not what the launched app showed. Not reproduced on a local iOS 27.0 Simulator with the software keyboard (2026-10-22): a real touch on a three-option `select` opened its menu and no keyboard. A phone is still to try.

- **The JS edit loop exceeds its 100 ms budget on main and the merged button program** (2026-10-07, `scripts/metrics.mjs`, Mac Studio): unchanged main ca2ae9252 measured 354 ms p50 / 470 ms p95; the merged program measured 316 ms p50 / 344 ms p95 and, in a second run, 411 ms p50 / 543 ms p95. All runs took place during verification load; measure on a quiet machine and separate watcher delay, rebuild and browser reload before attributing a regression.

- **The full native-buttons fixture shrinks buttons on the web** (2026-10-07, LLP 1104 merged-tip drive): `scripts/fixtures/native-buttons.contract` at 420×900 puts its native buttons in a fixed-height scrolling flex column; their `overflow: hidden` permits `flex-shrink: 1` to collapse direct children below the title. The same heights reproduce with the pre-merge 934cfa08b stylesheet; unchanged origin/main ca2ae9252 also collapses a scrolling column of 60 native filled buttons to 14px each. The smaller conformance fixture does not cover this scroll case. Hold the intended intrinsic floor without changing CSS sizing semantics, or give the fixture nonshrinking rows.

The merged program also alternates between that failure and a missing keyboard in the keyboard-bar fixture; a cold simulator restart did not cure it, and `com.apple.keyboard.preferences AutomaticMinimizationEnabled` is unset. Both fixtures use `appearance="none"`. Investigate responder activation across the driver's separate app launches; the cause is not established.

Yet `state` reports each canvas drawn (`draws: 1`, no error) and `agent.mjs … screenshot` shows every shape. A build of `28f29d821` (before LLP 1047.001's Surfaces move) is just as blank.
