- **Replay the garden bakeoff's exact2 lane through the look oracle** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). The in-tree garden's proof now looks with no tell on Linux, the web and macOS, but it has neither the brief's `meadow` model (the `#527b65` meadow) nor rain and snow, so RFC §4's acceptance case (the judged G4 and G9 frames failing on `particles-uncovered`, the meadow rendering green) needs the bakeoff's own lane (`game/bakeoff/garden/run.mjs`) run with the oracle. A `Material::hex("#527b65")` plane does read green in a look frame (`(124, 171, 155)` under the default sun and sky).
- **An HDR look frame with particles replaces the live frame's HDR attachments twice** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). Its count reads the scene's depth, so it needs that depth stored and sampleable; when the live frame's is transient (no depth prepass, soft particles, SSAO or final depth), `prepare_targets` makes retained ones for the look and the next live frame makes transient ones again: three textures each way at four samples, measured, with bloom's and SSAO's objects rebuilt. Neither keeps state between frames, so no picture changes; it is cost. A direct look frame already keeps attachments of its own (`look_direct`), released at the next live frame; the HDR path could do the same.
- **A look frame bypasses the static shadow cache** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). It draws every sun caster itself so that it leaves the cache as the live frames left it, so a defect only in the cache (a caster the cache dropped or kept stale) shows in live frames and escapes the oracle's `no-shadow-casters`. A look frame could read the cache's own maps as well, or count the live frame's texels.
- **`visible`'s reason names only the first non-mesh drawable that drew** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). `look.rs` `combine` answers `drawn` naming one drawable (`its sprite: …`) when a sprite and an emitter both drew; the reason could list every drawable's word.
- **No control has an emitter rescuing another drawable's rejection** (LLP 1127.000, 2026-10-18). `src/look_frame_tests.rs` covers a sprite that draws beside an empty emitter and beside a model out of view; an emitter whose particles draw beside a rejected mesh or sprite (`drawn`, naming the emitter) has no test.
- **Garden bakeoff follow-ups** (2026-10-06, historical run at `7716302242`; recheck on current main). exact2 placed third in the garden bakeoff (2026-10-06, [LLP 1046.010](llp/1046.010-garden-bakeoff.plan.md) §8): 81 fidelity at 3× Godot's cost. Engine frictions from the builder's diary: restore refuses components setup never inserted (only a fresh process catches it); the world hash includes the host clock; a Contract `every(50)` trips `TimerFireLimit` on an hour seek. Not yet attributed (engine or game?): a meadow rendered grey and rain particles alive but invisible. Discoverability: the builder never found `exactTime().epochAtZero`, so its game has no wall-clock away growth.

- **LLP 1108.001, slice 3 (`Blob`, object URLs, `blob:` sources): NOT READY after four design rounds** (2026-10-08); r5 folds round 4 unreviewed and needs a fifth review or a ruling that narrows it. Slice 2's download landed narrower as `exactBodyTo` (LLP 1108 D6 R2, ruled 2026-10-26): a 2xx body only, no `when` headers. Open: the Snapback client's download could switch to it once slice 1 lands; LLP 1108.001's `when` (write only a reply whose headers admit it) and a writer thread for a stalled disk are not built; the asynchronous executor (`EXACT_HTTP_ASYNC`) refuses it.
- **A 2 MB `ArrayBuffer` body still runs over a source's 100 ms step** (2026-10-08, the Bluesky clone): `fetch` base64-encodes a byte body in the prelude's JS (`toBase64`), in the same step as the call (a 2,000,000-byte `uploadBlob` took 106.8 ms on the iOS simulator). An upload from a file now goes by `exactBodyFrom` (LLP 1108 D6 R2), which moves no bytes through JS; a native encode would lift the byte body too.

- **A native module reading the app's current assets** (2026-10-07, Brooks port P2): `ExactModuleContext` has data, cache, temp, clock and change, but no resolver for the active asset generation, so a module's leaf pins embedded hashes and misses an asset-only update. A small API (bounded bytes or a file, with lifetime and cancellation); write it when a second app needs it or an app updates assets without a binary.
  2026-10-23 (lane q1022/aa): Parked. Commit `8fecb94104`. The existing context still has no active-generation asset resolver. The entry explicitly waits for a second consumer or asset-only update consumer; none is supplied by this sweep. Initial macOS and assigned iOS simulator builds passed with the current interface. No API design, product change, runtime reproduction or new test is c
- **Keyboard, the rest of #140** (2026-10-07, after `keyup`, `KeyboardEvent.code` and `.repeat` landed). Two parts wait on the owner's word: a capture-phase handler and its spelling (`keycapture=…`, ancestors outermost first, `stopPropagation()` keeping the key from the target), and a reserved held-modifier fact (`exactKeyboard`, reset on window blur, beside `keyup`; the web's way, keyup plus `exactPage().hasFocus`, is documented). A ⌘ chord during a composition now commits it and reaches the `key` handlers on macOS and iOS; not yet pressed with a real Korean or Japanese input source. Composing keys still differ by host: the web host hands every key Chrome sends while composing to `key` handlers (Contract has no `isComposing`, so a composer's Enter there can send mid-syllable), the native hosts keep them for the input method. Composed text is the same on every host since 2026-10-12 (LLP 1115.000 §5, ruling 6; `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/FieldComposition.swift`): `input` at each change with the marked text in the value, where AppKit had told it only once committed. What is left of that: (a) nobody has typed with a real Japanese, Chinese or Korean input method on macOS or iOS since (the tests drive `setMarkedText` and `insertText`); (b) an app's value never ends a composition, by Charlie's ruling of 2026-10-12 (LLP 1045 D5 stands): a different value written while text is marked is held and applied when the composition ends, since an action that rewrites what it hears would otherwise end one at its first keystroke and Contract gives it no way to know. macOS and iOS hold (`pendingValue`, `FieldComposition.swift`, each platform's `writeValue`), and so does the web's wasm target (`writeValue` in `host/web/glue.js`, `heldValues` until `compositionend`). Android and the web's JS target hold too since 2026-10-12: Android through the presenter (`Native.fieldComposing`, `presenter/hosted.rs`; not seen on a phone, which the entry on Android's text fields has), the JS target in `rt.js`'s `writeValue`, with the glue's rule and timing. Chrome does end a composition whose field's value is assigned: the marked text is replaced and the next keystroke begins another (`host/web/tests/compose-hold.test.mjs`, real compositions through CDP's `Input.imeSetComposition`, on both targets); (c) whether Chrome and UIKit fire an `input` at a commit that changes no text was not measured: macOS and iOS tell the app nothing then. Real keys (`orca computer`, events posted to the app's pid, macOS 26.6.2) gave Exact and Chrome 154 the same log: ⌘B is keydown Meta, keydown b, keyup b (with ⌘ still held), keyup Meta. A held key's auto-repeat is not yet pressed for real: the CLI cannot hold a key, and auto-repeat downs posted to a pid reached neither app; a keyboard by hand, or a HID-level post with the app frontmost, would show it. iOS reports no `repeat` (UIKit's presses carry none) and the terminal host sends no `keyup`.
- **Parley on Linux, what is left** (2026-10-07, [LLP 1085.000](llp/1085.000-parley-in-place-of-cosmic-text.plan.md) §7). An ltr line that starts in Hebrew keeps two visual-order inversions, as main did, under the first-strong base (LLP 1001 §1): it goes when the text-flow walker breaks an RTL run in an LTR paragraph and `shaping.rs` passes `BaseDirection::Ltr`. A Hebrew-in-ltr "…" pen sits 0.5 px off Chrome's, and emoji advances are 0.04–0.18 px narrower. The macOS and Windows fallback lists are cosmic-text's and unmeasured. Synthetic bold (fontique embolden) is not drawn. Tab stops count from the line start, not the block's start edge. Every vendored Parley patch (1–15) and fontique patch (1–3) is still to be sent upstream, and LLP 1085's questions to Nico Burns. Android: stage 3 ran on two Pixel 10 Pro XLs and a Pixel 10a (2026-10-07, §7); the Nothing Phone (4a) Pro, Expose and Windows are unmeasured. Crypto's `exact boot` slice is 1.6–2.0 ms longer with Parley on every phone (the cold start a user sees does not move); the slice has no sub-sections, so a simpleperf profile of both arms' boots is what would place it.
- **#84's journal lines, not ported** (2026-10-06; its Back and covered-edge fixes landed in 2cae1c92f and 74d782a36). - **Two checks red on main, seen in the PR sweep** (2026-10-06, at 856808161). - **`overflow-anchor` and priority candidates** (2026-10-07, #138 X23d). The `overflow-anchor: none` opt-out and the priority candidates (a focused editable) are not built on any native host.
- **Linux scroll anchoring: input inside the frame that carries an anchor correction** (2026-10-19, the fourth review of the Linux anchoring lane, q24/linux-scroll-anchor). With a display attached, a correction waits for its picture's acknowledgement, and wheel, reader, command or feed input in that one frame moves only the pending model (`presenter/collection/anchoring.rs` `anchor_input`, `presenter/scroll_end.rs` `scroll_end_input`). Three edges are left: (1) the input's `scroll` handler runs at once at the old acknowledged offset, then again at the new one. In `scroll_anchor_tests/review_tests.rs` `input_that_only_moves_the_pending_model`, the wheel's order is `s648;s610;e;`, where it should be `s610;e;` (seen by asserting the whole string). (2) An input that exactly cancels the pending correction (+48 against a −48 fold) clears the model and records no `scrollend`. (3) A reader sample that moves only the model takes the host clock for its completion, not the shell's stamp, and an earlier sample's armed completion stays due, so one fling can end twice. (1) was reproduced. (2) and (3) are the reviewers' traces, not run. None of them reaches a scroller with no anchor correction pending: there `anchor_input` returns `None` and input takes main's path. Also not done: an anchor candidate whose transformed quad crosses behind the viewer (a `perspective` parent with `rotate: y 75deg`) is skipped instead of clipped to the visible half, as `placement::clipped_bounds` does.
- **`text-decoration-color` and `text-underline-offset`** (2026-10-06, the concept of #75 by Jon Samp, whose branch main outran; its third row, `cursor`, landed in c6136f39d and f7e010921). Two CSS longhands as schema rows: `text-decoration-color` not inherited, `currentcolor` by default; `text-underline-offset` inherited, `auto` by default. The `text-decoration` shorthand takes a colour, which `shorthands.rs` refuses today because native painters draw only solid `currentcolor`. Draw them natively rather than admitting web-only rows: Apple's `underlineColor`/`strikethroughColor` (an offset needs a drawn underline, or a named refusal; `Text.swift` is near the cap), Linux's `paint/text_decoration.rs`, the terminal's SGR 58. The line takes the decorating box's colour, as CSS does.
  2026-10-23 (lane q1022/t, Apple side): Parked: shared schema/compiler work is outside this lane. Running the debug compiler on `/tmp/q1022-t-396-{color,offset}.contract` exits 1 with `lower-unknown-attr` for both longhands. Apple cannot receive either row. No source commit. Fleet: declare the two schema rows, grammar, shorthand and wire values first; web and Linux need their painters and propagation too. Apple then needs explicit decoration colour on each
- **Server-render CPU per page** (2026-10-06, the concept of #81, which main outran; the web-framework-bench's weakest result, 3–21× the best competitor). Re-port on main: skip pre-compressing `dist/.gen` at bind and the number-text fast path (both cherry-pick clean from #81); one site index per plan shared across requests (`runner.rs` is at the cap); a decoded constant pool for `vm.rs`, without #81's load-and-field fusion, which changes the machine `Vm.lean` proves; re-profile the per-template style memo after StyleProps' shrink (71792e51d). Measure on a quiet machine and run the theme-flip test #81 skipped. Also seen: the render server serves `dist/.gen` (intermediates and the plan's source map) to anyone; `static_file` refuses no hidden path, and `/.exact/` must keep working.
- **web-js: 10,000-row list updates** (2026-10-06, the concept of #82, LLP 1071.000, goal approved 2026-09-30). Every commit scans the page for `[navigationBack]`, about 40% of an update's script at 10k rows; the emitter can say a plan has no navigation roots. Then: a clone cache in `h()` keyed by the whole site, text bindings writing `Text.data`, one write per `css()`, a single-element move, and the data seam's per-row conversion (re-check #82's value-to-type memo against streamed answers, `ts-stream.js`, which mutate). `rt.js` (1,499 lines) and `emit.rs` (1,495) are at the cap. #82's grid numbers: update every 10th of 10k rows 537–1,039 ms against Svelte's 442.
- **Wake-driven activation has no end-to-end test** (2026-10-06, reviews of #193). Its tests fire the wake by hand: nothing checks that a loader finishing alone wakes Linux's display loop or Apple's session and presents the activated frame. And `native::when_loaded` treats a missing digest or a poisoned lock as loaded (the retry then asks `preload` again).
- **A camera module** (2026-10-02, Charlie lifted "No camera anything", `rules/DEFERRED.md` §Components). An app native module in LLP 1067.000's shape, as `apps/recorder` is: `<camera-preview>` and `start`/`stop`/`capture`/`flip` on one object per session, `capture` writing to `app:/camera/`, the `device.camera` grant, a deterministic agent substitute (and a preview `snapshot` drawn from the last frame, since a capture layer is absent from `drawHierarchy`). Web, iOS and macOS first. Needs its RFC with an implementer and a date; open question: whether a camera can be shared across apps while an app has one module.
- **iOS: a pressable glass button misses real touches over a transform drag's handle** (2026-10-06, the Bluesky clone's lightbox, b08 and b09). Close and menu buttons with `backgroundMaterial="glass"`, siblings painted over a full-page `transformDragFor` handle (`press=tap`, `dblclick`), took no HID tap on the iOS 27 simulator (`axe touch`): the tap toggled the handle's `press` instead. The agent's taps reached them, and with the material removed the same HID tap pressed the button. Interactive glass returns its effect's `contentView` from `hitTest` (`NodeViewIOS.swift`, "Interactive glass … answers a finger itself") and relies on the responder chain reaching the node; a recognizer on the effect or the sibling handle probably takes the touch first. Not reproduced on 2026-10-23 (iOS 27.0 simulator): a glass `button` over a full-page `transformDragFor` handle with its own `press` took the touch runner's real touch (it landed on the effect's content view, and the button's press ran, not the handle's). Still to try: `axe touch` as the entry used, and the Bluesky clone's lightbox itself.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/c2): not reproduced on an iPhone Air, iOS 27.0: a labelled glass button, padding taps and an empty glass pressable before a later `z-index=1` handle all take real XCTest touches; the fixture `glass-drag-touch.contract` is kept for a distinguishing reproduction.
- **A timer overdue at a gesture's event starts its transitions at the event's time** (2026-10-06, Grok's r2 review of `bsky/release-clock`). `advance_for_input` fires the timers due by a release or geometry at their own runner times, but the hold update (or geometry's `engine.advance`) has already moved the motion engine to the input's time, so a transition a timer starts begins there, not at its due time (`Engine::observe` starts at `self.now`). Only a timer the host's own wake missed is overdue at an input, so the shift is normally under a frame; committing the timers' receipts before the hold or geometry seeks the engine would remove it.
- **Linux content-region painting after hiding pending rows** (2026-10-24): `cargo test -p exact-linux --lib review_hidden_pending_content_paints -- --ignored` reproduces the CPU refusal `content text lacks final paint offer`. The ignored test in `host/linux/src/content_region/input_clock_tests.rs` boots `retained_actions::source()` with a registered content region, parks its body update at 100 ms, acknowledges the retained frame, dispatches `hide` at 150 ms, and requests another frame while the worker remains parked. Hidden text still needs a final paint offer.

- **Android start as seen: the grey wash was the platform's focus highlight; nth-C's Extra Heavy needs the phone unlocked** (2026-10-06, android/xstart; Pixel 10 Pro XL, `~/bench/android/results/video/xstart-1`, five warm launches each; reader patch `~/bench/android/results/xstart/reader-xstart.patch` against the bench reader's fce7c67). The daily app's window looked grey to the end of a recording, not for the cross-fade's last 25 ms: its view is focusable (keys), a launch by `am start` leaves the window out of touch mode, and Android draws its default focus highlight over the whole focused view (paper 223 for 254). With `defaultFocusHighlightEnabled = false` (the app draws its own focus): content visible 201-231 -> 187-195 ms (Views 183-190, Compose 180-195), settled 283-306 -> 287-301 (281-295, 288-304); xheavy unchanged, 216-224 / 306-310 (Views 210-222 / 302-314). exact2's content frame is still first (daily 78-96 ms against 107-113, xheavy 95-107 against 133-149), but what shows is the system's cross-fade from the launch, the same for every app: an earlier frame inside it shows no sooner by `am start`. Not built: the app's own task-open animation (a theme's window animations) would shorten what `am start` shows and nothing a launcher's own icon animation does. On the Nothing Phone (4a) Pro (nth-C) v8's Extra Heavy fling never ran on exact2 or Views: the phone had restarted and not been unlocked (`dumpsys user`: RUNNING_LOCKED), and MapLibre's `FileSource` reads SharedPreferences from credential-encrypted storage in an AsyncTask, which throws until the first unlock and takes the process down as the first map row is made (Compose makes its map later). Unlocking the phone once clears it; making the apps survive it means redirecting MapLibre's preferences and files to device-protected storage in each app (it resolves the application context itself).
- **Android: Extra Heavy's 24k blank, its causes, and the measurements still owed** (2026-10-06, android/xblank on android/explore 1ac9a73e3; Pixel 10 Pro XL; reader patch `~/bench/android/results/xblank/reader-xblank.patch` against the bench reader's aaf2f06, tools in `results/xblank/tools`). Measured on diagnostic builds over 9c8ccbead, one to five runs each; **the before/after tables on the final builds (xheavy, heavy, crypto; blank, fps, CPU) were not taken: the phones' host dropped off the tailnet (its node key expired)**; `results/xblank/tools/measure.sh` runs them (`*-exactxb0` before, `*-exactxb` after, APKs in `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-xblank/apk`). Found with a frame tag (`tag=1`: the reader draws each frame's number in the header and logs its feed's rows, `tagjoin.py` joins a recording's blank frames to them and to a trace): (1) `blank.py` took the launch animation for the driver's start in some runs, so their "24k" window measured 12k: every "bimodal" low reading (crypto's 0.3% baseline, xheavy's 0.5%) was that; fixed in `~/bench/android` ea94a6e. (2) `cover=1` read "covered" because a row's recorded bounds were its boxes unclipped: the list's spacer for unmounted rows is a row over a million px tall that draws nothing, and a horizontal list's content box 239,160 px wide; fixed here (each box within its clip, 1x1 for a row that recorded nothing). (3) About half of xheavy's blank at 24k (8-11% before, Views 1.3%) was the frontier: one exact-thread turn of refine, paint and pictures ran 13-35 ms (32-38 turns over 12 ms in each 2 s), no stream left meanwhile, and the view ran past its last drawn row. A fast feed's pass now builds what its measured cost per row fits in 8 ms, the rest after the queued steps (the reader re-posts a pending pass): 4.0-5.6%. (4) The other half was WebView cards: the reader made a WebView for each and destroyed it with its row, white until its page painted, where RecyclerView hands Views a recycled one still showing its last page. The reader now keeps three released WebViews (paused) for the next rows: 0.8-1.6% at 24k, 0.1% at 12k, recorded fps 110 -> 117-118 (two runs; 6.5% in the same build with `webpool=0`). Measured and not kept: a pool of threads making a stream's pictures while the stream waits for them (4.8 ms of a long turn is `HardwareBuffer.create` and the copy): no change. Left: the long turns' paint (8.7 ms) and pictures (4.8 ms) still stall the stream (pictures made where they are decoded, or drawn into their slots when they land, would take the second out); crypto and heavy with the 8 ms pass are unmeasured (crypto's rows cost under 1 ms, so its passes should stay whole). Cold start on this phone (warm shader cache, five launches each, `results/video/xbstart-base`): content visible 216-227 ms against Views 311-322 and Compose 313-318, settled 306-313 against 453-472 and 472-476; both follow the system's launch transition, which starts at the app's first frame (73-84 ms, still blank; content is in the next, 88-100 ms, before the fade shows anything), so nothing in exact2's boot (19-22 ms of CPU) moves them here. On the Nothing Phone (4a) Pro (`results/video/vwc`) the starting window stays until content, exact2's content frame (207-232 ms) is level with Views' (227-230), and that is where a shorter boot would show.
- **Android: crypto's 24k blank fixed; what the extra paints cost and what is left** (2026-10-05, android/cblank on android/explore 829cac9d1; Pixel 10 Pro XL, `~/bench/android/results/cblank-*`). Cause (Perfetto, the reader's `cover=1`): a refine (every third scroll step, ~7 ms) mounts the rows a viewport past the view; they are out of view, so the pass is quiet and frames move the last paint until the next one, which came 0-4 frames later (median 17.6 ms after the refine); at 200 dp a frame the nearest of those rows is under two frames of travel away, so the list ended at the last drawn row for 2-4 frames of every five. The exact thread was not short of time (440 ms/s, the same before and after), and the main thread took the paint within 0.6 ms (median). Fix (`host/linux/src/canvas/travel.rs`): the host measures the feed's travel per step and since the last pass, and after a quiet pass paints by the frame its rows can first show (at once at 24k; ≤6k never sooner than before). Three runs each, interleaved, 12k/24k blank: before 0.0/19.8-24.2%, after 0.0/0.0%, Views 0.0/0.0%. Traced fling (two runs each): fps and janky frames unchanged at every speed; CPU and main ms/s unchanged to 12k; at 24k CPU 1,370 -> 1,455 ms/s and main 230 -> 253: the RenderThread now draws the fifth of the view that was blank (its drawing as at 12k), and paints go 25 -> ~47/s, each ingest costing the main thread ~0.9 ms whatever it carries (Views at 24k: 862 CPU, 341 main). xheavy (paints every frame, so unaffected): 24k blank 0.5-11.3% before, 8.3-10.7% after, five runs each; fps and CPU unchanged. Left: (1) the reader's per-stream ingest cost (or rows published without a paint: exact sends a new row's recording and place, the reader adds it to the feed's group) would take back the 24k main time. (2) `cover=1`'s counters read 0 while a fifth of the view was blank: not row reuse but the spacer row's bounds; fixed on android/xblank (the entry above). (3) A shifted window (`lead()` moved toward travel instead of grown) and a pass every step (`pre=1`, reader patch `~/bench/android/results/cblank-reader-pre.patch`) each left 2.8-3.2% at 24k; not kept. (4) The once-measured 0.3% crypto baseline was `blank.py`'s onset taking the launch animation for the driver's start (the entry above).
- **Android: blank areas while flinging fast — measured, the reader's share fixed, the rest owed** (2026-10-05, android/blank on 268878a98; Pixel 10 Pro XL; the reader is `~/bench/lists/exact/android/canvas`, outside the repo, patch `~/bench/android/results/blank/reader-blank.patch`). The measure: `~/bench/android/bin/bench.py blank <app>` records a fling with screenrecord and `bin/blank.py` takes, per driver segment, the share of the list's viewport in full-width one-colour bands ≥56 dp (no row there), time-weighted, the same arithmetic for every stack. Before (two runs each, 12k/24k dp/s): xheavy 2.5–3.4% / 23.1–23.4% (16–18% of 24k frames more than half blank), heavy 0.0% / 2.7–3.8%, crypto 0.3–0.5% / 24.0–24.2%; Views xheavy 1.6% / 1.0%, Compose 0.2% / 0.1%, Views heavy and crypto 0.0% (no frame more than half blank in either). The mechanism on xheavy: rows come into view as they are first recorded (median 2 ms after the stream that defines them), and the reader recorded every row not in view at paint time on its worker, so its group drew nothing there for a frame or more. With such rows recorded on main when within 33 ms of travel of the view, and a worker-made row's group recorded again before the next frame: xheavy 0.6–0.8% / 7.0–9.3% (2–5% of 24k frames more than half blank), heavy 0.0% / 0.0–0.1%; fps, late frames, main-thread ms/s and PSS unchanged, CPU within run spread. Owed: (1) crypto at 24k: landed, android/cblank (the entry above). (2) xheavy's rest at 24k: WebView cards not drawn yet (an empty bordered box, as Views shows) and a few whole-view blank frames. (3) Measured and not kept: a velocity lead (`refine(-1, v)`; the reader passed 0) made every report dearer and blanked more (xheavy 24k 18–19% vs 12–14% without, CPU +4–12%; a lead following the measured speed mounted and retired a row every report at 1k); keeping the velocity for passes between refines (`refine_slice` resets it to 0) tripled first recordings; moving the scrolled list to the front of the report queue (each pass takes two lists and every report queues every mounted list, inner ones too) measured no change; clamping the compositor's scroll to the drawn rows (as a browser clamps async scroll) left the blank (6.5–7.7%) and moved 98–99% of the distance. (4) The feed's group keeps rows at the list's start and end mounted (the reader saw rows millions of px away in every paint): memory and walk cost, not blank.

- **`rem`/`em` the JS target still bakes, or the browser resolves elsewhere** (2026-10-06, after #136): SVG geometry written as attributes (`svg_props`: `x`, `y`, `width`, `height`, `cx`, `cy`, `r` on viewports, uses, masks, patterns, filters and gradients) still carries the kernel's 16 px resolution; and a node whose element keeps the UA's font (`button[data-button-style]`'s `font: revert`, `select`, both ~13.33 px in Chrome) resolves an `em` width or padding against that size in the browser where the kernel uses the inherited one, so the JS target and the wasm page disagree there.

- **LLP 1075.003.000.001, access hatches on every platform** (2026-10-06, accepted as a design; landing on main as it is built; its §13 is the as-built record). Done: the rename from hooks; of stage 1, on the web's JS target and on Apple, every call timed, `diagnostics` with its bounds, `state.hatches`' new shape, `perf hatches`, Save Trace's section and the web's throw rule. Also done: the per-platform `hatches` declaration with its typed keys, the plan's requirement, the cohort's capability, `classifyArtifacts`' branch, hosts that call only the words their module handles (`platform`, `unhandled`), late frames that name their hatches, the crash breadcrumb and `EXACT_HATCHES=off` on Apple. The plan's own per-platform row is built for Apple (a `hatches` table; a word not given to the platform reaches Swift unhatched). A node's calls are timed by its plan site on every host. Owed of stage 1: the wall-clock acceptance driven in a person's session (a late frame from a 30 ms hatch, read from Save Trace), and §9's cost series on a phone. Of stage 2, done on the web and Apple: the bounded act queue, `input(text)`, settle draining and naming a hatch that never stops, the app and window scopes with the embedder's two grants, the frame clock and `after` with the seek stopping at each hatch instant, and `?hatches=off` on the web. Root `data-*` words reach `app` as `app.data` on every host. Owed of stage 2: `input`'s remaining cases (a search controller keeping first responder, composition). Then: stage 3's leftovers (a part tap driven as a real touch on the simulator, and whether `owns` should take a `UIInteraction`, whose recognizers a hatch cannot declare today); Stage 4 is built on Linux (the trait, the overlay, observed input, the scopes, the frame clock, diagnostics, the breadcrumb); it builds, lints and passes the fixture smoke on a real Linux box too (x86_64, 2026-10-07); its Windows adapter is written and has never been compiled for Windows or run (a `cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` from Linux stops at `ring`'s C build, so it needs a Windows machine or a Windows C toolchain, and a Windows crate for the fixture), the Linux `build.rs` of each Rust-data app includes its hatches (`rust_hatch_entry`), the six that bake through `exact_js_bake` (canvas-gallery, completion-storm, duo-lab, expose, messages, weatherlight) do not yet, and an overlay draws no images or text yet. Stage 5, the `hatch` verb, the template's section and the guides, is done. Stage 6: Android runs stage 4's hatches (its in-repo host is the Linux presenter built for Android; 38 of 38 on a Pixel 10 Pro XL and a Pixel 10a); a Kotlin module and a `view` for a node that is a View wait for the Canvas reader to be in this repository, with their own RFC, and `smoke.mjs` has no `android` host. The design's r4 had no review before the ruling, so review should look first at the mid-seek act drain, each host's `input` primitive and the plan's per-platform words.

Split the adoption across steps, or run it as the module's long work (`NativeModule::later`).
- **A retryable `E_CONFLICT` blocks the Snapback4 client's outbox** (2026-10-08, app farm round 3, 34 GPT builds): a conditional write that lost its race comes back `failed`/`E_CONFLICT`/`retryable: true`; `round.rs` keeps it queued and stops the flush (`Stop::Retry`), so it and every write behind it retry forever. Follow Snapback's answer in `issues/20261008-snapback4-retryable-conflict-held-pending.md` (snapback repo); meanwhile bounding resends per write would unblock the outbox.

- **A stream that can send: Supabase Realtime and Convex without polling** (2026-10-05, Depot feedback: an equipment-lending app built on Snapback4, Convex and Supabase polled on all three). TypeScript sources send on a resource's socket from a mutation, its mapper or an answer on the web JS target, macOS, the iOS simulator and the Linux host run on a Mac (LLP 1016.000 D7; `issues/closed/20261009-runner-owned-bidirectional-streams.md`). Owed: Phoenix's join and Convex's sync protocol driven against Supabase and Convex as the proof; the web wasm host, whose module realm hands the mapper a handle that never opens (`open`, ordered and binary frames, `Closed`, the mutation flag and the op-14 door in `module-glue.js`); Rust sources, which have no handle; natively, a caller's abort ending the stream and mapping `Aborted` as the web JS target does (it only makes the handle stale: the prelude has no way to ask the runner to end a ticket, so it needs a door op, the runner and both native executors); a Chrome-oracle conformance case (text and binary both ways, order, close code and reason, `bufferedAmount` back to 0) and an async-lane echo test on the platform transport; and the X21 drive on a Linux machine, where the portable transport sends, and on an iPhone.
- **npm in `app.ts`, and backend SDKs** (2026-10-05, Depot feedback: three handwritten wire adapters, Supabase's session persistence and token refresh by hand). Two problems. Bundling: the bake refuses outside imports "until dependency capture is implemented" (`js/bake/src/lib.rs`, `sources`), and LLP 1091 D10's package linking for Contract is most of that capture. The model: an SDK lives between answers (refresh timers, a socket it writes, `localStorage`), and a data module runs only inside one, refusing a `fetch` or storage call outside it. Pure packages work once bundling does; an SDK needs a client lifetime beyond one answer or a first-party adapter. An RFC with supabase-js and the Convex client as the cases; offline (snapshot cache, purge on sign-out, the persisted half of the keep-last-value line below) follows from it.
- **An update store reopened while another thread spawns is refused** (2026-10-05, review of the exact-update test fix): a spawned child holds a copy of `owner.lock`'s descriptor until its exec closes it, so `Store::open` (d3c84c2ba) on one thread while another is inside `Command::spawn` finds an "exclusive owner". The hosts open the store once at launch and hold it, so nothing in the tree does this; an embedder that reopens would need to serialize the two, as the test harness does (`open_store`, `finished`).
- **Transitions and animations of SVG `points` and `d`** (2026-10-05, the Signal clone): Chrome interpolates a polygon's `points` and a path's `d` (`path()`) between shapes with matching commands, which makes Lottie-style morphs declarative; Contract has no such transition or keyframe value, so a morph is a canvas or a stack of crossfades.

- **Journal a route whose overflow nothing scrolls** (2026-10-05, Exact-new iOS feedback; `lower-route-scroll` covers the static half). A runner log was built and pulled after GPT-6-Astra and Grok 4.7 reviews: it read every layout offer as the route's box, so a sheet detent or `resizes-content` keyboard flagged a covered screen and latched its key; covered retained routes, absolute and transformed descendants, and host covers (padding, which Taffy's content bottom omits) gave wrong answers; and it ran on the boot layout. A real one journals only the active top route, at the settled window offer, after first pixel, counting in-flow content against the padding edge, and clears its key when the route fits again.

Lean realizes `Contract.stdlib`'s equation lemmas where a proof first unfolds it, under the command line's options (a `set_option` does not reach them), and its match over the roster's string names took about 200,000 heartbeats before `concat` and needs more after, so `semantics/lakefile.toml` raises `maxHeartbeats` for the library. Each roster entry grows it (origin/main's formats and `t` add more). Matching the router's thirteen entries in a function of their own, reached from one arm, would shrink the match; `stdlib_ty`'s and `stdlib_good`'s case analysis would follow.
- **`perf frames live` on Apple** (2026-10-04, fix/game, the platformer's diary R11). The web's wasm target lends the agent's clock to the wall for a window (LLP 1079 D4); macOS and iOS answer `virtual`. `FrameSampler.swift` already builds the reply; a live branch in `Agent.swift`'s `perf` would release `session.clock` for the window, turn the GPU module's seek off (`GpuModule` takes `agentFreezes`) and let the display link fire runner timers (`Session.swift` gates them on `!agentMode`), then take the clock back at the wall.

- **`z-index` across a non-stacking parent, as CSS** (2026-10-04, fix/geometry, chess #5, kanban, kanban2, LLP 1087 t4-kanban). LLP 1083.000 §3.1 holds every host to "`z-index` stays inside its holder" because Apple nests one view per node and `zPosition` orders only one layer's sublayers. The web would follow CSS by dropping isolation rule (z) (rules a and b keep native tree order for level 0); the Linux painter could defer an escaping box to its stacking context's phase with its ancestors' clip and transform (hit testing follows its box order); Apple would have to re-parent an escaping view to the context's view with the intermediate clips and transforms rebuilt, as flights and the Arrange lift do for one view. Deciding between one order everywhere and CSS where a host can is Charlie's; the pitfall and LLP 1001 say what holds now.
- **Native `frame()` reads transforms as committed** (2026-10-04, fix/geometry, LLP 1051.000 D1). A transform in flight, a 3D one's projection and a `-exact-layout-transition`'s offset are the web's alone (its client rect); natively the runner composes the rows. Hosts noting presented transforms as they note scroll offsets (`Runner::scrolled`) would close it, for the first consumer that reads geometry mid-motion.

If no path reads a slot another frame assigned earlier on that path, loading each slot as the last assignment on the path (or its starting value) equals the snapshot load; derives, resources, `pending` and sends lie outside the statement. That would prove `analyze-call-stale-read` sound against `exec`. No implementer yet.

- **Content regions take no `text-indent`, `hyphens: auto` or a soft hyphen's fit** (2026-10-04, fix/typo). `host/apple/src/content_region/wire.rs` sends neither row, and `RegionWorkerLayout` breaks without the indent and without the hyphen-fit rule `TextEngine.layout` has; justification and soft-hyphen ink are there. Thread both through the region wire when a region consumer wants book text.
- **The document window, what the studio pass left** (2026-10-04, fix/macdocs; LLP 1069.010 "As built: the document window"). A ⌘Q that a window's `beforeunload` keeps open is cancelled, not resumed once the app's question is answered (NSDocument's review continues the quit); `wheel` and `drop` have no iPadOS form (a trackpad's scroll, a drag from Files) and `title` no `UIToolTipInteraction`; a command's menu is by chord only, so Zoom to Fit ⌘9 stays in File beside View's zooms; the bake does not warn when a declared extension already belongs to another app's type on the Mac (Freeform's `.board`); repeating linear gradients and px stops are admitted by Charlie’s 2026-10-12 ruling 9 (LLP 1066 amendment); the open repeating-hatch issue tracks remaining host verification and deferred `background-attachment: local`.
- **A resource whose arguments change reads its shape's zero until the new reply** (2026-10-04, fix/tooling3, workout F6). Lift's diary reports its board resource's `ready: false` unmounting the whole screen on every search or filter change, a flash on real time and a zero board under a test's still clock; the web's data stacks (SWR, React Query's `keepPreviousData`) keep the last value while a refetch is in flight. Native `clock settle` already waits for every runner request (a parked storage turn included; the macOS drive could not reproduce F6's miss on this branch), so what is left is the semantics: keep the last answer, flagged pending, until the new one lands, on every host.
- **Hear LLP 1096 on a phone** (2026-10-04, impl/1096). The sound arm's interruption handling (a call, Siri: the engine stops, voices due meanwhile are journaled `sound skipped: interrupted`, the engine restarts) and `audio_session: "playback"` against the ring/silent switch were built and run on the iOS simulator only; drums on an iPhone, and tvOS's tier-2 build, are still to do.
- **`z-index="auto"` is refused** (2026-10-04, files F14, the lists lane). `z_index` is an `i32` row defaulting to 0, so a conditional `z-index` cannot fall back to CSS's `auto`, and `auto` and `0` differ in CSS (only `0` makes a stacking context). A lifted, dragged row writes `z-index=(lifted ? 2 : 0)` for now.
- **Should `clock settle` fire a pending one-shot timer?** (2026-10-04, authoring bench, LLP 1087 t7-wizard.) A `task boot mount` with `after(1, restore)` does not fire at `clock settle` alone (it waits for requests and advances to the end of motion, LLP 1012 §2, firing a timer only if it reaches it on the way); a builder needed `clock +50`. The docs said a timer lands at `clock settle` and now say what it does. Including the earliest pending `after` (not `every`, which has no fixed point) in settle's target would match what builders expect; Charlie's call.
- **Setup and the wasm build on a fresh Linux box** (2026-10-04, the authoring bench): With a private `CARGO_HOME` the `-Zbuild-std` path failed offline on std's own crates (`rustc-demangle`) until `cargo fetch --manifest-path <sysroot>/…/library/Cargo.toml` was run by hand, though `app.mjs` means to do that itself. `host/web/build.mjs`'s non-JS path (a game, `--wasm`, `--bake`; `exact deploy` creates the parent itself) still publishes into an `EXACT_WEB_DIST` whose parent is missing with ENOENT: `replaceBuild` (`host/web/serve.mjs`) writes `<dist>.lock` before anything creates the parent, which only the JS target's path does (`mkdirSync(dirname(dist))`, 6ddd0c07f7; checked 2026-10-22 by calling `replaceBuild` into a missing directory).

The iOS sheet cannot checkmark the remembered row, because `UIAlertAction` has no public checked state. `position-area` is unread by `UIMenu` and Linux.

The agent's screenshot uses `Capture.picture` (`AgentMac.swift`, 4863d26d7: subviews re-sorted by `zPosition` around `cacheDisplay`, presented animation values, box fills hidden); canvas uploads use LLP 1083.000's `paintOrderBitmap` (`Mac/CaptureMac.swift`: an explicit compositor, filter pictures, model values with animated key paths overlaid). Both order by the paint rank and both pass the paint-order driver; keep one, with both sets of tests (`PaintCaptureMacTests`, the shop/spreadsheet captures).
- **CSS font generic mappings for cursive/fantasy/math/emoji/fangsong and CSS-wide font-family values** (2026-10-04, CSS lane): currently refused precisely so a generic is never silently treated as a local family.
- **CSS intrinsic flex-basis keywords (content/min-content/max-content/fit-content)** (2026-10-04, CSS lane): dimension rows need an intrinsic sizing representation; compiler now diagnoses the declared mode limit precisely.
- **`width`/`height` intrinsic keywords (`fit-content`, `min-content`, `max-content`)** (2026-10-31, Pokédex agent benchmark round 5: two of five agents wrote `width="fit-content"` on a badge or a table and were refused by `lower-attr-value`). Vendored Taffy 0.14 already holds the keywords on `Dimension` (`ExpandedDimension::FitContent`, `MinContent`, `MaxContent`) and has a shrink-to-fit helper (`compute/common::fit_content_width`); the kernel's dimension rows would need the representation, and block and flex sizing would need to honour it, with Chrome rows in `browser_position.tsv`.
- **CSS native interaction/painter gaps (diaries kanban F11, minesweeper F4, ledger F1, pomodoro F2)** (2026-10-04, CSS lane): user-select contain on every host (text since 2026-10-12 and all since 2026-10-20 are honoured on the web, macOS and iOS; Linux, Windows and Android take both as auto); dotted/dashed and other border styles; decoration color/style/thickness and Linux underline skip-ink:auto. Current compiler/driver diagnoses each declared limit.
- **Linux authored glyph fallback cascades** (kanban F8, 2026-10-04): font lists select the first available family; cosmic-text still owns glyph fallback through its single Attrs.family. Preserve the rest of the authored list per shaping run before claiming full CSS per-glyph fallback parity.
- **Layout property transitions** (kanban F15, shop F5, 2026-10-04): CSS `transition="width 200ms ease"` is valid CSS, but the native engine cannot interpolate arbitrary layout rows or lay out per frame. The diagnostic names supported paint/transform/SVG properties and the admitted height path. Add width and other layout transitions only with an approved executor/layout design; `-exact-layout-transition` projects changes to the laid-out box today.
- **Viewport units on scalar CSS lengths** (kanban F9, 2026-10-04): dimension rows retain viewport units across wire and resolve on native resize; font-size, gap, spacing and border widths still use scalar rows. Extend their retained relative lengths and web emission before admitting viewport units there.

- **Clock timelines, what the reviews left** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1055.002](llp/1055.002-synced-animations.rfc.md) §5 "Known gaps"). Under the web agent clock an author's pause and resume is recorded once (`held`, as on main for every animation), so a resumed synced animation does not rejoin there, and one JS-agent seek that crosses a timer registers its joiner before older finite members are sought. `contract symbols` has no definition or reference for a `timeline`. Not yet driven after the review fixes: Lexy (its repository) on iOS and the web, macOS and Linux apps, Firefox, Core Animation across backgrounding.
  2026-10-23 (lane q1022/t, Apple side): Parked in part; no source change. Built and drove `/tmp/q1022-t-motion.plan` on iOS, macOS and the web. A second play mounted at 300 ms shares the first play's phase. Apple holds the first at 0.84 when paused at 800 ms; at 1300 ms the second is 0.76. After resume and a seek to 1800 ms, both Apple plays are 0.36 (screenshot channels `[255,163,163]` and `[163,163,255]`). Screenshots inspected; all operations succeeded.
- **`release` is every developer tool's profile too** (2026-10-04, the build-speed pass; LLP 1036.000 §5–7). The Linux host an agent drives and an app's render entry moved to `host-dev`, where a touched kernel line rebuilds in 3.6 s; at `release` (one codegen unit, thin LTO) it was 102 s on a Ryzen 3600, 58 of them the kernel on one core. So did the Contract compiler the smokes run for their fixtures. The web's tools are still `cargo run --release`: the dev loop's `exact-js-bake` (`host/web/dev.mjs`; its bake driver, an app's `-dev` bin or `exact-dev`, moved to `host-dev` on 2026-10-29), `parity.mjs`, `logic/bake` (`scripts/rust.mjs`), the `metrics` bins, and conformance's wasm side. Each rebuilds through that link after any change under it. Moving them to `host-dev` is the same one-line change each, left for the web's pass.
- **What `release` could shed: measured, not built** (2026-10-04, Hetzner Ryzen 3600, `caltrain-linux` / `caltrain-render`, stripped). Thin LTO, one codegen unit, `opt-level = 3` (today): 17.91 / 8.00 MB, 134 s from nothing. Fat LTO: 16.44 / 7.31 MB (−8%), 207 s. `opt-level = "s"`: 14.80 / 6.40 MB (−17%), 94 s. Both: 12.85 / 5.65 MB (−28%), 155 s. `opt-level = 2`: 17.15 MB (−4%). Sixteen codegen units: 19.42 MB (+8.5%), 97 s. Fat LTO costs only build time (73 s more there; run again later that day 143 → 210 s and 18.04 → 16.57 MB, boot 50.7 and 50.9 ms and layout 21.9 ms both ways over twelve alternating launches), and a Linux bake could say it as the Apple one now does, on Cargo's command line: a bake builds with `--target`, so it shares no artifact with the tools above. Sixteen codegen units under fat LTO, which on Apple build faster than today's profile, do not here: 193 s for 17.36 MB (−3.8%). `"s"` trades the hosts' speed, unmeasured there. Both are Charlie's to rule with the other platforms. An Apple production bake does not move the same way (M4, Caltrain's `ExactMac`, as built / with `strip -x`): thin LTO 11.98 / 9.06 MB; fat LTO 11.82 / 8.62 MB (−1% / −5%); `opt-level = "s"` 13.60 / 10.78 MB, larger by 14%, and its runner and layout at boot 7% slower (16.1 ms against 15.1). Fat LTO is built there (the bake says it for an Apple target, which no tool builds for; LLP 1036.000 §8); `"s"` is a loss. The Swift side has the same trade and it is also left: `-Osize` makes Sparkline's stripped binary 7.8% smaller (8.73 → 8.04 MB, 12.6% with fat LTO) and its first apply of 295 views 7% slower (17.9 ms against 16.7, sixteen alternating launches).
- **A cold bake of an app with a GPU crate is two Cargo builds end to end: measured, not built** (2026-10-04, Cargo's `--timings`, Caltrain on the ten-core M4, before the registry crates were kept; LLP 1036.000 §7). The GPU crate first, 39 s for 146 unit-seconds, because it is one chain (naga 14 s → wgpu-core 13 s → wgpu 7 s); then the app's archive, 49 s for 366, whose chain is the kernel (33 s) and the app (3.5 s). They are in sequence because the app's build script binds the GPU product (LLP 1009 D6), and nothing else can warm the app's dependencies meanwhile: asked for by another selection they are other units (`-p exact-apple` recompiles 25 crates in a warm target, the kernel among them; with the GPU crate, 32). Overlapping them needs the GPU crate in its own target directory and the app's dependencies compiled before its build script may run, which is a change to what `buildBake` promises on every platform. It is worth less than the chains suggest on ten cores, where ExactKit's compile already fills the GPU phase's idle ones (the three together are 660 CPU-seconds, 66 s at best), and more on eighteen. In a checkout that starts from the kept registry crates (§11) the two builds are 12.9 s (wgpu-hal 3 s → wgpu-core 9 s → wgpu 5 s, then ours) and 37 s (the kernel, 30 s), so overlapping them is worth at most those 13 s of a 58 s first build.
- **What a second checkout still compiles** (2026-10-04; LLP 1036.000 §7, §10, §11). The host's two Rust modules and the registry crates under an app are now kept for the machine (`~/.cache/exact/apple-modules`, `~/.cache/exact/apple-crates`): a second checkout's first build is 78 → 58 s on an M4 and 38 crates, all of them path crates or standing on one. What is left, in the order it costs: the kernel (33 s in one rustc front end, the chain every build waits on); `wgpu-core` and `wgpu`, registry crates rebuilt in every checkout because the vendored `wgpu-hal` under them is a path crate (20 s of the GPU crate's build; a patched crate consumed as a registry or git source would be kept like the rest); ExactKit (150 CPU-seconds, beside the bake, and not what the Rust waits for: at a lower priority the build is the same, 57.7–58.9 s against 57.3–58.6). The kernel alone is 12.6 s on an idle eighteen-core M5, 10 s of it rustc and 7 of those LLVM over its sixteen codegen units, so it is the cores and not the front end. sccache was tried on the Linux host and is not it (95 → 79 s): what it cannot share is the same path crates.
- **A binary that takes updates ships every loaded module** (2026-10-04; LLP 1047 D8, "after the web target"; LLP 1036.000 §8). A production Apple build at store level 0 now leaves out each of the four its plan cannot reach (the bake's `loads`: the Canvas 2D GPU module, the SVG island module, the video and web arms; Sparkline's Mac bundle 16.3 → 11.6 MB), because that plan is the only one the binary will ever run. A binary at any other level carries all four (4.7 MB on a Mac) for a later bundle that might reach one, and dropping them there needs the linked set as a compatibility input, so a bundle that reaches a module its binary lacks is refused before it is applied (D6, D8).
- **An abort a listener suppresses leaves a browser-realm fetch pending until its reply** (2026-10-04, both families' round-5 reviews of the app-diary fixes, 4a8). Hermes watches a fetch's signal through Ibex's abort hooks; the wasm target's browser module realm loads prelude.js alone, so `watchAbort` is an `addEventListener` an earlier `stopImmediatePropagation` skips, and the fetch rejects only when its reply lands (fulfill rechecks `signal.aborted`). The default web build (the JS target) uses the browser's own fetch and is unaffected.
- **A Rust source in a logic module is asked without its `Target`** (files F22, 2026-10-04, read in `logic/abi`, not reproduced). The ABI's ops 3/4 call `answer`/`parse`, whose defaults drop to `query` (the bake placeholder); in process (`Mixed`, `Storage`) the runner calls `answer_for`/`parse_for`. A source that parks a call by `Target` therefore answers its placeholder, silently, on the JS web target only. Carry the target across the ABI (both hosts' encoders, an ABI version), or refuse at load a module whose source overrides `answer_for` and not `answer`. No longer needed for `doc:` paths, which TypeScript reaches now (F2).
- **`contract types`' `Sources` demands the Rust half's sources from `app.ts`** (files F21, 2026-10-04). In a mixed app the generated `Sources` names every source, so the TypeScript half writes `Omit<Sources, …>` by hand. The composition that knows which sources are Rust's (`RUST_SOURCES`, `build_mixed_with`) is Rust code; `app.json` could name them, or the bake could emit a `TypeScriptSources` type from the composer it already runs.
- **Pointer events, what is left** (2026-10-04, [LLP 1056](llp/1056-canvas-2d.rfc.md) §8.6; the paint diary's F1). No `coalesced` list: a stroke is a polyline at the display's rate, and DOM's `getCoalescedEvents()` as a field needs a second record type (a record cannot list itself in the plan's types). An iPad's hovering pointer and pencil send no free `pointermove` (UIKit's hover recognizer). Linux delivers every move, as its `pan` does, not one a frame. The Mac agent's held drags read `pressedMouseButtons` beside the event's own button; a synthesized pen is never a tablet event, so pen pressure on macOS is untested by script. `exact new`'s template now passes the module's canvas roster; apps made before it (x2apps) have to add `.with_canvas_surfaces(CANVAS_SURFACES)` to their `apple/` and `web/` crates by hand.
- **CSS `outline`** (2026-10-04, x2apps spreadsheet F3): `outline-width/-color/-offset` are refused, and `outline`/`outline-style` take only `none`, on a text field or textarea, where it takes the field's ring off (LLP 1104 D6, #459). On the web they would be CSS (and `outline: none` takes the browser's focus ring off a focusable grid); natively nothing draws a focus ring on a box, so they need paint rows outside the border box, following `border-radius`, above the content, on Apple and Linux. Admit with the four rows and their shorthand, painted on every host, and a `:focus-visible` answer (none exists for any row yet).
- **Keys, what native hosts still lack** (2026-10-04, x2apps calc F3 / minesweeper F7; docs/contract-grammar.md#keys). `key` bubbles and `preventDefault()` claims a key on every host now, but on macOS and iOS a scroller does not scroll by arrow, Space or page keys (the web's default), and iOS carries no `aria-keyshortcuts` (`UIKeyCommand`s from the focused session's buttons, as `ShortcutsMac.swift` routes them). The iOS hardware-keyboard path (`TextField.pressesBegan`) is driven only through the agent's synthesized keys; no real keyboard has pressed it.
- **The JS target's data seam caps an answer at 16 MiB** (2026-10-04, LLP 1090 conformance). `rust-data.js` goes through the logic ABI, whose `MAX_MESSAGE` is `MAX_HOST_WORK_BYTES`; the runner's in-process data source has no cap, so an answer between 16 MiB and the runner's value bounds loads on wasm, native and Linux and fails on the JS target (docs/agent-pitfalls.md). Either bound answers alike on every host or let the JS seam carry what the runner does.
- **The JS jump lands what a timer sent at its last instant** (2026-10-04, LLP 1092 "As built"). `host/web-js/agent.js`'s `clock +N` waits for every reply after a stop and runs its `then`; the wasm glue, the Linux agent and Apple stop waiting once no timer is due by the jump's end, so a request a timer sends at exactly `to` lands there on real time and its `then` waits for the next operation. Conformance avoids it (`queue.contract`'s timer sends nothing); pick one behaviour and make the other hosts follow.
- **One-slot invariants of a queue mutation's slot** (2026-10-05, LLP 1092 D12, semantics/README.md "Queued sends and gated tasks"). `Reachable.slotIn` and its siblings are proved of slots no drain writes (`isQueue p x = false`). For a queue mutation's slot, carry the property of the answers its waiting sends will land (`∀ q ∈ c.queued, q.1 = x → ∀ v, V (.some v)`) through `runAction` and `nextCommit`. Lean's `pending(m)` of a waiting queue is false (the runner's is true): model it if a program needs it.
- **What the Lean semantics still leaves out** (2026-10-04, refreshed 2026-10-17; semantics/README.md "What the semantics leaves out"). Routes, `t(...)`, the `format*` entries, frame tasks, a mutation's `then` and form-control payloads landed. Left: geometry reads (`frame`, `measure`, `elementFromPoint`), `toLowerCase` (Unicode's case mapping, which the runner takes from Rust's `str::to_lowercase`), the host's `navigate` event, a reload, and `exactTransport`/`exactMIDI` reads. `difftest explore contract/corpus apps/*/app.contract` reports Metronome (`exactTransport`) and Shelf (`toLowerCase`) as outside it. A virtualized list's rows are left out of the observation (the runner windows them by layout), so a scripted tap on one is not compared meaningfully.
- **tvOS, what is owed** (2026-10-03, landed from Doug Lowder's `doug/tvos`; LLP 1008 §9 "tvOS"). iOS-only files that shared code names break it (twice in one landing: `PointerIOS.swift`, then `NavigationTitleIOS.swift` and `GroupedListIOS.swift`); the tier 2 async lane (`scripts/async.mjs --tier 2`, hourly) now builds tvOS and files what breaks, but nothing starts that lane on fleet hardware yet; since 2026-10-12 the per-commit lane compiles it too (`build.mjs --tvos --no-install`, which needs no simulator) for any range beyond documentation. `scripts/agent.mjs` has no tvOS target (it installs the iOS build), so `smoke.mjs` cannot drive a TV, and nothing here presses the Siri Remote by script (`simctl`/`devicectl` have no route; Doug drove it with Argent). No device builds, icons (layered brand assets) or Top Shelf. A grouped list's toggle row shows a checkmark but Select does not flip it.
- **Android TV, what is owed** (2026-10-20, [LLP 1115.001.003](llp/1115.001.003-android-tv.rfc.md) §4; stage 1 landed 2026-10-23: Caltrain on the `tv_api_34` emulator takes the D-pad, Select and Back). Owed: the look a hand-built Compose for TV app has (§5 Q2; on the emulator Caltrain's light cards show white text faintly, and its departure minutes are barely visible); a banner per app; `focusGuide="auto"`; play and pause; text entry with the TV's keyboard (a hosted field's D-pad goes to Android's focus search, not the presenter's); a control inside a pressable row, which no arrow reaches; a TV launcher tile checked by hand (Android lists the leanback entry; the tile was not reached on Google TV's Apps row); driving the real keys from the agent, and a TV smoke.
- **A plan decoded inside wasm at activation** (2026-10-03, LLP 1029.000's per-byte fuel). Activation hands the data module the whole encoded plan, which it decodes at about 430 wasmi instructions a byte: Caltrain's 46 KB plan is about 20 million. Unmeasured: what that costs in wall time on the native interpreted path against the 100 ms cold-start budget. If it matters, activation gives the module only the tables it reads.
- **Hosts outside the repository** (2026-10-03, Charlie: "out-of-tree is for like Amazon's new TV operating system thing"; `rules/DEFERRED.md` §Surfaces). An RFC beside LLP 1036.001 (apps outside the repository): what a host with its own UI stack consumes from exact2 (the plan and kernel wire, the runner, the agent protocol), and what exact2 would have to publish for it (a stable host ABI, `host/web-js/conform.mjs` runnable outside this repository, drivers that do not assume `host/*`). Amazon's Vega OS is the motivating case; first find out what native surface its SDK gives an app.
- **A standing web competitor bench** (2026-10-04, `~/bench/dioxus`). The perf scoreboard measures iOS against SwiftUI and UIKit and never the web or a bounded window's shift. One afternoon against Dioxus Web found the feed shipped three times (cf12cb9ae), the whole-list tap (now a development-build warning, `host/web-js/seam.js`), a lazy source's first-shift hitch, and the clamp bug that threw readers back 55–59 rows at every window shift on web, macOS and iOS (ca83e4eaa, 04c1822ec). The harness (`web/bench.mjs`: load, bytes, heap, scroll at four speeds, taps; `web/progress.mjs`: content progress by row) and the Dioxus port (`heavy-dx/`) are there: give them a run on each landing that touches the web host or collections, beside the iOS series.
- **Sub-second Rust edits without hot-patching** (2026-10-05, [LLP 1029.000.001](llp/1029.000.001-sub-second-rust-edits.rfc.md), Draft, awaiting Charlie). A one-line data-crate edit costs 2.3–3.2 s on the JS target's dev loop and 1.6–2.0 s with live replacement on native; the crate compiles in 0.13–0.55 s ([LLP 1029.000.000](llp/1029.000.000-hot-patching-rust-measured.research.md)). The RFC builds the dev module in `logic-dev`, swaps the module in the page for a Rust-only edit, and builds only the variants connected clients run, in parallel; Subsecond-style function patching was weighed and declined.
- **Grouped lists, what is left** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1084](llp/1084-native-grouped-lists.rfc.md) §4, §7). (The large title collapses with the collection view, and grouped rows take `delete=` and hand-written swipes: checked 2026-10-20.) The Signal Clone's `Section`/`Row`/`ActionRow` components can become a grouped list (its repository). `sidebar` styles wait for an iPad or Mac consumer.

- **Styleable materials** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) Q3). A `style` class cannot carry `backgroundMaterial` or `glassGroup` because they are props; LLP 1069.011 D12 already makes `buttonStyle` a styleable prop, and these two can join it. WebKit's `-apple-visual-effect` is not a browser name under LLP 1081 D1 (it is behind `useSystemAppearance`), so there is nothing to adopt.
- **Style names and their kinds in `schema.json`** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) Q5). LLP 1081 puts the author style names, with provenance, in Contract's `style_names.rs`. Moving them into the schema is a mapping design, not two new fields: names and rows are many to many (`gap` sets two rows, `translate` sets `Translate` and `TranslateZ`, `flex` sets three different values).
- **CamelCase for Contract's kebab-case props** (2026-10-03, [LLP 1081](llp/1081-names-exact-invents.rfc.md) Q6). `scroll-start`, `scroll-restoration`, `initial-item-count` and `bitmap-width`/`bitmap-height` look like CSS properties and are props; camelCase, Contract's spelling for its own props, would make a kebab-case name always mean CSS or an HTML attribute. Ask Charlie first about `bitmap-width`/`bitmap-height`, his "for now" spelling.
- **What `tree --ax` found on macOS and iOS** (2026-10-03, LLP 1080.002 §7). AppKit exposes the accessibility fixture's `aria-hidden` decoration and the button inside its `inert` column (`exposed-hidden` ×2, LLP 1001's "AppKit/Linux subtree enforcement remains open"), and its checkbox and password field are exposed through cells that answer no name (the label is set on the control view; whether VoiceOver speaks it is unverified). (On iOS a `role="img"` column is one image, its name its label, its button hidden, as on the web: checked 2026-10-20.) Not built: a projected segment's join to its tab, and the macOS window toolbar's walk (both reported as coverage limits). On iOS the read needs UIKit's accessibility runtime: `xcrun simctl spawn <udid> defaults write com.apple.Accessibility ApplicationAccessibilityEnabled -bool true` on a simulator, then relaunch; the driver could set it for an `ax` drive.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/b3, 6dcf3bed91, 9cb75a97a6): hidden and inert checkbox and secure-field subtrees stay suppressed through restoration, read through the AX client; the fixture's exposed-hidden decoration and inert-column button are unverified while the desktop is locked.
- **LLP 1079 on hardware, what is unrun** (2026-10-03, `perf`'s §As built). Linux's display-loop sampler and `SIGUSR1` trace were type-checked for `aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu`, never run on a DRM display. iOS Save Trace was clicked on a simulator (2026-10-05: saved, Share… offers the file, `agent.mjs trace --phone <simulator>` copied it from the container and read it); on a phone neither the share sheet's AirDrop nor the driver's `devicectl` copy has been run, and macOS's ⌥⌘T was not clicked. The `--stress-url` p95 comparison, measuring on and off, was not run.
- **The JS target's development `perf` costs about 3.7 µs a commit** (2026-10-03, LLP 1079 §As built). 800 caltrain hover commits took 9.7 ms against 6.7 ms with `perf.js` stubbed out. Two-thirds is the mutation observer that gives `authored`/`created`/`retired`, the rest the `pf` wrappers; production carries neither. A cheaper count would need `rt.js` to stamp its own writes, which every production bundle would then carry.
- **JS-target grant parity, what is left** (2026-10-02, landed from `web/grants` (c4e02b00, 207c2c04, dcd938ed) after three review rounds; both final reviews still mark these). Unicode spellings the Rust parser accepts and the web treats differently: U+0085 in a module's grant text is refused at `host/web/module-glue.js` prepare; with a U+FEFF line a parser-accepted `auth.session` never installs on the JS target, and the wasm page's surface check admits a U+FEFF line native refuses. A `Response` with an empty URL (an opaque, `no-cors` answer) skips the final-origin check, so an ungranted redirect can succeed there. A redirect whose hop the browser itself rejects reports `Network`, not native's `Refused` (a declared deviation, LLP 1071 §7: the page cannot see the destination). A Rust-only page exposes its file commands before its grant set is installed. Two `Store` facade differences from native in `host/web-js/ts-data.js`. `apps/fieldnotes/web/tests/worker.rs` is not migrated to grant-set arguments. Out of scope by Charlie's ruling (grants on the web are parity, not a sandbox: the page's own code can always reach the browser's fetch): forged, self-sealed or accessor-backed grant objects, and app code replacing the fetch late host modules use.
- **Animated scroll to the end on send, what is left** (2026-10-02, Signal Clone; iOS, macOS and the web host built 2026-10-03, LLP 1070.000 §11). Linux and the JS web target land a smooth correction at once. A plain `scroll` still jumps on a `scrollTop` write followed to the end.
- **Smooth collection jumps cross unbuilt rows** (2026-10-03, astra's review of the smooth-correction hosts, `llp/reviews/code-2026-10-03-signal-gaps.astra.md` #5). While a smooth correction animates, every host reports the destination: a mid-flight report told the runner that the reader had left the end, and that stopped the end-follow. So a smooth `scrollIntoView` farther than the mounted window crosses spacers no row fills. A fix needs the report to carry both the physical port, which is what to realize, and the destination, which is what to anchor. That is a wire change in `CollectionFacts`. A send's end-follow is within the window and never shows it.

Owed: James's two cases at a pinned revision in his app; real touches (a finger on a bar item, a finger's collapse and pull to top, a held sheet drag, a minimizing tab bar); a cold launch filmed on a phone with and without hatches, and a memory warning; Stage 4 (TypeScript by experiment), which needs NativeScript's runtime, a dependency nobody approved; web and AppKit hatches; the `element` hatch and the named action (Stage 5, on demand); `back()` and `open(url)` on `ExactTabContents`; the development ownership check on an app's tab container. - **A module view's input, twice** (2026-10-02, astra's delta review of the hatches' fixes). On both web targets a module view hears the page's own input as any element does (glue.js `attach`; rt.js `on` since e0ee5759f) and its module's reports (`event(code)`): a module whose own button reports `event(0)` for a click the page also sees delivers one press twice, and focus, blur and keys likewise. LLP 1024 should say which one a module view hears for each kind (the module's report only for what the page cannot see, or the module's only); Apple's hosts are the third to check.

- **Kept answers: repeated activation and slow first answers** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1027.005](llp/1027.005-resource-identity-and-request-context.rfc.md) D4/D5). Two issues reproduced during review, neither part of the `with` landing. (1) Consecutive `data_ready()` calls before an asynchronous reply issued tickets 1 and 2: a forced ask bypasses `enqueue`'s identical-request rule. No host calls it twice today (Apple and Linux guard with `data_activated`, wasm activates once per boot), so the fix is small and only for a future host. (2) Kept answers are admitted only for a source unready at boot and then remain visible while its asynchronous reply is pending: on the JS runtime, Fieldnotes's real TypeScript source showed the bake's "Opening your notebook…" until a controlled SQLite reply, and the runner does the same for any source ready at boot. The JS target also keeps nothing for a Rust module loaded after first paint. Whether a consumer needs either closed is follow-up 3 of the RFC.

- **Web motion followers** (2026-10-03). The release-follower lifecycle bug is fixed by a targeted change: post-commit reconciliation cancels a follower as soon as its consumer no longer resolves to that source. `web/motion2` (shelved on expo-build-1000) tried and abandoned compositor followers for CSS-eased sources: reproducing CSS animation semantics in retimed followers kept diverging, so main's per-frame seeking stays for that case. Still owed (the final review, codex sol): the real-plan test's later samples are corrected by the agent clock itself (each `clock` op reconciles timelines on both web targets), so it would stay green if the handed-off per-frame callback stopped running; add a check that lets the page's own `requestAnimationFrame` loop run without agent clock operations between samples.
- **Agent web drives, what the no-GPU fix left** (2026-10-02): `screenshot` settles the GPU before its two frame waits and image wait, not after; the JS target's agent settle does not await the GPU module's first import; `surfaceWork` ignores a pending `settled()`; `fallbackApplied` survives a successful module swap. None is the user-facing hang (fixed); each is a narrower race in the agent's drive.
- **JS dev reload carry, what is left** (2026-10-02): the logic revision hashes the app chunk and `app.module.wasm` only, so a native page module served at a stable URL (`./modules/index.js`) can change without dropping settled answers; a checkpoint is deleted before its page is written, so a retried GET boots fresh silently; the route-stack assertion would pass with router carry removed; a blurred autofocus field can take focus again after a carried reload (plausible); Caltrain's edit-to-frame is 273 ms p50 against the 100 ms budget (it was over before the carry).
- **Web test freshness and prerequisites from the build** (2026-10-02, `web/webtests` b8b93ed5, reverted before landing). One source of truth (`build.mjs --check` running the build's own checks; freshness roots from Cargo's dependency closure) is the right design, but that commit made the check a mandatory preflight that runs Cargo before a game's `.shells` workspace exists, breaking every clean game web build, and it still missed the render binary's and GPU crates' inputs and deleted inputs. `CHROME=''` is still taken as a browser path.
- **Firefox/WebKit carrier fidelity** (2026-10-02, the async `conform-firefox`/`conform-webkit` steps): keys go to an iframe guest before Chrome's browser-owned path and non-button keys take a different route than Chrome's; a wheel moves the pointer first (hover becomes part of the input, so the baselined RealWorld scroll gap is not a pure wheel-delta difference); a root with a `testId` double-counts document scroll; the public session advertises held contacts the carrier refuses; synthetic plans still compile after a launch failure. WebKit runs on a Mac (Linux lacks 36 system libraries and the builders have no sudo): first run 2026-10-02 on the M5 mini (WebKit 26.6, `bunx playwright@1.63.0 install webkit`, Chrome for Testing 153 as the oracle) matched Chrome's state on all six apps, 0 failures against `known-webkit.json` (a button is not focused by a click, native input widths). Real Safari through `safaridriver` stays optional: it needs `safaridriver --enable` once, by an admin.
- **CSS grid on the web, what is left** (2026-10-02, landed with the web program: every grid row lowers on both web targets, the kernel's grid grammar parses through cssparser, and `host/web-js/conformance/grid.contract` holds browser boxes to the kernel's with `--linux`). The final review's leftovers: an auto-placed named span (`auto / span 2 foo`) lays out as `span 1` natively (Taffy resolves named spans only against an explicit line — declare it in LLP 1001 or patch Taffy); grids that resolve past Taffy's 10,000 tracks (`repeat(auto-fill, 1px)` in a wide box, `10000 / span 2`) are not refused.
- **A covered route column with a width of its own runs past the screen** (2026-10-17, guard-rail evaluation round 2, a grocery app on the iPhone Duo fully open and closed). While Exact's containers take the whole view, a route's sides are covered by the safe area (`routeEdges`), and the kernel adds a cover to the route's padding (`kernel/src/kernel/cover.rs`), which under `content-box` lies outside a width the author wrote: a route column with `width="100%"` beside the Duo's 84-point side strip is 84 points wider than the viewport, and its content runs under the strip; with `.automatic` route scrollers, a grouped list's cards there run past the screen's edge. A route written with insets and no width (`inset=0`), or with its width and `box-sizing="border-box"`, is laid out right; `inset=0` beside a `width="100%"` still overflows. Taking the cover out of the authored size instead was tried and dropped: a cover deliberately grows a box so its authored content keeps its size (an authored tablist over the home indicator), and rewriting the size breaks a ratio and an explicit `flex-basis`. LLP 1123 stage 2's wrapper (D3), which shrinks the containing block and leaves the authored box alone, is the fix.
- **A route column moved down a laptop fold keeps the window's top inset** (2026-10-20, Xcode 27.1, iOS 27.1 24A94425, the Duo simulator turned and folded to 130°, under UIKit's bars). The guidance evaluation's grocery app moves its route column to the lower segment (`top="env(viewport-segment-top 0 1)"`). With `navigationBar="none"` its header paints in the column, but 82 points below the segment's top, the window's top safe area with the device turned (`smoke-duo.mjs`'s `TURNED`), and the column, with a height of its own under `content-box`, grows by those 82 points, past the screen's bottom (one placed by `top` and `bottom` keeps its box and starts its content 82 points down). The host covers a route's top with its controller's top safe area wherever the route sits (`NavigationCoversIOS.routeEdges`), its sides likewise while the containers take the whole view, and its bottom only as far as the route reaches it. Covering each edge only as far as the route reaches it, as the authored tablist's cover does, would fix it. LLP 1123 stage 2 (D3, the entry above) does not fix it as specified: a route's clearance is its controller's view's safe area, and the inset wrapper is the containing block of an absolutely positioned root too, so the column would sit 82 points below the segment it names. Stage 2 needs the same remedy, with the reach measured with no clearance, as D3 measures a scroller's, and segment positions relative to the box a pane is placed in (the wrapper's inner box): `env(viewport-segment-*)` stays the window's (D2), so a pane placed by it inside a cleared route would move down by the clearance too. Meanwhile the foldables recipe keeps scrolling content in place, as UIKit's own list does, and warns that a route moved off an edge is still padded.
- **Form controls, what is left** (2026-10-02, landed: a control in block flow is shrink-to-fit as in Chrome, 960 recorded Chrome cases; Caltrain's station Back button's tap area is its label now, as on the web). Caltrain's station search field is 20 characters wide in its card on every host (the text-field rule of 2026-09-30); if it should fill the card, its plan says `width="100%"`.
