- **Android memory after a crypto fling, where it goes** (2026-10-05, Pixel 10 Pro XL, exact2 320 vs Views 196 MB PSS: graphics 200 vs 153, native heap 76 vs 31, Java 19–21 vs 6). `dumpsys gfxinfo`: Skia's GPU cache holds 88 MB of scratch surfaces in ~12,600 entries for exact2 against 62 MB in ~5,900 for Views (37 vs 12 MB purgeable); marking single-draw animated layers non-overlapping (`setHasOverlappingRendering(false)`) did not change it, so it is not the reader's opacity layers; candidates are rounded-rect/path clips (row clips, avatars) needing mask surfaces, the dashed sparkline re-recorded each draw-in frame, and even-odd `RING` paths. Native heap (heapprofd): HWUI's Vulkan pipelines 12.6 MB and textures ~9 MB (more distinct draw kinds than Views'), exact2's own ~16 MB (settled data values 5, plan 2.75, layout leaves 1.7, rows 1.2, shaping ~1.5). The Java heap is not the stream copies (recycling them left it unchanged).
- **Android CPU after `android/cpu2`** (2026-10-05, Pixel 10a, 6 runs a variant against Views in one session; cells are process CPU ms/s, exact / Views). Landed: the text cache's per-paint trim walks only when a budget could be over; a list row holding a scroller (easy's swipe-to-delete) is a kept row while the scroller stays put (every easy paint used to record every row that showed again); symbol pictures take their em square before their commit's layout (no second layout per row built); symbol paths parsed once; a symbol's font size read as one row, not a copied style; image visibility indexes the painted boxes once a paint; the reader (bench patch `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/cpu2-reader.patch`) draws a dashed stroke as its path's segments, and a layer's draw-in dash reads and measures its path once, not every frame (crypto's main thread −25% at 12k). easy fling 1k 499/643, 3k 783/605, 6k 636/560, 12k 683/684, 24k 754/913 (before: 562, 683, 975, 776, 986; the `exact` thread 63–274 from 91–334 ms/s; Mcycles/s now below Views at every speed but 3k). crypto fling 760/714, 836/734, 985/717, 1064/773, 1285/947; crypto rest 713/655. Left: (1) a row costs ~2.8 M instructions on crypto (a big-core probe, `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/crypto/probe`): binding evaluation in the VM (the chart's 48-point `points`, ~8%; decode ~20% of that), Taffy flexbox over five flex containers a row and text measured twice (max-content, then the definite width) after shaping (~20%), apply and its style cascade (~9%), motion's restatement of renewed nodes (~5%), the row's recording (~15%); Views' crypto row is a hand laid-out ViewGroup. (2) RenderThread runs on the little cores for whole segments (200–390 ms/s there against ~240 on the mid cores; Views' rarely does): the main thread's frames are short, so HWUI's hints ask for less; pinning it to the mid and big cores (tried: `fast_cores` by tid) kept it there but at lower clocks, mixed in ms/s, not kept. (3) crypto rest: the tick path is ~45% of the `exact` thread (the source's list copy, the old list's drop, `update_data`'s two passes over 5,000 items, conformance); a source that names the rows it changed would remove most of it (the runner takes `Changed { list, positions }` beside a value and skips the compare and rekey for the rest).
- **Android Canvas host against Views, by thread, on Parley** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL, ms/s exact - Views). crypto 6k: exact thread +255, RenderThread +27..40, hwuiTask +46..80, main -27; 12k: exact +350, RenderThread +29..43, hwuiTask +27, main -74. heavy 6k: exact +174, RenderThread +25, decode level (63 vs ~70 of the Views app's dispatcher and upload threads), main -59; 12k: exact +259, RenderThread +38, main -84. The gap is the exact thread less what the main thread saves; nothing else is worth a tenth of it. Probe shares of that thread at 6k-12k dp/s on Parley: layout 18.6% (48% of it text measuring, 22% shaping new strings, which paint needs anyway), the rebind's bindings 17% (the chart's `points` in the VM 6.3%; its parse at paint is 2.3%, so a parse cache buys little), recording rows 14% (the `svg` 6.4%), apply 7%, other commit effects 11%. RenderThread's excess on crypto: text placed again each frame (drawTextBlob 2.4 times the Views app's: Skia reuses a run's glyph placement only across whole-pixel moves, and the feed moves by fractions) and more path masks made (the software path renderer strokes 1.5 times as many contours; why is not found: the pulse ring and dot as rounded rects instead of four-cubic paths changed nothing). Rows and moved groups on whole pixels in the reader (`~/bench/lists/exact-c2v11/cpu2-snap.patch`) took 9-19 ms/s off RenderThread on both lists but added ~35 ms/s to heavy's main thread at 1k-6k, unexplained, so it is not merged.
- **Android Canvas host, a list row's cost while it scrolls: what the crypto probe shows** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL; `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/probe.sh`, `DP=1` for the bench driver's dp/s). After held spares and tracks with moves, at 6k-24k dp/s the exact thread is per-row work: layout 21% (the list is already the layout root and clean rows are cache hits; a rebound row is laid out whole because its five texts changed, ~7 measure callbacks a text of which ~3 reach the text engine, 45% of layout in measuring; a text whose box cannot change, nowrap in a stretched column, still dirties its ancestors), the rebind's bindings 18% (a third of it the VM building the chart's 48-point `points` string, which the kernel then parses again at paint), recording the row 14% (mostly the `svg`), apply 6.5%. Not tried: a row-local layout when a rebind changes no child order (the list's children change in the same commit, so the root is dirty); `points` as numbers end to end; recording only a rebound row's changed nodes (the recorder keeps a row whole, compared after it is walked). A report inside the window is still O(mounted rows) on both sides (the host sends every row's height each report, ~0.25 ms on a mid core, 80 a second).
- **`lead=1` on the Android reader (a window leading toward the travel) builds and destroys the lead every pass**: `refine_slice` resets the velocity to 0 when it returns, and the next unheld frame's pass (velocity 0) retires the rows the lead built (probe `VEL=1`: 2,093 rows built in 2 s at 24k px/s for 286 entering). Off by default, so no run measured it.
- **Android Canvas host, cold start: the first frame with content is three or four Vulkan pipelines** (easy on a Pixel 10 Pro XL: RenderThread 31-46 ms in `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`, PowerVR compiling shaders, for FillRectOp, FillRRectOp, CircularRRectOp and text; the Views app's first frame is 11 ms). The reader draws a uniform rounded rect (Skia's CircularRRectOp) and one with unequal corners (FillRRectOp) differently: one op for both would drop a pipeline. The benchmark force-stops within 3 s, before HWUI saves its pipeline cache. The engine's boot is 18 ms of which half is the list's 10,000 items (decode 21%, `rekey_full` 27%, shape check 4%), but first content waits for the frame after the boot ends, so a shorter boot moves it only by whole frames.
- **Android GPU canvas in hardware buffers: what is owed** (2026-10-07; the reader's part is `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/cpu2-gpubuffers.patch`, against bench e6e9118). Run on PowerVR (Pixel 10 Pro XL) only. Owed: (1) a run on Mali and Adreno with a screenshot of a shader row: the buffer is rendered by wgpu's Vulkan device and sampled by HWUI's with no queue-family transfer to `VK_QUEUE_FAMILY_FOREIGN_EXT` and no fence, only wgpu's word that the submission completed, which PowerVR accepts; `gpubuf=0` is the window path. (2) The reader's fallback when buffers are refused (`canvasFrame` -2: it swaps the element's view for a TextureView) has never run. (3) A frame is published at the exact thread's next frame after its GPU work completes and drawn at the reader's next: up to a frame later than the window path showed it. (4) What it bought is small (Extra Heavy 1k 386 -> 362 mW, 3k 393 -> 379): with canvases off entirely Extra Heavy is ~340 mW at 1k, so most of a canvas's cost is its own render and `vkQueueSubmit` (a third of the exact thread while one shows; PowerVR's `RGXKickTA` and fence merges in the call). The lever left is not submitting from the exact thread at all: the shader in the reader's pass (AGSL from the same source), or a submit thread.
- **`-exact-animation-trigger` on the hosts that do not have it** (2026-10-06, [LLP 1055](llp/1055-svg-shapes-and-css-animations.rfc.md) D13). `view` is the default on Linux and Android. - **crypto and xheavy energy on the Pixel 10 Pro XL, what was tried** (2026-10-06, `bin/energy.py`, CPU+GPU rails). Fixed: a collection pass took the feed's whole snapshot twice to count the rows it built (+2% of crypto's probe since `android/xblank`). Not the cause or not worth it: (1) crypto's `hwuiTask` threads at 3k-24k (Skia's software path masks, ~250-340 ms/s against Views' ~150-200; ~10 at 1k) are the charts' draw-in: a new path a frame for every visible row. The animation starts at mount, a viewport ahead, so rows show the last half of it (long paths, ~625 us of masks a frame against Views' ~345, which shows the first half) and none of it at 1k. Drawing the drawn part as round-capped `drawLines` removes the masks but puts +180 ms/s on RenderThread and triples CPU power at 3k-6k (315 -> 960 mW): RenderThread time is the expensive kind. Skipping recordings under half a pixel of dash travel cut the masks 14% at 3k with nothing measurable in mW; not kept. (2) `fast=0` (the exact thread on any core) and `boost=0` (no ADPF hint) change neither mW nor frames on crypto. (3) xheavy at 1k: the shader canvas's wgpu submit and present are a third of the exact thread (~80 ms/s) and the GPU rail is ~90 mW against Views' ~58; a canvas out of view already draws nothing (skipping by viewport changed nothing). Drawing a GPU module's canvas in the reader's own pass is still the lever.
- **Android list CPU after `android/cpu2`'s structural pass** (2026-10-06, Pixel 10a; the bench reader's part is `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-cpu2/cpu2-hwpictures.patch`). Done: StyleProps' 20 rare rows behind one shared pointer (`"rare": true` in schema.json), a decode under way finishing into the cache, JPEG decoded at its shown size by the platform, and platform-decoded pictures decoded straight into the `AHardwareBuffer` the reader wraps (`hardwarePictures`/`imageBuffer`; a heavy fling run made and filled ~1,900 buffers on the exact thread for 106 decodes, because the reader's copy went ten paints after a picture left). Tried and not worth it: a memo of apply's set-style patches (−0.1% of crypto's probe), the SVG cascade's clone (~0.3%), cosmic-text's `shape-run-cache` (−0.6% of heavy's probe: its words rarely repeat within the cache's life). Left, heavy at 24k against Views (~1,150 vs ~810 ms/s): the exact thread and main together (~530 vs Views' main ~290: layout 20% of the exact thread, text measure 12% of it; paint 16%; `sync_images_moved` 11%, of which a third is its own index rebuild at every commit and a third `Images::poll` walking every view), RenderThread (~230–330 vs ~200), and the decode threads (~150–350 vs ~70: the same libjpeg work a picture, plus the bilinear scale to the shown size, 17% of them; Views' loader lets the GPU scale). PNG pictures and canvases still take the copy route. `android/xblank`'s time-budgeted refine costs crypto's and easy's probes +2% (3.462 → 3.536 G instructions on crypto); not looked into.
- **Android list rows after `android/row`, second pass: what was tried and what a rebound row still costs** (2026-10-08, Pixel 10 Pro XL; probes and tools in `~/bench/lists/exact-rowv11`: `rprobe.sh`, `istat.sh`, `batchab.sh`, `prof/shares.py`, `abtable.py`). Landed first: the row layout memo (Taffy patch 29), restated values equal to the style's presenting nothing, a picture's arrival drawing its slot and not its row, the image sync's box index built when asked. Landed second: a collection pass waits for a quarter viewport of travel (`Travel::waits`), so rows mount two or more a commit below 12,000 dp/s: the exact thread, ms/s at 1k/3k/6k/12k/24k, easy 56/98/164/237/262 to 51/88/135/236/260, heavy 63/102/162/244/360 to 59/99/157/234/351 (a waiting pass still asks for the pictures that came into view, which is most of what a heavy pass costs: without that it was 50/85/144 and its placeholders showed for three points more of the view), crypto 96/174/254/351/484 to 69/133/216/340/481. Tried and not landed, each measured on the probe at 12,000 dp/s: (1) **each flex container in a row as its own memo root** (branch `android/row-nested`, 0963be075: a style starts deep, one trace a row, and turns shallow, a trace a container, when under half its boxes replay; traces by style and input, a bound a style): crypto replays 95% of its containers and the instructions are the same (1,955 to 1,946 M), heavy the same, easy +2.5%. Crypto's and heavy's layout time is text measure (shaping 8%, line breaking 6% on crypto), not the flex algorithm (7%), and a replay a container costs about what the flex it saves does. (2) `Engine::remove` by a mask of the properties a node holds, and an allocation's pin count read without its lock (branch `android/row-micro`): 0.3% of easy's instructions, cycles the same. (3) A bump arena for the transaction's scratch: allocation inside `apply_document` is 1.4% of the thread on easy and heavy; the 16% is spread over recording 3.7, the runner 3.4, commit effects 2.5, layout 1.5, no site over 0.6. Left: (a) **recording is 20% of easy's thread** (boxes, symbols and order 16.5, text 3.2). A rebound row whose frames equal the row it was could redraw only its text and symbol leaves in slots, as pictures now are; in easy a rebind keeps its height one time in three or four, so that is about 3.5%. A template across rows, keyed by the row's geometry and styles, would reach most rebinds (8 to 10%) and is a redesign of the row walk: boxes for hit testing, paragraph leases, frames and layers all come from that walk. (b) **Easy's layout is still 16%**: the replay 6.6 (about 270 steps a row), leaf measures 6.5 (68 leaf computes a row, 38 of them two texts'), publication 2. A text's answers cached by its content would be stale after a font loads; it needs the measurer to say when its answers change. (c) **Heavy's image sync, 12.5%**: at each of three calls a pass `Images::poll` walks every view (4.3%), the box index is rebuilt after each commit (2.4%), and each request and cancel counts cold entries by reading every entry's pins (1.5%). The batching above leaves them as they were (the picture sync runs for a waiting pass too); a poll only when a decode woke or a view changed needs the waiting-for-budget retries kept, and would be worth about what the waiting passes' sync costs: heavy's exact thread at 1,000 to 6,000 dp/s was 15 to 20% lower without it. (d) Above 12,000 dp/s nothing changed: a pass every third step is already more than a quarter viewport. The Canvas recorder's tests (`canvas::slot`, `canvas::stream`) and the pass batching's host half compile only for Android and no lane runs them: `cargo test -p exact-linux --target aarch64-linux-android --lib --no-run`, push the binary, run it with `canvas::` on a phone. **Third pass** (2026-10-08, same phone and tools; `ledab.sh`, the probes take `STEPS=1` for a step a frame and `EVERY=N` for the pass cadence). Landed: a renewed node the motion engine holds only settled values for, restated bare, is not forgotten first (`Engine::at_rest`, `MotionSync::renewed`): easy 696 to 666 M instructions at 12,000 dp/s, crypto 1,953 to 1,895. What commit effects still do for an easy rebind, of the thread: the sync's rows applied a node (set transitions, layout transition, clock, animations, timeline: nine calls a node for rows that are empty), `motion_sync` building them 1.3%, paint sync 0.8%, lowering eligibility 0.7%, the two handle discoveries 0.7% when a node was created. (e) A pass every sixth step instead of every third was measured worth 18 to 25% of easy's exact thread at 12,000 and 24,000 dp/s and needed the window to lead; it landed in the fourth pass, below. Measured and not landed: (f) **The recording template across rows** was scoped, not built. What it needs: the recorder to set leaves apart inline (a hole a text or symbol leaf: the clips around it and which the row had written, the matrix forgotten after it, as a picture's slot does; SLOT, LAYER and IMAGE ops refuse a template in a first version, so heavy and crypto rows are recorded as now); the painter to keep, a recorded row, each node's type, style (the arena's shared one), frame in the row, presented values, and the props its walk reads (`Type`, `Disabled`, `Value`, `SemanticTag`, `Text`, `SwipeIndicator`, `Markup`, `Href`, `BackgroundMaterial`, `TestId`, `Placeholder`, `Checked`, `ButtonStyle`, `AccessibilityRole`, the image and SVG sources), and for a row to record, to walk its nodes against a template's (proved a node at a time, as the layout memo's visits are), copy the template's ops between holes, record each leaf again in its hole, and take boxes, frames and scrolls from the template by node index. Worth about 11% of easy's exact thread at 12,000 dp/s (recording 21.6% less text 3.6, symbols 1.6, the paint order 2.2, the dirty walk 1.2 and the walk against the template), which is 3% of the app's CPU there; nothing for crypto or heavy until slots and layers are carried. **Fourth pass** (2026-10-09, branch `android/led`, same phone; probes and tools in `~/bench/lists/exact-led`: `leadab.sh`, `thr.py`, `blanktab.py`, `gaps.py`, `midlate.py`). Landed: from 8,000 dp/s (until 4,000, a turn or 100 ms without a step) the Canvas host reports the feed with a lead of all the runner allows and a pass waits for it (`Travel::lead`, `Travel::waits_led`; `EXACT_PASS_LEAD=0`: off): a pass each sixth step at 24,000 dp/s and each fifteenth at 12,000. Three things were in its way. (1) Every report between two passes must carry the lead: the reader's ask for what a slice left, when it came 12 ms after the last step, and a frame's own report went without it, found the rows mounted ahead past their window and retired the farthest. (2) The runner: with more than a viewport of travel between passes the rows behind are all two viewports past the port when a pass sees them, so the far-row rule retires them in the first slice, which builds only its limit; the rest were past the hold's reach and destroyed, and the slices after built from nothing (easy at 24,000 dp/s: 135 builds in 661 mounts, 18 without a lead). A slice now holds as many retiring rows as its window still needs (`build_needed`'s `more`): 33 builds, the 16 more the window growing as each of the run's two leads starts. (3) The painter told its backend a rebound row was the row it had been, so the reader drew the other item's recording in its place until the next stream: at 3,000 dp/s toward the start, on main too, a wrong and shorter row for up to five frames; under the lead one frame each pass. A renewed row now drops its kept recording (`Dirty::commit`, `rows_dirty`). Exact thread, ms/s at 12,000 and 24,000 dp/s (three runs each way): easy 229/252 to 131/214, crypto 331/483 to 237/408, heavy 238/356 to 205/333; 1,000 to 6,000 as they were. The process: easy 816/853 to 712/840, crypto 1,113/1,274 to 947/1,201, heavy 991/1,204 to 924/1,212 (at 24,000 the reader records rows that mount far from the view on its worker, 45 to 70 ms/s there, where main made them in the frame). Blank area 0.0% at every speed in all three (main: easy 0.2% at 3,000, crypto 0.1 to 0.3% and heavy 0.1% at 24,000). Left: (g) **The bench reader has to make far rows over later frames for this** (`~/bench/lists/exact-led/reader-later-rows.patch`, against the bench's `exact-v11`; not in this repo). A led pass mounts some twenty crypto rows in one stream, and the reader made each row's layers (three a row, 0.07 ms each), took its tracks and copied it for its worker as the stream arrived: 7 to 10 ms of one main-thread frame (`exact ingest`), and a frame presented late about once in eight seconds at 12,000 and 24,000 dp/s (6 gaps over 12.5 ms in 24 segments; none in 40 on main). With the patch a row out of view only has what it defines made as its stream arrives; its node, layers and tracks are made in the frames after, 2 ms a frame, nearest the travel first (ingest 3 ms at most, no gap in 40 segments). A reader in this repo, when there is one, needs the same. (h) **A pass costs about 52,000 instructions a mounted row whether or not the row changed** (easy at 24,000 dp/s with the lead and no waiting: 906 to 1,056 M instructions for nine more rows mounted over 320 passes): the list's layout, publication, the paint order and the stream walk each visit every row. That is 300 M of main's 906 M at 24,000 dp/s, and why the lead gains 15% there and not 25%: half the passes, each half again as dear. A window that trimmed the side behind while it leads would take a viewport of rows off each pass; the runner's `lead` is where. (i) **A turn at speed ends the lead for eight steps**, the window goes back to a viewport each side and then leads the other way: 13 builds on easy that a lead carried through the turn would rebind. Only the bench turns at 24,000 dp/s. The kept rows of `paint/rows.rs` are tested on Linux through a backend that keeps rows and draws nothing (`presenter/kept_rows_tests.rs`). **Fifth pass** (2026-10-09, branch `android/led-mem`): what the led window cost in memory after a fling, Pixel 10 Pro XL. (1) A reader asks `owed` as its last step's frame goes out; when that frame was a paint nothing was owed yet, no timer was due, and the window stayed led at rest: `next_due` now names the pass that ends a lead (`settle_due`). (2) xheavy 565 to 699 MB: the reader gives a row's map its view a viewport ahead and keeps it half a viewport behind, and a led window names the row ahead while the last still has its view, so a second map (110 MB) was made and both were kept. The reader now lends the view of a row out of view before it makes another (`reader-memory-and-soon.patch`, against the bench's `exact-v11`; the same patch makes a waiting row the scroll is about to show at once, whatever the frame's budget: xheavy rows take more than the 2 ms to make and came into view still waiting, 5 to 7% of the view blank at 12,000 and 24,000 dp/s, 0.0 to 0.3% with it). (3) When a lead's rows have retired the system allocator gives its freed pages back (`jni::purge`, 2 MB on crypto). Left: (k) **crypto and easy rest about 5 MB higher after a fling than before the lead** (crypto 130 to 137 MB): 2 to 3 MB of this library's heap (the probe, with no reader, shows 2 MB more after a fling with the same thirty rows mounted) and 4 to 5 of the reader's, neither freed memory an allocator holds (`mi_collect` and `M_PURGE` leave them) nor a collection that grew (the reader's rows, layers and tokens count the same). A led window mounts 70 crypto rows at its peak against 45, and what is sized by the most nodes alive (the kernel's arena, layout's slabs, the reader's) keeps that size. A window that trimmed the side behind while it leads (item h) would lower the peak by a viewport of rows. **Sixth pass** (2026-10-10, branch `android/led-low`; `lowab.sh`, `cfgtab.py`, `thrfull.py` in `~/bench/lists/exact-led`). Landed: the window leads in three tiers (`TIERS` in `canvas/travel.rs`): from 2,500 dp/s half a viewport and a pass each three quarters of one, from 5,000 one viewport and a pass each one and a quarter, from 8,000 two as before; the two slower only where a pass's slice holds four rows or more (heavy and xheavy gained nothing from them, xheavy 10% more cycles at 6,000). Exact thread at 3,000 and 6,000 dp/s: easy 86/134 to 67/92, crypto 134/213 to 100/152; half a viewport and one were the least that paid (a whole one at 3,000 and two at 6,000 gave no more). A window may lean (`CollectionFill::lean`, the wire's flag bit 3: half a viewport behind its travel, not one), and this host asks for it only with `EXACT_LEAN=1`: crypto then rests 136 MB after a fling, from 140 (129 before any lead), but a turn back at 24,000 dp/s shows one to four frames with 7 to 58% of its view blank, against two frames and 8 to 35% with a viewport behind (a quarter behind: 135 MB, four frames up to 40%; none: 134 MB, the whole view for two frames). After a turn from a led travel no pass waits and one follows every step until the window leads again (`Travel::turned`). A pass that waits under the two slower tiers tells the runner where the view is each quarter viewport of travel (`Runner::collection_shown`, no report; `Travel::shows`), which is as often as a pass ran before those tiers waited longer: the runner learns that a row shows from a report, and what waits for its row to show (crypto's chart drawing in) otherwise waited for the next pass, up to 21 steps at 6,000 dp/s (crypto's flat area there 54.0% on main, 56.0 without, 54.1 with). A report limited to no rows did the same and undid the waiting: it changed the list's snapshot, so each was a commit, a layout and a paint (easy 136 to 182 at 6,000 dp/s). Under the last tier a row waits for its pass as it has since that tier landed (each sixth or fifteenth step). Tried and dropped: building what a step brings into view inside the step after a turn (the report ran before the frame that lays the list out and built nothing; with it kept on until the lead resumed, no pass ran at all). Left: (l) **xheavy against Views** (the by-thread table, 3,000 and 12,000 dp/s, ms/s): the exact thread 227 and 405 is two thirds of the gap of 336 and 601; ExoPlayer, MediaCodec and CodecLooper are 97 and 164 more than Views' (the reader makes a player for every mounted video row; Views binds one as a row comes near: the reader's lazy views cover maps and web views, not video); the reader's main thread is level with Views' (146 against 133, 197 against 213), RenderThread 14 and 48 more, picture decode 33 against Views' 118 at 12,000. What the exact thread spends on an xheavy row has not been profiled since the row passes. Seen on the way, not this branch's: heavy's exact thread at 12,000 and 24,000 dp/s is 204 and 333 ms/s on 1110709a5 against 180 and 286 on 9daf3fb64 (one build each, same phone and session), and xheavy's 416 and 583 against 399 and 542.
- **Android CPU after `android/cpu3`: what is left on heavy and xheavy** (2026-10-05, Pixel 10 Pro XL, same-session runs against Views; the bench reader's changes are `~/bench/logs-cpu3/bench-cpu3.patch`). Found and fixed: MapLibre's "Worker" threads (~1,900 ms/s at 24k against Views' ~430) were the map module's `MapLibreMap.clear()` on every rebind, which makes MapLibre lay out every loaded tile again; removing its own markers instead brings them to Views' level (bench `NativeMap.kt`). An animated GPU canvas made a `VkImageView` and `VkFramebuffer` every frame (PowerVR sets up and tears down a render target for each: `vkmem_free` ~90 ms/s), staged its uniform through a transfer job, and present submitted a PRESENT→PRESENT barrier alone (wgpu-hal EXACT-PATCHES 6–7, `FrameUniform` on Vulkan). Results: xheavy fling CPU 1k/12k/24k 1526/2664/4134 → 1317/2145/2740 ms/s (Views 797/1524/2168), rest 1278 → 984 (Views 634). Left: (1) xheavy fps at 24k is ~2 lower (110 → 108; Views 78) and 12k ~1 lower: the main thread does the same Mcycles at ~2.4 GHz instead of ~2.7 now that MapLibre's waste no longer holds the cluster's clock up (DVFS); a per-frame cut on main/RenderThread (WebView construction, native-view recording) would buy it back. (2) The shader canvas still costs the exact thread ~130–170 ms/s at 120 Hz (one wgpu submit + present a frame through PowerVR's kernel driver, the acquire thread ~30, `vk_tlsem4_bg` ~18) and RenderThread the TextureView's layer update; Views draws it as AGSL inside HWUI's pass — drawing a GPU module's canvas in the reader's RenderThread pass is the remaining lever. (3) Dashed circles (the live row's rings) are drawn as arcs by the reader now (HWUI had stroked them in software every frame, hwuiTask ~70 ms/s); other dashed or concave paths still go through Skia's software path renderer. (4) heavy: pictures are resampled to the size they show at and shared across sizes, 12 viewports kept (decodes 330 → 184 a fling; CPU at 12k/24k 1217/1576 → 1103/1344 ms/s, Views 740/900; slow speeds unchanged, 1k 668 vs Views 570; end PSS by `Debug.MemoryInfo` ~715 → ~525 MB, Views ~485); what remains is per-row construction on the exact thread (taffy layout, paint, collection feedback, `sync_images_moved` ~12% at 24k), the picture hand-off (a `HardwareBuffer` made and copied into on the exact thread, ~12% of main+exact at 24k; decoding straight into one would drop the copy and the Rust-side duplicate), and Views' main thread doing its binding alone. (5) The bench's `pss_all_mb` (compact `dumpsys meminfo` after the run) is bimodal for heavy (~270 or ~600 MB on the same build, by whether the dump lands before HWUI's graphics release); compare `driver.json`'s `pssEndMB`.
- **Android memory on the Nothing (4a) Pro after `android/mem`** (2026-10-05, `dumpsys meminfo` App Summary at the end of a fling; tools and the bench-copy patch in `~/bench/android/results/mem-patch`). Crypto 97 vs Views 55 MB before: the library's resident pages ~17 MB (Views' code is shared and counts ~1 MB), native heap + mimalloc 36 vs 15, Java 8 vs 9–17, graphics equal. Landed: the Canvas host lets go of its library's read-only pages 2 s after boot (`android::release_library_pages`): .rodata 6.6 -> 0.7 MB resident (the baked market and coins.json are read once), CPU, fps and cold start unchanged. With the bench build's order below, medians of three on 268878a98: crypto fling 96.9 -> 90.1 MB (Views 55.6), daily 0.1–8.7 MB lower per scenario but still 2–13 over Views, xheavy within its ±20 MB noise and under Views (418–428 vs 456). In the bench build only (`order-clang`, `order.txt`): lld's `--symbol-ordering-file` puts the functions a run executes first, then boot-only ones, from an instrumented (`-Cprofile-generate`) build's counters (boot and run dumped apart) plus simpleperf samples for precompiled std and C; text resident after a fling 6.0 -> 3.7 MB on crypto, ~1 MB on daily, CPU unchanged. What is left: (1) the text that stays touched (~1 MB of the 3.7 sits in 64 KB fault-around windows around rarely run, uninstrumented code: drop glue, `core::fmt`, ring); the order file goes stale as code moves and is regenerated by hand. (2) The reader's dex: Media3 ExoPlayer is in every app's reader though only xheavy plays video; moving it to xheavy (a `VideoPlayer` the app gives, `bench-slim-reader-unshipped.patch`) took crypto's classes.dex 1,074 -> 108 KB and PSS -2 to -4 MB, but most installs of that build then ran the reader's `Layers.sample` -> `Player.replay` in ART's interpreter (nterp, `ResolveFieldWithAccessChecks`): main thread +55 ms/s; the cause (verification at runtime?) is not found, so it is not shipped. (3) Rust heap ~11 MB live on crypto (the baked market's values 4.7 MB: 48 numbers a coin at 16 bytes; layout leaves, realized rows, text caches) and mimalloc ~2.6 MB over scudo for the same blocks. (4) xheavy: exact2's decoded-picture cache (8 viewports, ~107 MB at 1260x2650) is most of its 133 MB of mimalloc pages, beside the reader's GPU copies of what is drawn; `imgvp=2` took PSS 449 -> 385 but decodes again on the way back. (5) Graphics is counted in whole swapchain buffers, ~7 MB each (1260x2800x4 shared with SurfaceFlinger), and an app holds 1–4 of them depending on how its first frames queued: daily's PSS moves by 7 MB between runs of one build, so its comparisons need several rounds (the warm Starred/Settings pages cost ~3 MB of heap; the rest of their 45 -> 61 MB was buffers).
- **Android steady-state CPU, what is left after `android/cpu`** (2026-10-05, merged as 98b178bd2; Pixel 10a: the exact thread −3 to −20%, fps unchanged, still more total CPU than Views at every app and speed). (1) Done on `android/cpu2` (the text cache walks only when a budget could be over). (2)–(4): see the `android/cpu3` entry above. (5) crypto at rest: the bench's source clones the 5,000-row list each 10 Hz tick (~26 ms/s) and `update_data` diffs it (~15 ms/s); a source that says which rows changed (as the iOS crypto note above asks) removes both. (6) Per-row construction: rows are now rebound, not built, in the Canvas host (LLP 1078, `android/reuse`: the `exact` thread −17 to −28% on easy at 6k–24k, −8 to −16% on crypto, heavy and xheavy within noise). What a rebound row still costs is its content: layout of its changed text, binding evaluation (crypto's chart `points`), recording it again, and for heavy/xheavy shaping and pictures; easy's process CPU ties Views at 24k and is ~8% over at 12k, crypto's ~60% over. Rows holding a refused leaf (canvas, map, video, web view, inner list) are still built; the Apple and web hosts do not opt in yet. (7) scudo was ~60 ms/s; mimalloc 2.x is now the default (2026-10-05, dd34e6948; `EXACT_MALLOC=system` to compare).
- **Android's Canvas host on an owner thread: what is owed** (2026-10-04, LLP 1072 on Android; the reader is `~/bench/lists/exact/android/canvas/.../Owner.kt`, outside the repo; `owner=0` is the old main-thread host). exact2 runs on an `exact` HandlerThread from boot (its looper takes executor and image fds; its Choreographer the turns); each op stream is copied and published, the main thread ingests and replays it, and a scroll translates the feed's group on main at once (the group holds every mounted row, so the mounted window is the coverage). Fling fps now ties or beats Views at every speed on all four lists on a Pixel 10 Pro XL, a Pixel 10a and a Nothing Phone (4a) Pro (xheavy 24k: 101–106 vs Views 79–83 on the Pixels, 74 vs 63 on the Nothing; owner=0 60–71). Owed: (1) CPU: more ms/s than owner=0 on light lists (easy +10–25%), fewer Mcycles on the Pixel, more on the Nothing; the exact thread is not boosted as main and RenderThread are (ADPF hint sessions measured no better). (2) Input: a press waits ≤5 ms and a key ≤2 ms for the answer within the frame; a 16 ms wait held frames (9–15 janky per daily scenario); a tab switch's ~14 ms of `pointer_up` arrives a frame or two late (39 vs Views' 16 ms). (3) WebView rows: `WebViewFunctor::drawVk` costs 2.3× Views' per draw (12.7 vs 5.5 ms) inside HWUI's `flush layers`, with `initVk` and shader compiles each draw (Views pays the init too). (4) Memory after a crypto fling on the Pixel: 319 vs 197 MB (graphics 200 vs 153, native heap 76 vs 32, Java 19 vs 6); recycling the stream copies did not move the Java heap. (5) Slicing a collection pass between frames on main (GapWorker-style, `refine(limit, velocity)`) painted every frame and cost fps; the limit plumbing stays for fills.
- **Daily's first detail open, after the press is laid out ahead** (2026-10-11, Pixel 10 Pro XL, twelve interleaved runs each; `Runner::foresee`, `presenter/ahead.rs`, the reader's `reader-late4.patch`). What a later open reuses is not a cache a static warm can fill: with the detail unmounted at every close (a local variant) a second open costs layout 1.2 ms against the first's 3.3, and with its body text made different at every open 2.8, so the 2 ms is shaping these strings, and no font, style or shaper state. Shaped at the touch down (3 slices, 2.8-4.1 ms in all, done 6-8 ms after the down; 0.05 ms for a press that mounts nothing) the first open is 20.8 -> 15.6 ms (Views 13.4): layout 3.0 -> 0.7 ms, answer ready 8.7 -> 6.2, ingest 3.5 -> 2.7. What is left over a later open (11.3) is the mount itself: dispatch 1.9 against 0.6 ms (the detail's tree), ingest 2.7 against 0.8 (its rows recorded), the frame 2.6 against 1.8 and the RenderThread 4.2 against 3.2 (an icon's texture, new glyphs). Only a commit prepared at the press and adopted at the release moves those (not built: the runner would need to run a press against a checkpoint and keep its outcome, with every effect held back). The first sheet (15 ms against 10), dialog (14 against 10) and tab (12-13 against 10) mount nothing at the press and are as they were: their extra is the first paint of rows mounted earlier. A one-time run of the reader's layer and bounds paths at idle (1.9 ms) moved nothing and is not in the patch.
- **Daily's first detail open: cold state ruled out** (2026-10-11, Pixel 10 Pro XL, six runs each way). Not page faults: with the 2 s release of the library's pages off (a local `EXACT_RELEASE_PAGES=0` switch, not kept) the first open's stages are the same (dispatch 2.1 -> 2.1 ms, layout 3.0 -> 2.8, answer ready 8.9 -> 8.7, latency 18.8 -> 19.6). Not the clock or the core: the exact thread runs the first open on a mid core at 1785-3052 MHz, later opens at 1785 after a ramp. It is the new screen's own work: mounting its tree, measuring its eight paragraphs, recording their rows; in all ~19 ms of CPU across the exact thread (7.5), the main thread (ingest 4.4, its frame 2.5) and the RenderThread (4.6), against Views' ~8.7 in one frame, and two of exact's stages end before its frame can begin. Only doing the mount, layout and recording before the release closes it (at the press, 60 ms earlier: a speculative commit the runner can discard; not built). The v24 -> v25 nav reading on another phone (0 -> 3 janky, 11/14 -> 13/17 ms in two runs) did not reproduce here in six interleaved pairs: p50 11.65 vs 11.95 ms, p90 14.5 vs 14.95, janky 2 vs 1 in six runs; v25's nav creates no executor or decoder thread at all (none is asked for).
- **Daily's nav latency, by stage** (2026-10-11, Pixel 10 Pro XL, six runs each; `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-late-tools/navstages.py`, the reader's change `reader-late3.patch`). Every tap paid ~1.1 ms between the answer being ready and the main thread's ingest of it: the main thread, parked for the answer, was woken on another, idle core (a later open's answer was ready at 4.6 ms and drawn from 7.2 ms; Views' frame began at 4.9). The reader now keeps the waiter on its core for the wait (`Native.colocate` on itself, undone when the wait ends; the exact thread is answering on that core): ingest ends ~1 ms sooner, nav 11.5/13.4 -> 10.9/12.7 ms (Views 11.6/13.2), tabs 11.2/15.0 -> 10.1/12.7 (Views 13.6/18.0), no engine change. The first open is unchanged (22 ms, Views 13.5): once-only work in three places, against a later open: dispatch 2.0 vs 0.6 ms (the detail's tree mounted), layout 3.2 vs 1.1 (its eight paragraphs measured), ingest 4.4 vs 1.0 (their rows recorded), so the answer is ready at 9.0 ms and ingested by 13.8, two vsyncs after the tap; the RenderThread draw is 4.5 ms against Views' 5.4, with no pipeline compile. Memory on nav stays +2.0 MB over Views (settings +2.8, tabs +0.7 in the bench's PSS): the link order is current and covers the daily taps (2,701 functions, made 2026-10-07 from all seven scenarios), mimalloc purges at once, and Skia's 4 MB "Other" GPU cache entry comes with reader-played layers (gone with `lower=0`) without moving `GL mtrack`; unbounded layer nodes (`layerbounds=0`) read 1.2 MB less GPU memory and damage more.
- **Daily's memory against Views, and the Java garbage in a fling** (2026-10-10/11, Pixel 10 Pro XL; `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-late-tools/`: `reader-late2.patch`, `driver-alloc.patch`, `memdump.sh`/`memtab.py`). The Java heap's swing in a fling (easy 110 or 126 MB) was the bench driver's, in every stack: `File.readText()` for /proc/self/statm every fourth frame and for two files a thread at each segment boundary, ~45 MB of garbage a run (easy 63 -> 18 MB allocated a run with both read into a kept buffer; heavy 69 -> 22; crypto 111 -> 66). The reader's own: its stream copies are 2.6 MB a run; on crypto a boxed `Float?` a track a frame, `(Int) -> T` lambdas and `HashMap<Int, _>` row maps were another 17 MB (66 -> 49, fixed in the reader patch). PSS at the sample still moves by whether ART's last collection came before it (crypto 130 -> 146 MB with less garbage: two collections, not three): the harness should collect before it samples. Daily after a scenario, `dumpsys meminfo` medians of 3, exact minus Views: nav +3.5 MB, settings +0.8, tabs -0.6, idle -1.2. What exact holds that Views does not: its library's pages from the APK (+4.6 to +5.4 MB after taps, +0.8 at idle: Views' engine is the shared framework's), the Rust heap under `Unknown` (+4.7 to +5.1, against 4-6 less `Native Heap`), thread stacks (+0.33), the fonts mapped a second time (+0.3), and on nav GPU memory (+1.9; Skia's text-blob cache is at its 4 MB cap after the detail, 2.4 for Views); less Java heap (-1.5 to -2.2) and dex (-2.3). Not closed: nav and settings stay over Views. Nav's p90 over six runs each: 13.9 ms [12.4-16.6] against 12.7 [12.4-14.5]: the first open (19-24 ms, Views 11-15) is always the slowest tap, and the p90 is the next slowest open (13-17 ms).
- **Idle workers on Android** (2026-10-10, Pixel 10 Pro XL, four interleaved runs, patched driver). The executor's seven `exact-io` workers were woken after every commit (`Core::forget` ended in `notify_all`) and now are not, and start with the first request; the two picture decoders start with the first picture (f71ed611d). Process CPU, before -> after: easy fling 758 -> 742 ms/s (646 -> 639 Mcycles/s), crypto 1030 -> 992 (1392 -> 1369), daily typing 214 -> 208 (270 -> 257), heavy 876 -> 862 (905 -> 896), xheavy 1566 -> 1560; daily idle 114 -> 115, all of it the bench driver's own frame callback on the main thread (exact's share there was the decoders' ~1 ms/s, now 0). Cold first content unchanged (n=8: easy 53 -> 52 ms, heavy 77 -> 78, daily 46 -> 46), heavy's first picture at ~92 ms either way. Left alone: `exact-log` (blocked in a pipe read, 0.1 ms a run) and the reader's `exact warm` (made at the first press's warm-up, ~1 ms a run); once started, a decoder still wakes on its 200 ms backstop. Easy's PSS reads 110 or 126 MB by build, four runs of four each way: the Java heap (1.3 or 13.5 MB of stream copies not yet collected when the dump is taken), native, graphics and code the same. Crypto's steady late gaps: 3 in 16 runs (Views 1 in 16): one a 16 ms RenderThread draw with every frame around it already late-presented, two with every app frame on time and SurfaceFlinger latching a period late (tracing daemons running); none the exact thread or the reader.
- **Android list flings, like for like** (2026-10-10, Pixel 10 Pro XL; `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-late-tools/driver-book.patch` and `driver-book-views.patch`, the same change to each stack's bench driver: a segment's per-thread CPU snapshot is read on a thread of its own between segments, and the next segment begins eight frames on). With it in both stacks, four interleaved runs a list, janky / late-gap counts a run, exact vs Views: easy 0 / 0-1 vs 0 / 0-1; heavy 0 / 0-1 vs 1-3 / 1-2; crypto 0 / 0-1 vs 0 / 0; xheavy 4-7 / 4-6 vs 61-75 / 63-68. What the counts were made of before: the snapshot in the frame callback (the entry below); a buffer of the ended segment latched just inside the next one; the two periods the first frame after any pause takes to be shown (both stacks: a frame that draws nothing empties the queue HWUI otherwise keeps one buffer ahead in); and the one paint exact2 owes ~16 ms after a scroll stops, shown during the pause. None is a frame late in motion. CPU read in ms/s is 2-5% higher for exact with the patched driver and equal or lower in cycles (easy 669 -> 643 Mcycles/s, heavy 939 -> 906, crypto 1395 -> 1420, xheavy 2559 -> 2546): the main thread does the same work at a lower clock without the old driver's 20 ms burst at every boundary. Owed: crypto's two steady gaps in four runs (14-20 ms, not looked at); exact's own threads: `exact-io` is seven threads that each wake ~1 ms/s in a list that fetches nothing, `exact-png` two that decode nothing on easy and crypto, `exact-log` and the reader's `exact warm` idle: eleven of exact's thirteen own threads could start at first use.
- **Android list flings, janky and late frames against Views** (2026-10-10, Pixel 10 Pro XL, four runs a list and stack; the classifier is `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-late-tools/janky.py`, the reader and driver patches beside it). On easy, heavy and crypto every janky frame of exact's (6-8 a run, Views 0-4) is the last frame of a segment: the bench driver reads two /proc files a thread in its frame callback there, 18-23 ms of main thread for exact's ~35 threads against 13-15 for Views' ~27, so SurfaceFlinger's deadline (~16.6 ms) falls between them; outside those frames exact has none (Views heavy ~1 a run). With the snapshots read off the main thread (`driver-book.patch`): exact 0 janky on all three. xheavy's own: the main thread waited inside its ingest for the exact thread whenever a buffered GPU canvas's row was freed (`Exact.detachBuffers` was a `sync`: 10-24 ms behind a collection pass, the next frame that late); not waited for now (the reader, `reader-late.patch`): steady janky 6.2 -> 3.5 a run, steady late gaps 7.5 -> 3.5 (Views ~62 and ~63), blank area unchanged. Owed: what is left on xheavy is the WebView rows' functor draw on the RenderThread (9-13 ms in `flush layers`); and with the driver patched, easy and heavy show 2-3 latch gaps of two periods a run at the first frame after each change of direction, not janky by SurfaceFlinger (the frame is drawn in ~4 ms and latched a vsync later; Views' gap at the same place straddles the segment marker and is not counted): not explained.
- **Daily's first detail open, the engine options measured** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL, simpleperf over the first open only, four runs; nothing built). The answer must gain ~3 ms to make the vsync. (a) Laying out and painting only what the viewport shows: ~70% of the detail's body is in view at once (36 lines of 24 in ~700 of ~1,010 points), so the bound is ~30% of layout and paint, ~1.3 ms on the exact thread. (b) Sharing text work between layout and paint: there is none to share, paint reads the paragraphs layout made (`paragraph_for` 0.3% of the frame); text measure is ~9% of the touch, shaping ~6%. (c) Mounting at the press was not attempted: it needs the runner to run an action on a state it can throw away. The touch itself has no one cost: the action and the update ~21%, the commit's layout ~15%, `after_commit` ~18% (collections ~8%, requests ~7%), hit testing, allocation ~9%. Note for whoever returns to this: the detail slides in from x = W, so its first frame shows nothing of it in any pipeline; a response split into "the layer, empty" then "its content" would read ~10 ms on the bench and show the content no sooner than now (the content's frame still begins at ~+15 ms), so it was not built. The reader's half of the fourth pass's colour path is now tied with the app's cross-fade (a recoloured text layer recorded from its kept runs, not by walking its ops: tabs 304 vs 299 ms/s, medians of four).
- **Daily on Android, fourth pass: colour transitions and the first detail open on Parley** (2026-10-07, Pixel 10 Pro XL; the reader's half is `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-lat4/tools-lat/reader-lat4.patch` against the Parley-ready copy). Landed: a `color` transition of a text without inline runs, and a `background-color` transition of a box's own flat background, are played by the Canvas host's reader (`lower.rs` `LOWER_COLOR`; `EXACT_LOWER_COLOR=0` to compare): the engine presents the target, the node is a layer, and its tracks are the colour's straight channels along the engine's own interpolation in eight linear pieces; the reader records that one layer again in each frame's colour. Daily's tabs with the app's plain `color` transition: 444 -> 319 ms/s (the app's opacity cross-fade: 304; pixels at rest the same). Owed there: the 5% over the cross-fade is the layer recorded again each frame (main thread +16 ms/s for two labels: the glyph arrays and font looked up per record; RenderThread +10%); keyframe animations of colours are still sampled by the engine. The first detail open on Parley, 17-22 ms against ~10 for any other tap: on the exact thread dispatch (the action and the mount) 2.0 ms, layout 3.0 (text measure ~60% of it, shaping ~1 ms: shaping ahead would not make the vsync), paint 1.6; the answer is there at +7.5-8.5 ms, 1-3 ms after the vsync; ingest 2.4-4.7 ms (the eight paragraphs' rows 0.7 of it); the frame then 5-8 ms, with no pipeline compile and a glyph upload of 0.4 ms. Tried: the next frame's callbacks wait for the late answer and that frame draws it (first open 17.4-18.8 -> 15.1-16.8 ms, but 3-5 janky frames a run where there were none): not kept. What would close it is not doing the mount and layout inside the tap (a mounted, hidden detail, as Starred and Settings are; or layout skipped for a subtree translated out of view).
- **Daily on Android, third pass: the Pixel 10a** (2026-10-07; `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-lat/tools-lat/reader-lat3.patch` against the reader at 12b07f4, with the daily app's contract). (1) Tabs' CPU (492 vs Views 446 ms/s) was not the phone's: on every phone the tab labels' `color` transition is painted each of its 250 ms' frames (300 paints a run: the exact thread ~105 ms/s, the main thread recording the view again, the RenderThread the whole window); the 10a only showed it because Views is cheap there (446 against 530-550 on the Pro XLs). The app's labels now cross-fade by opacity, which the reader plays (the plain label stepped out once covered; at rest the pixels are the same): tabs 494 -> 286 ms/s on the 10a, 467 -> 294 on a Pro XL. Lowering `color` and `background-color` transitions in the engine (a layer recorded again with the sampled colour, as a dash phase is) would do this for every app. (2) Nav's p90 on the 10a is ordinary taps a frame late, not only the first open: the main thread wakes for the release on a little core (820 MHz), where `colocate` declines, so the exact thread starts 1.3-2.9 ms late on an idle mid core at 357 MHz, and the workload hint reaches the governor 1.4-4.3 ms on (0.5 ms on a Pro XL). The reader keeps the main thread to the fast cores while a press is held (`android_hint::fast_thread`): late taps 14 of 66 -> 7 of 66, nav 11.8/16.7 -> 10.7/15.7 ms (Views 11.5/13.3), tabs 15.5/18.0 -> 11.0/15.8. Not kept: the hint again at each warm poke (no gain); the threads' own `uclamp.min` through `sched_setattr` (refused to an app). Owed: the hint's latency on this phone (the rest of the late taps), and the first detail open (exact thread 7 ms, ingest ending at +18 ms): shaping its paragraphs ahead would save ~4 of ~12 ms, not enough alone to make the first vsync.
- **Daily on Android, second pass** (2026-10-06, Pixel 10 Pro XL; all of it is the reader's and the bench driver's, `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-lat/tools-lat/reader-lat2.patch` against the reader at aaf2f06 and `daily-driver.patch`). (1) Tabs' p90 was the first screen with a blurred rounded rect: a blur mask filter makes its mask on the GPU (offscreen blur passes, three `FillRectOp` and a `CircularRRectOp` pipeline, compiled after an install). The reader now makes each shadow's mask on the CPU once per shape (an A8 bitmap, drawn as nine pieces, the middle stretched; within 5/255 of the GPU blur): tabs 10.6/20.5 -> 10.2/12.1 ms (Views 13.4/18.1). (2) A fade exact2 began after a late frame's vsync (the main thread waited across it for the answer) was sampled at that vsync, opacity 0, so HWUI drew nothing and the dialog's first frame came a vsync later; such a frame samples at the present time: dialog p90 13.3 -> 10.6. (3) The driver dispatched keys inside its Choreographer callback, so a key's own frame began before it and input-to-frame read ~90 ms for Views and for exact's fast keys; keys are now posted after the frame, in all three stacks: type exact 13.6/15.3, Views 14.1/15.6, Compose 13.6/14.8, and CPU 204 vs Views 205 (it read 183 vs 161 before, when Views drew a key in the frame already under way). Owed: the first detail open (dispatch 1.8 + layout 4.3 + paint 1.6 ms on the exact thread at 1.8 GHz, then 2.1 ms of ingest: 17-23 ms; it sets nav's p90 only on a slower phone); a late-answer hint and `notifyWorkloadSpike` did not help (spike: nav 11.3/13.5 -> 15.0/18.2). Typing's exact thread (~33 ms/s) is 44% paint: the textarea shaped again a key (cosmic-text's `shape-run-cache` took shaping 8.8 -> 6.4% of the thread, not kept), every icon's SVG scene resolved again a paint (~9% of paint, `computed_style` clones), mimalloc's purge at delay 0 (~6%); and each key damages the whole window (RenderThread 3.7 vs Views' 3.1 ms a key).
- **Daily (the mail app) on Android's owner thread: what is owed** (2026-10-05, `android/damage`, Nothing Phone (4a) Pro; the reader's half is a bench patch, `~/bench/lists/exact-wt-damage/tools-dmg/reader-damage.patch`). Landed: a touch down that changes nothing is no frame (`canvas/stream.rs`: a stream whose drawing is the reader's and that defines nothing); a press wakes the GPU offscreen (the reader's `Warm`, a 1x1 renderer of its own), so a release's response no longer pays 7-10 ms of cold GPU; the reader retains the view's drawing when a stream's top level is the last one's (kept rows recorded again into their predecessor's node, changed groups only) and bounds each animated layer's node (clipped), so a toggle damages its row, not the window; `scroll > column > rows` keeps the column's children as rows. Janky frames, exact vs Views, 3 runs interleaved: tabs 7/6, nav 2/19, sheet 3/12, settings 14/3, dialog 3/13, type 71/99 (before: 9/6, 12/21, 12/12, 10/2, 12/8, 66/102). Owed: (1) settings: what is left is SurfaceFlinger's, not the app's: a first frame after ~200 ms idle presents a vsync late ("Display HAL", then "Unknown Jank" and "SurfaceFlinger Scheduling"), 1-5 such per run (each three janky frames) against Views' 0-1; not damage (retain=0 the same), not the release's wait (wait=0 the same), not a window frame at the press (worse). (2) Tabs p90 latency (44-61 vs Views 42) is the first visit of Starred (the runner mounts the list, 17-21 ms in `exact touch`), and in about half the runs with the press warm-up one frame after it takes 30-35 ms of GPU (never with warm=0); unexplained. (3) Colour transitions (`background-color`, `color`) are still painted every frame: the tab labels' 250 ms redraw the window (the top level changes); lowering them, or a top level split into nodes, would make those frames partial. (4) The type scenario's ~95 ms for Views is the metric's (the driver dispatches keys inside a Choreographer callback).
- **Glass morph, parked** (2026-10-02, Charlie: revisit after native buttons; [LLP 1053.000.000.001](llp/1053.000.000.001-glass-morph.rfc.md)). A ghost glass on the motion engine's clock, springing from a leaving glass's box to an entering one's within a glass group (SwiftUI's `glassEffectID`). Its status line lists what the two reviews require: glass separated from content on both platforms, per-commit trees in the Rust host's commit loop, timestamped curve starts, presented geometry, source isolation, cancellation shared by both hosts.
- **Native buttons, what is owed** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1069.011](llp/1069.011-native-buttons.rfc.md) §7; its contexts and row reuse are [LLP 1069.011.000](llp/1069.011.000-native-buttons-everywhere.rfc.md)). Raster images and subtitles in a face. A real-touch drive of Messages' native swipe actions on a simulator (a swipe that reveals UIKit's actions, then a tap on each; a swipe and a scroll starting on a native body button), owed by D10: at landing the Mac's screen was locked and the agent's held contact reached no window; XCTests cover the actions' faces, colours, presses and the body button's touch cancel.
  2026-10-22 (lane q1022/c3, 37aaf8ba86, 4aaf727046, 682dc471df): iOS body buttons paint raster faces through the native button pipeline, decoded at the face's displayed size, and swipe actions have real-touch drives; the compiler refuses a raster `image` on a native button until every graphical host paints it (the lowerer cannot tell iOS from the Mac and Linux), so the iOS path is unreachable by authors until the Mac and Linux paint `face.image`. Bar and menu projections remain.
- **Contract after the apps, what is left** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1035.005.000](llp/1035.005.000-contract-after-the-apps.rfc.md) §3 "As built"). Stages 1–3 landed (`then` on the JavaScript target's clock, RealWorld's redirects as `then`, `contract` blocks refused, Caltrain's formatters as its own `fn`s, inferred `writes`, `provide` sections, records and action `let`, Fieldnotes on records). Left: Messages adopts `then` for a new conversation (the chat-demo Messages, on main since 8c1a551be, took the D1 and D9 rewrites in the merge that brought it); D6's `asks`/`rows` and its exemplar fixes (Messages' per-frame `time` argument first); D10 after a recount; stage 4 (D7b, D4a, D11, D5), each with its consumer. Outside the repo, Interview, Weird Castle and the Bluesky copy need the D1 command and the D9 script (each in its commit's message) before they build against main; so does any Contract source written before today.
- **Conformance after the Messages migration and SF Symbols** (2026-10-02, found landing LLP 1035.005.000). Messages' `swiped` now agrees on all three (fix/health6: the boot's own scroll offsets fire no `scroll` on Linux, as on the page), and `messages --build --linux --strict` compared 4/4 on 2026-10-04 with no local AI service running. Left: `synthetic-symbols`' screenshots differed in 5.53% of pixels.
- **Canonical Messages chat-demo fidelity** (2026-09-30). `apps/messages` now ports Expo's `chat-demo`. Remaining source-measured differences are recorded in `apps/messages/README.md`: longest-line balloon fitting, independent-axis send deformation and tail retraction, UIKit's keyboard-overlapping Add popover, and long-history virtualization. A side-by-side Expo recording and physical keyboard/gesture drive are still needed to establish pixel and motion parity.
- **Glass groups, what is owed** (2026-10-02, [LLP 1053.000.000](llp/1053.000.000-glass-groups.rfc.md) §5, built on `lane/glass-group`). (1) The press: interactive glass swelling across the spacing into a neighbour (Messages' inbox bar is grouped at `glassGroup=8` for it), seen on a phone or with the driver's held contact, which moves the Mac's pointer. (2) From James's prototype: `auto` spacing from the row's `gap`, and a morph when a member is replaced. (3) Whether a `style` class may carry host-policy props (`backgroundMaterial`, `glassGroup`): today "a style holds style rows only", where James's style-row spelling could be put in a class. (4) A UIKit glass made another way (a stock glass button) must register with `GlassGroups` to be isolated.
- **A folded text's element is still styled: two more styled elements a grid row than Solid's** (2026-10-02, LLP 1007.001 §5). A lone plain text is its box's content now (`display: contents`, the box a block), so the grid's layout tree is Solid's size (create 10,000: 220,022 against 220,033 layout nodes), but its element stays and is style-recalculated: 170,003 against 150,003 elements. Dropping the element too means the JS runtime writes a folded text's string into its parent, the agent's tree reads the node from the plan rather than an element, and the region anchors around it move; measure what the 20,000 recalculations cost first (create 10,000, mobile: style 354 against Solid's 289 ms).
- **A GPU canvas's drawables after its layer dies** (2026-09-30, the rest lane). The Extra Heavy shader rows' purgeable GPU memory grew with the rows passed because Core Animation lets a dead `CAMetalLayer`'s drawables go late: an iPad's 19-kind fling made 37–38 layers with at most two or three alive, and the purgeable ledger held 100–121 MB at the peak. Pooling the layers (LLP 1068 §0.4: 12 made, three spares kept) took the peak 322 → 288 MB (medians of three rounds; SwiftUI's 319 is the bar). What is left: the spares' count against rows turning over faster than the pool refills (six spares measured next), and a canvas that leaves the screen still holds its layer and its three drawables until its row is retired — a target lent only while on screen (`Module::attach`/`detach`, kept on `perf/canvas-pool`) would take the shader-only feed's 12–14 alive layers down to the four on screen.
- **The crypto list at rest, after the app's fix** (2026-09-30, iPhone 13 Pro Max, `~/bench/cryptobench/results/rest-trace/iphone-{svgi,uikit}`). The SVG port formatted every changed coin's 48-point `points` string in its data source each tick, off-screen coins included: 54 of 117 ms/s at rest (`Coin::value` -> `points`, Rust float formatting). SwiftUI and UIKit build a row's path when it shows, so this was the app, not exact2. `exact-svgi` keeps `series: list<number>` and maps it in the view (LLP 1017.003): rest 92 -> 65 ms/s over three rounds (UIKit 22), footprint 27.2 -> 23.5 MB (UIKit 29.4). The traced 63 ms/s that remains (UIKit 27) breaks down as: the port's source 7 (cloning the 5,000-row list and formatting the price); per-tick work proportional to the list, about 10 (the runner's `update_data` 3.7, dropping the replaced rows 3.0, the clone, `compare::same`); layout 3; commit 4; the host's apply and collection flush about 16 (`Frames.tick` outside the runner); UIKit and Core Animation 18. A source that could say which rows changed would take away the per-list part.
- **Signposts cost nothing measurable unless something records them** (2026-09-30). An `OSSignposter` begin/end pair costs about 0.4 us with nothing recording, well under 1 ms/s at our rates. Under Time Profiler, whose kdebug session makes each emit expensive, exact2's own intervals (`scrolled`, `pump-*`, `apply`, `text-raster-*`) cost 17-29 ms/s on the iPhone. So traces overstate exact2 by that much against SwiftUI and UIKit; subtract it when comparing. Not gated. The iPhone 13 Pro Max also logs CFPrefs lookups at debug level (about 20-50 ms/s of `os_log` in every app's traces, SwiftUI's and UIKit's included), which is where the "48 ms/s of logging on main" came from. Safe-area and layout-margin updates cost 4-5 ms/s in exact2 against 3-4 in SwiftUI and UIKit: not worth a change.
- **Agent drives, what is left** (2026-09-30, [LLP 1012.001.000](llp/1012.001.000-trustworthy-agent-drives.rfc.md)): stages 1 and the ruled parts of 2 landed (stale builds refused, film, 420×900 everywhere, source maps on every host, the glue tests in the async lane). Left: D1b the web build marker records its inputs, so the web's staleness warning can refuse; D7 one grammar table for the driver's CLI; D8's rows as authored + computed under the runner's names, `font_family` by name, the page's view numbering and rounding; D10 LLP 1012's crash log to `issues/`.
- **SpringBoard can lose the first turn of the iPhone Duo after it folds closed** (2026-10-19, Xcode 27.1, iOS 27.1 24A94425). `smoke.mjs duo`'s rotate leg lost its first turn after closing the Duo in 3 of 21 runs beside another simulator's drives: no app turned, though CoreDevice reported the device turned. SpringBoard sets its fold transition style to `.fade` as the Duo closes and back once its display swap ends, by itself or with the first pose that reaches it then; under load it sometimes waits for that pose, which sets the style back with no orientation update. A plain UIKit app turned in 102 of 102 turns within a second of closing, which missed the window. The leg now spends that pose on an upside-down turn made as SpringBoard's log shows the swap end (LLP 1078, "As built (rotation)"). Look again on a later release.
  2026-10-23 (lane q1022/ab): no iPhone Duo simulator exists on this Mac (`xcrun simctl list devices available | grep -i duo` is empty); not attempted.
  2026-10-24: `--only rotate,resize` again lost the scene's first turn; the existing hinge retry recovered it.
- **An absolutely positioned child of a covered route resolves its percentages against the whole box** (2026-10-20, the Duo simulator, iOS 27.1). Since 4df50fcaa9 a tab's route under UIKit's bars and `viewport-fit="cover"` ends the home indicator's inset below the tab bar's top (902 of 951 points turned, the bar at 868), and the kernel moves a child's length insets past that cover (`cover.rs`), so `bottom: 0` ends with the route, while `100%` in its `max-height` or `height` is still the whole 951. duo-lab's note sheet, capped at `calc(100% - env(viewport-segment-top 0 1))` on a laptop fold, came out 49 points too tall and put its drag handle on the band, until its screen hid the tab bar. On the web the route itself ends above the tablist, so both agree there; resolving the child's percentages against the box inside the cover too (LLP 1123 D3's inner box) would match it.
- **On the web at 900px and wider, a fold's panes miss their segments by the sidebar's width** (2026-10-20, Chrome headless under `prefer segments 2x1 gap 40`, duo-lab at 1000×700). The web's sidebar takes the tablist to the left at 240px, and the routes start beside it, so duo-lab's detail pane, placed at `env(viewport-segment-left 1 0)` (520) inside a route, sits at 760 and runs off the window's right edge. At 852 wide the tablist is a bottom bar and the panes sit on the segments. Segment positions in the route's own coordinates (LLP 1123's deferred owner-local segments) would fix it.
- **The agent's accessibility export omits Duo's native tab-rail buttons** (2026-10-24, iOS 27.1). An actual XCTest snapshot exposes a Toolbar containing `tab-fold`, `tab-images` and the other tabs, with no Tab Bar accessibility group. The in-process view walk prunes their empty native ancestor. Plain unclipped empty containers still exclude their children in XCTest, so relaxing empty-bounds pruning globally is incorrect. The walk reports incomplete coverage when it omits an otherwise accessible native bar. Exporting that missing projection remains open: public accessibility-container accessors declare no children on the empty wrapper, and walking the public `UITabBar` as another root produces a group without the real buttons. Read the native accessibility projection before claiming its elements or absence. Evidence: `/tmp/duo-reliability/ax-native-snapshots.json`, `/tmp/duo-reliability/public-ax-containers.json` and `/tmp/duo-reliability/public-ax-bar-root.json`.

After the second pop, the router selected Sheet but the expected one-controller sheet stack did not settle, and `nativeBack` remained non-null. The observation can describe either the sheet or background stack, so this does not establish which controller remained active. Animated pops and real UIKit Back taps pass the same route sequence. - **A Duo display swap can collapse native content allocation** (2026-10-25, Xcode 27.1, iOS 27.1 24A94425). In Duo Lab, after a cold handoff from the cover display to the inner display, UIKit's tab bar can fill its 951×669 container, with its content layout guide at zero height. The selected `HeldStack` and `RouteController` then report a 669-point bottom safe area; the window and the tab controller's view report 34, and no controller adds insets. The collapse persists across captures, appears before a debugger attaches, and reproduces with a reduced plan that has no resources, lists or native hooks. - **A minimal iOS embed weighs 4.1–4.7 MB with every compiler lever pulled, not 1** (2026-09-30, measured: a one-text-node plan over `exact_apple::host!` with an empty data source, linked into a UIKit `main.swift` against a 73 KB UIKit floor, arm64 iPhone, `-dead_strip`, `strip`; harness and link maps in `~/bench/embedsize`, outside the repo). Today's shapes: the production profile (`release`: opt-level 3, thin LTO; `swift build -c release`) is 7.23 MB over the floor; a real app's `apple-dev` archive (Type Tour's) 8.45, of which wasmi 1.0 MB, linked into every app through `exact_logic::configured!` for the swappable data module (LLP 1029.000) whether or not the app swaps one, the app's data and plan 0.7, the kernel 1.33 unoptimized for size. The ladder on the probe: opt-level z + thin LTO 5.66 → fat LTO 5.42 → Swift `-Osize` 4.92 → `-Xfrontend -internalize-at-link -enforce-exclusivity=unchecked` 4.65 (the export penalty vanishes with it: ordinary exports cost 1.2 MB before, 5 KB after) → nightly `-Zbuild-std=std,panic_abort` with `optimize_for_size` 4.34 → `-Cpanic=immediate-abort` 4.22 (the backtrace machinery was 161 KB) → `-Zlocation-detail=none -Zfmt-debug=none` 4.21 → `-Xfrontend -disable-reflection-metadata` 4.09 (not free: `String(describing:)` of an enum then prints its type name, `State`, not its case, `ready`; the sample hosts and the agent print states that way). opt-level s in place of z costs +0.8–0.9 MB. What the 4.09 holds: ExactKit 1.98 MB (core presenter files ~0.8; Canvas, SVG, GPU, video, web, native modules, pickers, collections, navigation, menus, modals, swipe ~0.7; generic specializations and metadata ~0.2), Rust 1.75 (taffy 222 KB of which grid 161; runner 221; kernel 220; exact_apple 145; core, alloc and std 280; motion 30; markdown 28; plan 28; textflow and unicode-linebreak 41; url, idna and ibex2 46; route 11; raster and canvas 14), linker-synthesized stubs and unwind 0.14. The stable flags-only build (fat LTO, z, `-Osize -internalize-at-link`) boots and paints on the iPhone 17 Pro simulator; the nightly one is checked below it. Why 1 MB is out of reach without a different design: the feature code is linked by dispatch, not by use (every op handler is reachable from `Presenter.apply` and `NodeView`; every kernel row's grammar from the plan decoder), so removing it means separate artifacts a consumer links (CLAUDE.md's rule), and the core beneath — Swift ~1.0 MB, Rust ~1.3 — is still 2–3× the budget. Owed: whether `-Osize` and opt-level z cost frames on the iPad benchmarks, before either becomes the iOS production profile. Linking by use on Apple, Swift included, is LLP 1047.001 (its §6 is the order of work).
- **Considered and deferred: an edge fires only over a measured band** (2026-09-29, LLP 1010 §6.5, the lead's ruling). `reachstart`/`reachend` fire by the estimate today: a report whose window reaches the edge row by estimated heights dispatches, and only re-arming needs the overscan band measured. Requiring the band measured to fire too would stop an under-estimate from paging at load. The feed benchmark's case is closed without it (a realistic estimate plus `initial-item-count`). It costs rewriting about 20 edge tests (`contract/cli/tests/it/collection_edges.rs`, `collection_edge_async.rs`), which send estimate-only reports, and the same change in `list.js`.
- **Render host CPU a page against Solid and Svelte** (2026-09-29, the SSR-cost lane, LLP 1048.000 D10). RealWorld's `/` is ~4.3 ms of CPU a page on this Mac under load (Solid ~0.8), and the grid benchmark's 1,000-row `/` ~125 ms (Solid 24 on the same load), after this lane's commits. Measured by A/B, not by profile alone: (1) Apple's transport was ~30% of it; the render host now uses rustls on macOS too (Charlie, 2026-09-29; LLP 1048.000 D10), −17–18%. (2) The fresh Hermes realm is ~14% (`EXACT_RENDER_REALMS=warm`'s saving): Runtime creation (its internal bytecode, the global object) and teardown; this Hermes has no heap snapshot, and a realm's module state lives in closures no reflection reaches, so a proven reset needs an engine snapshot or a bake that proves a module keeps no mutable state. (3) The kernel's apply and the projection are gone for pages the plan allows (LLP 1048.004 stage 1, 2026-09-30: RealWorld −18%, grid −49%); the rest is the render itself: the tree's realization and bindings (the plan VM), the kernel's apply (style validation, inheritance, the testId index), the projection and its CSS, JSON across the Hermes boundary. Reaching Solid's cost needs a render that writes the document from the plan and the settled state without the kernel (precompiled static fragments, as Solid and Svelte do), plus (1) and (2). At concurrency 64 the server's defaults (`--renders 4 --queue 32`) answer most requests 503 at once, so a CPU-per-page number there counts cheap refusals unless statuses are checked.

The rows LLP 1077 added (`box-shadow` lists, conic gradients and layers, `mask-image`, `text-shadow`, `corner-shape`, `-webkit-text-stroke`, `rotate`'s axis, `-exact-symbol-palette`) parse through generated style code every core reaches, so an app that uses none of them carries all of their grammars. Link them by use as `gradients` (2edce3faf) and `grid` (e5d8707d, dcbfa8c0) are: the parser reached on wasm only through a registered link, set by a `host/web-capabilities` capability when the plan binds the row, a plan that binds it unlinked refused at boot. - **Standing rule: a new optional web capability links by use** (2026-09-28, Charlie's ruling (a) on the web cores' growth). A feature an app may not use — a device request, a host command, a style or prop family — reaches the web core only through what the generated entry links (`Linked` / `HostLinks` / `RunnerLinks` / `DeviceLinks`), detected at build from the plan (`exact_runner::uses`: its rows, tags and `Command`s) or, for a data module's requests, from the bake's grant ceiling; a plan that uses it unlinked is refused at boot by name. The gate (since 2026-10-02, Charlie: gate what ships) is `metrics.mjs --long`'s app.js budgets, the JS target each app ships (Caltrain 19, RealWorld 30, video-player 9 KiB brotli-11); the wasm cores (what games, conformance and fixtures build) keep reported reference numbers (310 / 304 / 249 KiB) that print "over" and are not filed. Smaller is still better for both, so a lane measures its app cores and app.js before and after and says the difference in its commit. Precedents: Canvas 2D stage 2, `format`, `materials`, `backdrop`, `auth`, `share`, `documents`, `picker`, `text_transform`, `effects` (filter, clip-path), `animations`, `gradients`. Numbers in core crates read and print through `exact-num`, never `str::parse::<f64>` or `{}` of a float: either one brings back core's tables (~12 KiB and ~4.5 KiB).
- **Canvas 2D on the GPU owes** (2026-09-29, LLP 1056 §8.5, `land/canvas-gpu`). (1) Memory: F2 full screen ended 19 MB (iPhone) and 4–7 MB (iPad) over SwiftUI's at the landing tip (24 and 32 MB on the bake branches), within one full-screen IOSurface, the second a canvas drawn off the main thread keeps while Core Animation shows the first; a smaller-than-device backing for a canvas that animates, or one surface when the lists always cover, would close it. (2) vello's main thread was 172–191 ms/s against Skia's 118–148: traced (2026-09-30, iPhone, the same app with each module swapped in; `~/bench/canvasbake/swap`, `exp`, `ab`). It is not main's own work (main's CPU 29 against 25 ms/s) but its wait for the runtime owner's canvas draw each frame (`Runtime.advance` → `Owner.sync`): the same draw costs 2.9 ms under vello and 1.75 under Skia, slower evenly in every function, both on the performance cores, and 2.4 ms with no replay at all, so the performance cluster runs slower when vello leaves the CPU idle while the GPU works (Skia's replay burns ~5 ms of CPU a frame). Cut on `perf/canvas-gpu-main`: main's font scan reads record headers (main CPU 29 → 22 ms/s), and the recorder keeps parsed palette colours and the reply finds text by headers (owner −6 to −9 ms/s, main busy 173 → 167). The lever left is the next line. (3) The module is in every Apple app's bundle (3.98 MB on iOS), as the SVG island module is; embed it only for a plan with a 2D surface. (4) It needs Xcode's Metal toolchain to build; a Mac without it builds apps that draw animating canvases with Core Animation's recording (§8.4), which then remains only as that fallback: delete it when every build machine has the toolchain. (5) A canvas is given to the GPU at its fresh bitmap's first lists; one that starts animating later stays on Core Graphics until its next fresh bitmap (a state prologue from the tracker would move it). (6) Text on the GPU is filled glyph outlines (no colour glyphs); Skia drew glyph masks. (7) The same module could replay Linux's canvases (kernels to SPIR-V at build time), so Apple and Linux would match each other.

- **CSS `filter` on a box, Linux and what Apple still owes** (2026-09-29, LLP 1055.000 §0 "CSS `filter` on a box"). Linux draws a filtered box or image unfiltered; `fx-box-filter` and `fx-image-filter` are declared unsupported there (`scripts/svgparity.mjs`). Linux can reuse its island painter (`paint/island.rs`) over the box's subtree. On Apple the picture is redrawn after each batch, so what changes a box without a batch (an image decode landing, a text raster painted later, a video frame) shows at the next batch; a `url()` filter on a box is not taken; the picture is drawn on the main thread at the box's full region (the live SVG path's off-main drawing would suit an animated one). On macOS the picture is `CALayer.render(in:)` of the box, which ignores `maskedCorners`: a filtered box with some corners rounded and others square (a layer-property box, `BoxLayerMac.swift`) is pictured with all its corners rounded; one radius on every corner is exact.

- **SVG feed memory on the iPhone** (2026-09-29, BENCH_VM peaks on the Extra Heavy SVG-only feed, two rounds each): the 43 → 55 MB rise after the gradient raster cache (bbfe3b46, 11e1feaa) does not reproduce: the cache-only build 42.4–45.2 MB, cache and strips 44.8, main 40.2–45.9, SwiftUI 34.8–34.9. The cache is small (the CGImage and CG data tags under 3.3 MB). - **What F1 and F3 still cost against SwiftUI** (2026-09-29, M1 iPad Pro, LLP 1055.000 §0 "F1 and F3, the second round"). F3: the app's CPU 413 ms/s (120) and peak memory 145 MB (24), at 120 fps and main thread 73 (84). SwiftUI's filters run in the render server; ours render the sub-scene with `CARenderer` in process (its CPU scan converter fills the shapes, ~50 ms/s), submit two command buffers a frame (~95 ms/s in the driver) and keep the target, two working textures and four surfaces (~80 MB of GPU memory). A picture drawn only when something in it moves (the orbit's rate, not the display's) or a render-server path (a `CAFilter`-free chain in a `CALayer` the server composites) would close it. F1: launch lifetime peak 121 MB (99–116) and steady 88 (49), measured while the vignette was a mask island (23 MB); it is a gradient layer now (first ink below), memory not re-measured.
- **F3's first ink after an install** (2026-09-30, iPhone 13 Pro Max, LLP 1055.000 §0 "First ink"). With the kernels compiled at build, the first launch after an install draws F3 at 280–348 ms against 124–133 warm (SwiftUI 61–68): the GPU compiler's work on each pipeline and Metal Performance Shaders' blur, inside the feature's commit. A `MTLBinaryArchive` harvested per GPU family (or pipelines made on the boot prewarm and the first picture drawn a frame later, against D1's never-without-the-effect rule) would take it out. Warm, F3's commit is ~20 ms on the phone's main thread: the GPU wait (6–7 ms; the first picture is drawn in its commit), the sub-scene's CPU gradient (3–4) and `CARenderer`'s make (2.5–3).

- **The expression VM costs about 6 ns an instruction** (2026-09-27, feat/contract-listmap). A crypto row's `points` is about 60 instructions and four string allocations a point: 23 us for 48 points, five times Rust's `format!`. Each instruction is decoded from bytes through the operand table (`vm::decode`), and a template's `+` allocates per part. A pre-decoded instruction array per code body, or an n-ary concatenation for templates, are the levers; measure on the crypto fling (row mounts) before building either.

Record whether builders hit either, and whether a `"asc" | "desc"` value for the direction is worth its rule.
