What is left is wgpu-core's bookkeeping for a pass and a submit, on the main thread, and a starved canvas's two passes a frame — D8 (the module on a thread of its own) is the design for the main thread; process CPU stays about 1.4× UIKit's for one canvas at 120 Hz with wgpu-core encoding the pass. - **Upstream Naga's unnecessary f16 uniform-storage capability** (2026-10-17, `vendor/wgpu-hal/EXACT-PATCHES.md`, patch 11). Exact's Android-only instance opt-in and uniform rejection keep Adreno usable in the meantime.

- **What is left of the heavy list's off-main CPU and late frames on iOS** (2026-09-26, perf/offmain, M1 iPad Pro Time Profiler and System Trace). Off-main is image decode (about two thirds at 6k pt/s and up) and worker text; inside a decode, ImageIO's own thumbnail (hardware JPEG decode, then a high-quality resample from full size) is nearly all of it, so what is left to cut is decode count, not decode cost. Four pieces: (1) a fifth of decodes still draw into a copy because the plan rounds a side up (`RasterDecodePlan.scaled`) where ImageIO rounds down, so the thumbnail is one pixel short and gets stretched; agreeing on rounding drops the draw but moves those images' pixels by that stretch. (2) Core Animation no longer copies image or text layer contents at commit (perf/memory: every raster is Core Graphics' own image, BGRA, 64-byte rows; a device Allocations trace shows no `create_image_by_copying` left). (3) The text-pixel cache (`TextRasterizer.kept`) is iOS only; the AppKit rasterizer has the same repeated labels. (4) With off-main load lower, the main thread at ±12k/24k pt/s spends more CPU-ms for the same rows (more samples on E cores, fewer P-core cycles per ms); total CPU still falls. The one late frame left per ladder run at the +12k start is the probe's segment checkpoint (6-12 ms of JSON written on main in `-[LBProbe emit:]`) plus rows the next frame needs; the harness could write it off main.

- **`adopt` covers the compiled value only** (2026-09-26, perf/adopt, LLP 1027 D11). A value carried across a reload, a kept store-reader answer or a document's seed is not adopted, and the TypeScript executor and a worker-placed source ignore it (their values cross as copies). A source that edits what it answers still rebuilds its data after a dev reload (the heavy-list port parses its JSON on first edit then). Extend it when a consumer shows the cost. The heavy-list port also still bundles its 7 MB messages.json into the device binary for the bake's sake; untouched, those pages are clean and not charged to the footprint.
- **Weird Castle does not build against exact2 main** (2026-09-26, found checking perf/adopt against it). `weird-castle-data` matches `Outcome` without `Storage` and `Surface` (data/src/lib.rs, `parse_login` and `parse_switch`), and `weird-castle`'s src/lib.rs calls `Runner::boot` with 3 arguments (it takes 5 now: the viewport and the launch URL). Neither is from `adopt`; with the two matches given a fallback in a scratch copy, the data crate checks clean.
- **What is left of a slow heavy-list scroll on iOS** (2026-09-26, perf/slow-frame, Time Profiler on the M1 iPad Pro trace and the iPad Pro 13" simulator). At 1k pt/s, after the focus-search, text-scan, timer and travel-report fixes, the main thread is mostly building rows: `Host::commit` layout and `create_row` in Rust, `Presenter.apply` in Swift, and the CA commit. Three pieces are separable. An ellipsized paragraph (`text-overflow`) never rasters on a worker, so each new row's author name is shaped and drawn on main in `NodeView.draw` (`paragraphLayout`, ~2% of main on the iPad); a worker raster that truncates would take it off. Every commit re-serializes every mounted row's snapshot (`collections_json`) and Swift re-parses it (`applySnapshots`, `CollectionSnapshot.init`), ~3%, including the measurement-only report that follows each built row. And each report still carries every mounted row's height (`CollectionHost.height` walks each row's ancestors for `hidden`), which the runner now ignores when unchanged but the host still gathers and encodes.

- **A hardware keyboard on a headless simulator** (2026-09-26). This Xcode has no Simulator.app, so a simulator has no hardware keyboard and UIKit's focus system never starts. One HID keyboard event from SimulatorKit's `SimDeviceLegacyHIDClient` (`IndigoHIDMessageForKeyboardArbitrary`) attaches one for the rest of the boot, which is how perf/slow-frame reproduced the iPad's focus-search cost; the keys themselves did not reach the app, so a real Tab walk still needs a device or Simulator.app.

- **What is left of a heavy-list live tick** (2026-09-26, perf/live-ticks, `sample` on the iOS simulator in live mode). Keying and type-checking now follow what changed (the runner's share fell from about 17 to 1.3 ms a tick), and the timer callback is about 2.4 ms: the runner about 1 ms, of which `SumTree::new` reallocating and zeroing its 2×16,384-slot buffers on every splice is about 0.2 ms (reuse them when the base size holds, keeping the splice transactional); the host's commit and layout about 0.3 ms; and the Swift side (`ExactSession.apply`, `Batch.decode`) about 0.8 ms. A same-length reorder still keys, by position, every row between the moved ones.

- **iOS boxes on the pixel grid, what is owed** (2026-10-17, `host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/PixelGridIOS.swift`). Every node view and flat leaf is drawn with its absolute edges rounded half up to device pixels, as Chromium's `PixelSnappedIntRect` rounds them; text wraps and native editors and collections measure at layout's size (`layoutBounds`). Typetour's first screen at 3x has 20% fewer horizontal edges blended across two pixel rows (21,118 to 16,943); Shelf's first screen was on the grid already. Owed: (1) offsets UIKit's own views take, a grouped list's `UICollectionView` and a dialog sheet's scroller, and Exact's other programmatic writers (scroll anchoring, reading-position restore, keyboard avoidance, collection anchoring and its animated offsets), which can leave content a fraction of a pixel off; `writeScrollPosition` and content sizes are on it; (2) border widths: Chromium snaps a computed border width (under one device pixel to one, else down to whole pixels), which belongs in the kernel for every host; (3) what blurs inside a box: a paragraph's lines sit at fractional baselines; (4) a carried grouped-list row keeps its children's places from its authored parent, which can differ from Chromium's by a pixel; (5) the Mac draws where layout falls, as iOS did.

- **`smoke.mjs web --app <name>` fails for an app with a data module** (2026-09-26, found running Messages for LLP 1047.000). The generic host fixtures reload a bare plan into the app's `EXACT_WEB_LINK=all` build, and `exact.reload` refuses with "module reload requires a paired generation" when the build carries a module. So Messages, Fieldnotes and the other TypeScript apps pass only `--app-only`. The fixtures could run on a build without the app's module, or the smoke could default to `--app-only` for such apps.

- **What LLP 1074 left** (2026-09-30, lane/taffy-css). (3) An exclusion (`wrap-flow: both`) under a static parent is placed against a box further up and wraps nothing; the compiler could say so (`wrap-flow: both` needs a positioned parent) where today the text is only refused auto height. (4) Apps outside the repo (Weird Castle, Interview) are not swept for `position: static`: the parent of each absolute box, and each box with an inset and no position, needs `position: relative`. (5) The one deviation left, that a clipping box is a containing block, goes only when native hosts paint by stacking context and re-parent a positioned view to its containing block (LLP 1074 §7); `position: fixed` waits on the same. (6) Patches 10, 11, 12, 14, 17, 18 and 20 are marked to upstream and none has been sent.

- **The rest of LLP 1050.000 stage 1** (2026-09-25, perf/fill-hosts). iOS, macOS and web collections now report `velocity` and `limit` and fill in slices; a web authored jump builds its rows before it moves (stage 2), and macOS clamps `prepareContent(in:)` to the built rows (D5). Owed: D5 with real trackpad input (responsive scrolling takes window-server events, which `tap … wheel … fling` now posts, LLP 1142 D3; checked so far only by XCTest); a macOS authored `scrollTop` still reports on the next main-queue turn, not in its batch (whether AppKit can commit a frame between is unmeasured); the anchor rule while moving; pending intervals in the reply; and the cost memo (D3). Linux stays unlimited: it has no fling and its wheel steps are small. Give it a limit when it gains a fling (LLP 1015).

- **A list benchmark row is now mostly the runner and the batch** (2026-09-25, perf/rowcost, simulator Time Profiler over the fill slices; it was mostly UIKit). The iOS presenter's share of a built listbench row fell from about 1.5 to 0.75 ms (no window layout engine, no scroll view per closed swipe row, parked row views reused by shape: `NodePoolIOS.swift`). What is left per row: the runner's commit and a Taffy layout from the root on every report (about 0.7 ms, the perf/layout lane), `Batch.decode`'s JSON (about 0.25 ms), `CollectionSnapshot` parsing the whole snapshot each batch (about 0.12 ms, `Collection.swift`), and `UIImageView` resolving a symbol it is handed anew (about 0.08 ms). Layout from the collection, a binary batch, or a snapshot that sends only what changed would each go further. View reuse is iOS-only; macOS and web could follow the same contract.

- **The gallery's dismiss drag leaves the viewer open** (2026-09-28, LLP 1057.003 phase 1). At Fit a far or fast release sends the photo off on its spring and the backdrop fades to 0 with it, but the viewer stays, empty, until Back. Closing it when the exit ends needs an end-of-motion event (CSS's `transitionend`) or an action that can call the `command` prop; Contract has neither. C6's thrown card waits for "Deal again" for the same reason.
- **`timeline-scope: all` has no browser oracle** (2026-09-28, LLP 1057.003 phase 3). Chrome 154 drops the declaration (`CSS.supports('timeline-scope', 'all')` is false), so the kernel and the glue follow the specification's text, and the web's own CSS carries no `all`: phase 4's scroll timelines, which the browser resolves, would not see one. Recheck Chrome (and Safari) before phase 4, or refuse `all` until one parses it. The lookup's other 23 cases are Chrome's answers recorded in `kernel/tests/it/timeline_scope.rs` from a scratch page; `parity.html` rendering those trees with scroll timelines would make them a fixture.
- **A drag timeline's source eased by a CSS transition is sought per frame on the web** (2026-09-28, LLP 1057.003 phase 2). A spring release hands each consumer a follower animation and runs nothing per frame; a `translate` transition with a CSS easing (`0.4s ease-out`) is the browser's, so `motion-glue.js` seeks the consumers in each frame it runs (18–26 callbacks a release in the gallery). Composing it too means sampling the easing (a `KeyframeEffect` with no target answers its progress at any time) into the same `linear()` points; steps() would need its jumps.

- **A drop whose move arrives after the return spring still scrolls the List to follow the row** (2026-10-23, the reorder-anchor lane, abd0ae25ea). A committed drop now anchors the first visible row other than the dropped one, but only while the preview lives: `finish_preview` (runner/src/instance/collection/reorder.rs) and `finishPreview` (host/web-js/reorder.js) drop the preview, and its exclusion with it, so an ungrouped drop whose data write lands after the return spring ends (a slow mutation) anchors the moved row again and the List jumps by its travel. Both reviewers of that lane read it in the code; not reproduced in a test. Keeping the exclusion until the move shows, or until a bounded wait like a grouped hold's, would close it.

- **A served RealWorld article is only partly adopted** (2026-09-24, the activation lane, LLP 1048/1047). At d73af7b0, booting a served `/article/…` page keeps 82 of the document's 113 elements; the rest are rebuilt. It happens on the tip, not only in the split prototype. Adoption should keep every element when the canonical documents match. Find what diverges (the Markdown body's projection is a likely suspect) and whether the digest or the element matching is wrong.

- **Presize the transaction's per-commit sets from the batch** (2026-09-24, the size lane): `txn::apply`'s `created` set, `Staged`'s maps (`created`, `parents`, `children`, the root sets) and `Detach`'s `seen` start empty on every commit and grow as its ops arrive, about seven doublings each for a batch that moves 220 children. Under a compact id map that growth cost 55–70% more cycles on a native churn bench (500 commits, each creating 20 rows, moving 220 and destroying 20: 203M → 313–348M cycles, for 1% more instructions), which is why hashbrown stays (LLP 1047 §10). hashbrown pays for the same growth, less. Sizing each set from the batch's op count before its first insert would take that growth off the commit path, on native and the web.

- **What RealWorld found in Contract** (2026-09-23, `apps/realworld`): (1) ~~Nothing runs when a mutation answers~~: `mutation … then action` (LLP 1016.001, 2026-09-26). Mutations, resources and tasks live only in the root, so a page's form state that touches data moves up.
- **Surrogate keys that name only what shaped a page** (2026-09-23, render lane): the server keys a page by every source its render asked (LLP 1048.000 D11), so a source whose anonymous answer never changes (Interview's `loadDraft`, `accounts`) puts every page in its purge. Naming only the answers the document read needs the projection to report the resources it read; the runner doesn't track that.
- **A published web root doesn't serve its documents past `/`** (2026-09-23, render lane; a JS build's tree takes the hosting rule since LLP 1153 D3, `buildTreeFile`): `readWebRequest` (a published root's `plan.published`, and a wasm build) still answers any app path with `/index.html`, which a `render=build` home page replaces, so a published root serves a client route, or an unknown one, `/`'s page with 200; and a wasm build's `listPublicFiles` does not inventory `404.html`, `shell.html`, `sitemap.xml`, `robots.txt` or `<location>/index.html`. A 2026-10-24 lane (branch `q24/serve-pages`, parked after three fix rounds) found what that touches: (1) admit a `<location>/index.html` only when its checkpoint location matches its path and a `render=build` route, else an arbitrary directory (`posts/private/` under `/posts/:slug`) becomes public; (2) `host/web/tests/navigation.mjs` injects its fixture only into `index.html`, so shell and 404 need it too; (3) a published nested document keeps `href="./exact.json"`: relocate it in `webRelease` (scripts/deploy.mjs) before the cards, not when serving, which bumps the release id and so needs `classify` to compare the desired id or old origins stay `current`. What stopped it: telling an app document from an authenticated `.html` asset in `webRelease` (a prefix list misses a rendered `/plans/pro`; main rewrites any `.html` with `<base href="/">`, which already breaks an HTML asset's envelope card): use membership in `exact.json`'s assets instead.
- **The web core's rich style codecs, linked by use** (2026-09-27, [LLP 1047.000 §9](llp/1047.000-staged-loading-by-capability.rfc.md)). RealWorld's core grew 70.7 KB in a day (238.9 → 309.6 KB brotli); Canvas 2D is now linked by use (`c1663d51`, −10.3 KB). What remains is style rows whose `CssValue` codecs parse CSS text (paint, marker, dash array, paint order, transform and its origin, filter, clip path, shape outside, aspect ratio, background image): the generated `StyleProps` code reaches every codec, so every app carries every parser. A throwaway build stubbing every `CssValue` parse and format call bounds the saving at **10.9 KB** (299,236 → 288,375 B), so the type-erased storage Charlie agreed to (rich rows behind a trait, parsers linked by use; `kernel/build/codec.rs`'s one template plus ~45 consumer sites across the SVG and paint lanes) waits until those lanes next reshape `StyleProps`. Also left: Canvas 2D's TypeScript draw seam and per-batch web turn (~3.5 KB), which need a registration in the generated entry. `metrics.mjs --long` reports each tracked app's core against a reference size (`WEB_CORE_KIB`), not gated since 852101fa73.
- **`app.wasm` across deploys, what is left** (2026-09-26, `6e0b8874`; [LLP 1047.000 §9](llp/1047.000-staged-loading-by-capability.rfc.md)). A returning reader now gets the next build as a delta (9.5 KB against 238.6 after an app edit) when the render server runs with `--generations`, which only the bench passes: hosting should. A page left open across a deploy still loads the new build and can skew against its checkpoint; the server keeps the earlier builds now, so it could serve each at its own `?v=` (LLP 1048.000 D10's "kept for pages still open"). The stage files are named by content but go `no-cache`; `immutable` would drop their revalidation.
- **GPU modules, what D6 left** (2026-09-23, split lane; [LLP 1009 D6](llp/1009-gpu-canvas.rfc.md)): natively a module gets no runtime shader assets (the ABI names no registry's shaders; the web asks `gpu_shader_names`), so a module compiles the WGSL it carries; natively every artifact links its own wgpu, naga and Metal backend (Weird Castle: 3.34 + 3.92 MB stripped against 4.06 MB for one module), which a shared wgpu library would remove; a destroyed device with two artifacts live has been reasoned and unit-tested, not driven.

- **Contract compiler follow-ups** (2026-09-22, branch `compiler/expansion`): Lowering canonicalizes and opens a file on every compile (6–14% of lowering, `lower_with_sites`); the resident compiler could keep it. Unoptimized `infer` takes ~10 KB of stack per level, which is what holds an expression to 100 deep (release has room for ten times as much); splitting its arms would raise the bound. `change=add` for a parameterless `add` reports only the unrelated `setDraft`'s uninferable parameter, since analysis's handler arity needs types first. Weird Castle does not compile at `c3b585bf` (`app.contract:289`: `iframe` has no attribute `postMessage`).

- **Native Markdown undo boundaries** (2026-09-22; [LLP 1045 D5](llp/1045-markdown-editor.rfc.md)): UIKit must retain its specialized per-editor undo manager; source undo/redo across natural run-loop turns and independent histories are verified with it. Still owed on native: a source replacement and formatting command in one automatic event group can undo together, skipping the intermediate source. Three bounded automatic-group repairs did not establish a safe boundary; the final attempt was reverted after scheduled run-loop closure exceptions on both platforms. Preserve native grouping semantics; the one-command-one-undo requirement remains incomplete. Physical phone input and intrinsic editor sizing remain unproven.

- **Markdown editor** (Charlie, 2026-09-21; [LLP 1045](llp/1045-markdown-editor.rfc.md)): source-backed editing now has shared Rust formatting/selection APIs, TextKit 2 adapters on iOS/macOS, and a dependency-free `contentEditable` web editor whose rules are `exact-markdown-editor`, an on-demand wasm (Charlie, 2026-09-22: CodeMirror removed). The stress app has a formatting toolbar and link field. Interview's separate consumer branch adds composition, source-preserving publication and durable account/prompt drafts. Native syntax stays visible at full advance but for link destinations, which Apple hides by `exact-markdown-editor`'s rules (LLP 1045 D5 r6, 2026-10-20); the web hides all of it (WYSIWYG). The DEFERRED take is approved. - **`press` on a container holding its own input** (2026-09-23, buttons lane): Messages' two emoji-picker columns (`apps/messages/app.contract` `column press=showEmojiPicker`, lines 817 and 837) put `press` on a container that holds the picker's full-size transparent `input`, so neither can be a `button` (an input inside a `<button>` is interactive content in interactive content). Once they are restructured — the input could take the tap itself — `press` can belong to `button` and `link` alone and be a compile error elsewhere, where today it makes a `<div>` with a click listener and no keyboard access.

Owed: a swipe row (the Signal chat list's) never parks in the iOS node pool, menu or not (its live scroll view), and that list is not a virtualized `list` anyway; macOS's `MenuHost.context` and right-mouse path have no test (`popUp` is modal); the compiler does not refuse a literal `contextPopover` naming no popover, or two `contextPreview` rows (iOS logs both, macOS the first, the web neither); no interactive content inside a preview (UIKit takes any touch on it as the commit), so Signal's reaction bar stays authored; after a bar item's menu, `ExactView`'s focus (key commands) comes back only at the next touch on the page, not when the menu ends (UIKit says nothing then), and any touch on such a bar blurs a focused node (UIKit exposes no bar item's view to tell a menu item's touch from another); Linux shows nothing; on the web a bound `contextPopover` that the node's own action changes opens the old popover when the commit is deferred (a view transition on the JS target, presence loading on the wasm host): resolve it after the applied commit, not in a timeout.

The launched app gets its storage; the agent's should get the same.

Still per-answer: the web's wasm module realm (`host/web/module-glue.js` runs one answer at a time, so the wait would block the reply it waits for; the JS target, the web build, runs real promises) and a worker-placed source (its owner runs one turn to its end).
- **Bounded application work: plan r3, reviewed by two families** (Charlie, 2026-09-20): [LLP 1027.003.000](llp/1027.003.000-bounded-application-work-claude.plan.md) (Claude) and [LLP 1027.003.001](llp/1027.003.001-bounded-application-work-astra.plan.md) (Astra max) answer LLP 1027.003 §11–§13 independently. They agree: Fieldnotes first (a legal 1,000-note notebook cannot be opened), then Messages changed-record writes, then native filling; no heap, deltas or new apparatus. r2 §9 recommended 1027.003.001 as the spine with four amendments; r3 is the synthesis, reviewed by Astra max and Grok 4.6 xhigh (both MATERIAL FINDINGS, six defects raised by both families, dispositions in `llp/reviews/`) — this document's summary-only Fieldnotes answer with no pager, its sibling's chunked exact search with no stored folded column, the three-second idle sync tick as its own first Messages change, and hoisting `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` out of the per-operation path. 1027.003.001 §8 found what both repairs cannot fix: browser SQLite exports the whole database after every non-query op (`host/web/storage-worker.js:113-123,189`) and every `storage-fs` operation `getAll()`s every file with contents (`host/web/storage-fs.js:166`). A contract compiler fix landed (a bool `derive` could not be a ternary condition); Fieldnotes now fetches short prefixes for unfiltered previews, with bounded 32-row full-body fallback, and loads only the selected editor body. Exact search still scans every body. TypeScript reads and Rust backups now check the stored schema instead of issuing no-op CREATE writes; browser whole-database export on actual writes remains open. Filesystem operations now fetch their required records or subtree keys; removal reads no deleted-file contents, and rename loads only the moved subtree and records it validates. Messages idle sync now skips full-history reads/comparisons unless reconciliation is owed; reset/adoption, apply and settlement failures retain the obligation until successful read/publication. Browser idle metadata commits now skip persistence when a bounded SQLite comparison finds every final value unchanged. Unchanged reply timer ticks now skip full-model snapshot/diff work too. Durable source footprints now avoid full-history snapshot/diff and held-map copies on local edits. Receipt transitions now visit only outgoing messages still awaiting Read. Sends and contact edits now preserve existing person positions and select only affected contacts. Recovery footprints now include only selected messages and expiry candidates; due expiry metadata scans and whole-database export for actual browser writes remain.
- **Action spelling diagnostics** (LLP 1006 §3): handlers, action props and timers now use authored scopes; forwarded prop/provider errors link their supplied argument. Action-valued derived expressions and other untraceable substitution origins conservatively get no hint. Extend only with original binding provenance, never expanded-root name guesses.
- **Messages remaining write costs** (LLP 1027.004 D5): durable source footprints now compare only their declared records, detaching changed payloads before storage awaits and updating held entries after admission. Sends/contact edits select affected people with stable positions; exact-ID contact lookups reuse that index. Sends retain only one pending reply for rollback. Receipt transitions are proportional to outgoing messages still awaiting Read. Recovery footprints now avoid unrelated archived payloads, including global expiry candidates and selected messages only. Forward idle reply ticks now use a conservative next-event time without scanning/copying pending schedules. Non-idle footprint selection traverses pending directly and creates entries only for due events. It also identifies receipt-only ticks and no-op rewinds that need no pending-map rollback copy. Reply removals retain only removed entries and their original positions; refusal rebuilds schedule order before model restore. Single blocking/deletion removals now retain one activity and its ordinal through a key-only `forEach` scan, rebuilding order only on refusal. Small message deletions now update surviving thread indexes in place, preserving receipt order; deleting at least half rebuilds the fewer surviving rows. Transcript/affected-reply/receipt filtering remains linear. Recovery operations skip expiry metadata scans before a conservative earliest deadline and skip future buckets when another bucket is due; restore rebuilds the bound. Recently Deleted summaries use each archive’s maintained minimum expiry instead of walking its messages; contact enumeration remains. Deletion appends to the owned archive without copying its existing rows; footprint selection still scans that conversation’s archive metadata. Inbox, recipient matches, and Recently Deleted now decorate/transfer/mount at most 200 people through Previous/More controls. Deleted selection and Recover All targets/count still span all archived contacts; scanning, the global target string, and selected-token removal strings remain unbounded. Name/address searches, ordinal scans on actual blocking/deletion, scans on due ticks/rewinds/stale early deadlines, and row metadata scans within due archive buckets remain bounded-work targets. Startup, changed sync and exceptional rollback still restore the full model. Snapback's 512-record cap refuses oversized bulk edits whole; retain that atomic refusal while improving the explanation and preflight.
- **Linux popover presentation and Messages reply input** (2026-09-20): closed popovers are hidden and inert, fixing the hidden `permanentlyDelete` interception of ordinary inbox taps, and presentation, placement and light dismissal are built (2026-10-26, LLP 1115.004 D10.7). The earlier reply drive loses its held contact, and the Linux carrier refuses context-menu input. The ordinary scroll-property path and the headless 215-send/earlier/later/latest drive are fixed. Evidence: `/tmp/exact-linux-popovers-306428c2/` and `/tmp/exact-scroll-commands-0a819c84/`.
- **Virtualize the Messages transcript** (LLP 1010 §6 consumer sweep; LLP 1027.004 D5): its horizontal timestamp-reveal wrapper is outside the collection's supported shape. Once virtualized, `reachstart`/`reachend` replace the explicit earlier/later rows with no source change.

What would make sense to do next, in rough order. This is not a spec and decides
nothing: `rules/RULES.md` §Scope says a spec without an implementer and a date isn't
written, so a line here is one to three lines — the thing, why, what it needs. When
someone picks one up it becomes an LLP with a name and a date (archive a link when
the working set is full); when it lands, delete the line. Anyone, agents
included, may add or remove a line. `rules/DEFERRED.md` still binds: an entry that
sits on that list carries the trade it would take.

## Next, in order (2026-08-29)

- **Game texture families in bundles and on the wire** (2026-09-23, textures lane; [LLP 1046.003](llp/1046.003-game-engine-as-built.explainer.md#compressed-textures-2026-09-23)): every baked texture ships as `x.tex` (RGBA8), `x.bc.tex` and `x.astc.tex`, and a device fetches one — on the web. Native bundles copy all three (the Fox's 5.6 MB becomes 8.4 MB); a macOS bundle needs BC plus the fallback, an iOS device ASTC plus the fallback, but the bake's compatibility inventory lists every asset, so thinning is a bake change, not a copy filter. Separately, `host/web/serve.mjs` sends `.tex`/`.model` as uncompressed `application/octet-stream`; RGBA8 payloads gzip 70× (the Fox's 5.6 MB → 80 KB) and blocks 1.2–17× — a compressible type for game assets would cut the fallback's download.

## Cheap, any time

- **Android's `linear()` evaluator still has the old edge rules** (2026-10-19, the `linear()` reviews). `piecewise` in `host/android/canvas/src/main/java/dev/exact/canvas/Layers.kt` holds the outer stops and takes the earlier stop at a shared input; the engine's (`motion/src/easing.rs`, `piecewise`) answers as Chrome 155 does: at a stop's exact input, the last stop there; before the first stop or past the last, the outer segment extrapolated; before a first pair that shares an input, the second's output. Two that differ today: `linear(0 25%, 1 25%, 1)` at 0.1 (Chrome 1, Android 0) and `linear(0 25%, 1 75%)` at 0 (Chrome −0.5, Android 0). Port the rule and check it on a phone or the JVM.

- **Compiler recovery and fresh-store drives: three review leftovers** (2026-10-18): (1) `contract/cli/src/sources/refused.rs:45` `hides()` matches a refused name anywhere in a diagnostic's message, so a broken `state number =` hides an unrelated `type-operand` that mentions `number`; restrict it to missing-symbol diagnostics about that declaration. (2) An incomplete derive whose write was in a refused action is still reported as `type-derive-cycle` (`contract/types/src/component.rs:235`); drop it with `type-cannot-infer`. (3) `scripts/verify.mjs:228`: a fresh-store refusal calls `stop()`, which kills a `server-*` a crashed drive left answering; on refusal drop only this drive's lease. 2026-10-23 attempt (450a, parked after three review rounds; branch q24/recovery-leftovers on that box only, not pushed): all three reproduced on 02a382460. (3) is one line: on the refusal call `off()` and `rmSync(mine)` instead of `stop()`. (2): `type-derive-cycle` must stay for a real cycle (`state broken =` beside `derive d = d` reports both), so classify at the cause: an incomplete derive not on a cycle of `derive_reads` is `type-cannot-infer`, an untyped `[]` keeps its own `type-cannot-infer` at that span first, and `derive_reads` must skip binders (arrow params, `match` binders, expression `let`, call heads) or `derive xs = [].map(xs => xs)` reads itself. (1): a whole-word match is what main relies on to hide many consequences, so a subject match must list them all: `type-unknown-name`, `type-unknown`, the three unknown-component ids, `type-unknown-function` (incl. "declare `routes` to use `<name>`" and the `empty()` idiom), `analyze-unknown-action`, `type-unknown-command`, `type-assign-not-state`, `type-send-not-mutation`, `type-refresh(es)-not-resource`, `type-pending/failed/failure-argument`, `type-now-renamed`, and `type-refused-idiom`. The last one is where it stopped: `idioms::refusal` gives `len`, `count` and `size` one message that names none of them, so matching a refused name by its refusal text hides an unrelated `count()` beside a refused `fn len`; those messages need to name their callee (the pinned `count(xs)` text in contract/cli/tests/it/lint.rs moves with them), or a message only one name produces may be matched. A general "first backquoted word is the subject" rule is wrong: `1 - true` quotes `number` first.
- **Evaluate a component's computed prop once per instance** (2026-10-18; worth ~2 points of euxy's playing CPU): a prop read several times in a stateless one-box component is re-evaluated at every read. A memo in the inliner was tried and removed (review: it hoisted reads under `&&`/`||`, `?:`, expression `match` arms and arrow bodies, so `range(-1)` ran where the program skipped it). Redo it with the guaranteed-evaluation rule `contract/syntax/src/inline/derives.rs` `summarize` already uses, never memoize handler arguments or clock-reading props, and mirror it in `semantics/Contract/Expand.lean`; `semantics/corpus/components/prop-guarded.contract` and `prop-event-time.contract` are the cases it must keep agreeing on.
- **A large reply's owed Hermes collection can wait forever** (2026-10-17; `js/src/prelude.js:1051,1187`, `js/src/lib.rs:863,1084,1128`): the full collection a promoted large string owes runs only when no other answer is pending, so a fetch that never settles keeps a dropped 376 KB QR buffer for the life of the process; and a reply whose serialization throws (capture limit, cycle) leaves its call in `calls` as `done`, blocking every later owed collection. Run the owed collection on the reply and re-stamp pending calls' epochs; `__exact_forget` a call whose settle fails.
- **Bold Text on Material popup typography** (2026-10-24; LLP 1115.000 D12.3): `host/linux/src/presenter/top/look.rs` builds dialog and menu paragraphs at fixed weights (400 for headline/body, 500 for labels), and its popup symbols stroke at a fixed regular weight (`(1.1 + 300.0 / 400.0)`, ~line 298). Confirmed in `paragraph`: it takes neither a node nor the preference; `Inputs` carries only scheme, font scale and window size. Carry the applicable Bold Text setting into surface layout and its cache inputs, and step these host-assigned weights once for measurement and paint.
- **Two exact-web-js paint tests fail on main on Linux** (2026-10-24, 450b, 5c786cfcf8): `cargo test -p exact-web-js --bins` fails `paint_tests::server_client_and_adopted_paint_agree_on_the_chrome_corpus` and `paint_tests::waiting_rows_recompute_parent_and_sibling_facts_against_kernel` (125 pass) at `host/web-js/src/paint_tests.rs:381`: the Bun fixture `host/web/tests/paint-order/compare.mjs` gets rolldown 1.2.9's `[INVALID_OPTION] Invalid value "iife" for option "output.format" - UMD and IIFE are not supported for code-splitting builds`. Find which bundle call in the fixture's path asks for IIFE with more than one entry or a dynamic import, and set `codeSplitting: false` or split it.
- **Two timing-budget tests fail on main on 450b** (2026-10-24, seen on 485521e590, each also alone): `cargo test -p contract --test it tamber_rows_check::a_worst_case_write_is_checked_within_the_budget` takes 13.4 ms against its 10 ms budget, and `cargo test -p exact-svg-file --lib tests::a_file_that_expands_past_a_limit_draws_nothing_and_names_it` takes 5.28 s to refuse one of its files against its 5 s bound (`host/svg-file/src/lib.rs:439`, a debug build). Both pass on other machines; find whether the budgets assume a faster box or a release build, and make them hold on a 12-thread Linux box.
- **The Apple host drops a store write that fails to persist** (2026-10-14): the runner keeps the value, but the host does not retry a failed write. Retain it for a later commit and report the failure once per name until it succeeds or is replaced. Setting an unchanged value is no write, so an identical `set` does not retry it.
- **Linux element reveals land at once** (2026-10-18; LLP 1070.000 §14, LLP 1115.000 D8.4; `host/linux/src/presenter/reveal.rs`): macOS and UIKit animate `scrollIntoView(id)` with omitted, `auto` or `smooth` behavior and hold the scroller's `scrollend` until the animation ends; the Linux host lands every element jump at once. Animate it there too, landing at once for `instant`, reduced motion and the frozen agent, a new request or `scrollTop` write replacing a running one.
- **scrollend gaps on the hosts that have it** (2026-10-13; LLP 1005):
  - Apple unbracketed moves use a 100 ms `DispatchWorkItem`; known instant commands and discrete wheel notches should complete without that quiet delay.
  - Apple visible layout clamps can arm an end through `scrollEndMoved`; classify clamps separately from scroll operations.
  - Apple hidden-scroller completion differs from Chrome: `take()` precedes `hasScrollLayoutBox`, so a hidden callback consumes an owed end, while hide/show before the callback can retain unfinished work; distinguish unfinished scrolling from an already queued end.
  - Apple settlement must include unfinished scrolling and queued completions: `Agent.nativeInFlight()` does not count `ScrollEnd.owed` or its quiet work item.
  - Web `clock settle` can return `settled: true` during smooth scrolling and before a `scrollend` handler's jump chain finishes, as measured by LLP 1005's runtime probes.
  - Verify web lifetime parity: `rt.js` suppresses `scrollend` for a disconnected element or a gone owner but still delivers `scroll`; the `glue.js` end listener has no equivalent guard.
  - Physical macOS momentum completion remains unqualified; verify one end after the coast with a real trackpad, as in the existing momentum entry below.
- **`scrollend` on the Linux and Android host: full timing and device qualification**
  (2026-10-15; [LLP 1005, Scroll completion](llp/1005-plan-and-runner-v1.spec.md#scroll-completion)):
  the Presenter now completes moving notches and instant jumps at its next delivery
  point (deferred notches share one completion after their frame's `scroll`),
  reader samples after 100 ms quiet, and owned edge scrolling when it stops.
  Lifetime cancellation and agent settle are covered; apps without handlers retain
  main's behavior. Declared gap: viewport resize can classify end-follow layout
  movement as a new scroll. A 100 px scroller with `scrollFollowEnd`, a child of
  height `100vh`, and a viewport growing from 500 to 600 px emits a completion.
  Remaining: the full deadline-before-later-timers rule and its
  snapshot/reentry budgets, plus Android device qualification. The first step keeps
  Host's catch-up and shell call order. The earlier full implementation remains
  parked on `q1013/scrollend-impl`; use the no-handler differential harness at
  `~/lanes/q1013/wt/scrolldiff` to guard any later timing work.
- **One presenter pass for the batches a frame lands together (iOS)** (2026-10-13, from the
  Bluesky clone's quotes fling): most fling frames apply four batches: the app's `scroll=` batch
  (the clone writes state every frame), one or two list fill slices that `landFill` lands inside
  it to keep the owner's order (T4), and a clock batch. Each that reaches `Presenter.apply`'s full
  pass (not a collections-only slice `applySnapshots` takes, nor a scroll batch `applyUnlessEmpty`
  skips) pays its fixed work whatever it holds: NavigationHost `batchApplied`/`prepare`, `onlyListRows`,
  `syncAccessibility`, `ControlHost.sync`, CollectionHost begin/end, about a quarter of the main
  thread's time in that fling (Time Profiler, 1136 samples). Merging the slices landed inside one
  apply into a single pass would pay it once. It touches the T4 ordering code, so parked until a
  device shows the cost.
- **Physical iPhone HDR qualification** (2026-10-13):
  On the iOS 27 simulator (iPhone 17 Pro), independent Apple API probes
  report `RGBA8Uint` for `.RGBA16Float` and `.gray8Uint` layer format hints,
  headroom 1 instead of 4 for gain-map HDR decodes, retained PQ/HLG for SDR
  decodes, and no ISO gain map when encoding the gain-map fixtures. The
  same requests work on macOS 27.0.1. Each declined assertion group records
  its fixture and observed result; storage, headroom and neutral-pixel
  comparisons still run. See [Apple's format hint](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/quartzcore/calayer/contentsformat)
  and [headroom semantics](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/coreimage/ciimage/contentheadroom).
  Physical iOS remains unqualified: run `HDRColorTests`, `HDRImageTests`,
  `PickerColorTests` and `RasterColorTests` on a connected iPhone and require
  no declined assertion groups, skips or expected failures. Their colour
  fixtures live in `host/apple/tests/ExactKitTests/ColorFixtures` as regular
  files, copied into SwiftPM resources and loaded through `Bundle.module`.
  From the repository root, build the device archive and unsigned test bundle
  (the generic iOS build passed without a phone):

  ```sh
  export PATH=$HOME/.bun/bin:$HOME/.local/bin:$HOME/.cargo/bin:/opt/homebrew/bin:$PATH
  export DEVELOPER_DIR=/Applications/Xcode.app/Contents/Developer
  SDKROOT="$(xcrun --sdk iphoneos --show-sdk-path)" \
  HOST_CXXFLAGS="-isysroot $(xcrun --sdk macosx --show-sdk-path)" \
  MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=14.0 IPHONEOS_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=17.0 \
  EXACT_UPDATE_TRUST=development HERMES_LEAN_SYS_OFFLINE=1 \
    cargo rustc --crate-type staticlib --profile host-dev -p caltrain-apple --lib --target aarch64-apple-ios
  export EXACT_TESTS=1 EXACT_LIB=caltrain_apple
  export EXACT_LIB_DIR="$PWD/target/aarch64-apple-ios/host-dev"
  cd host/apple
  xcodebuild build-for-testing -scheme Exact -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' \
    -derivedDataPath ../../target/ios-hdr2/device-tests \
    CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO LIBRARY_SEARCH_PATHS="$EXACT_LIB_DIR"
  ```

  `xcodebuild test` cannot run these on a phone: a package's tests are
  tool-hosted, and Xcode refuses that on a device ("Tool-hosted testing is
  unavailable on device destinations", tried 2026-10-13 on an iPhone 17 Pro
  Max). Qualifying them needs a host app for the test bundle (as
  `host/apple/touches.mjs` makes for the UI tests), or a minimal app that runs
  the same ImageIO, Core Image and `CALayer.contentsFormat` probes and is
  launched with `xcrun devicectl device process launch --console` on an
  unlocked phone; a person at the phone is needed either way.

## Declared gaps, by system (each spec's "Not in v1")

- **Kernel / layout** (LLP 1001, 1010): `position: fixed`; `text_align: start`;
  presentation transforms in scrollable overflow; `overscroll-behavior`; scroll
  snapping beyond the admitted horizontal subset; Linux scroll events.
- **Durable state** (LLP 1018 §5, built 2026-08-30 for Weird Castle's token): the
  `plain` tier — persisted slots, with Caltrain's "last station"; `me`; the iOS
  first-launch keychain wipe; the data-protection keychain on macOS (a bundle and a
  profile); the Linux file store when that host links ibex2.
- **Plan / runner** (LLP 1005 §8): per-instance derives or resources inside `each`.
- **Contract** (LLP 1006 §8): inlining budget; `contract` blocks as assertions;
  `cursor`; per-instance state; LSP.
- **Web host** (LLP 1007 §9): a text-measurement bridge (the kernel's layout does not
  run on the web — and only grid has a kernel-against-browser layout check,
  `host/web-js/conformance/grid.contract` with `--linux`; a general layout parity
  corpus would be the analog of `parity.mjs`); scroll position across a reload; a
  spring interrupted by an easing.
- **Apple host** (LLP 1008 §7): images; toggles; accessibility beyond `testId`,
  `accessibilityLabel` and macOS's button/link/image/heading roles (iOS has no
  image or header trait); justified text; rubber-banding on inner
  scroll nodes; a generated header. **iOS** (LLP 1008 §9): the agent API on a
  phone (`build.mjs --device --run` installs over USB or Wi-Fi, but nothing drives
  the app there); a
  synthesized touch for the agent's `tap` (UIKit's hit-test and the responder-chain
  rule today); a pan chaining out of a nested scroll view at its edge (UIKit's own;
  the agent's wheel chains).
- **The keyboard's blink on a field hand-off** (iOS): UIKit rebuilds the accessory bar
  when first responder moves between two `UITextField`s — one bar-less frame (308 →
  335), reproduced in a from-scratch two-field app with any traits and by re-traiting
  one field in place; a phone shows it faintly. Safari does not, so WebKit hides it
  somewhere below the responder (its `UITextInput`-conforming content view and its own
  input-assistant handling). Worth finding out how, if a form ever needs it gone
  (LLP 1008 §9).
- **Linux host** (LLP 1015 §7): evdev has carried no real event yet; `canvas`; libinput and
  xkbcommon (acceleration, touchpad gestures, hotplug, non-US keymaps); Wayland/X11
  windows; selection, IME, a caret blink; `text_decoration`, `font_family`, RTL;
  shadows, gradients, grid; JPEG, image URLs; accessibility; a font cache; text pixel
  fixtures against Chrome (the Linux tests pin a font, `host/linux/tests/pinned/`; Chrome draws the system's).
- **GPU** (LLP 1009 §4): readback bands for blending and MSAA; a generated module ABI
  once there is a second consumer; the `Custom(u16)` animatable-property extension
  when a surface needs it.

## Later

- **Shared elements, stage 2** (LLP 1013.000 §3; moved here 2026-10-19 as the take
  for LLP 1020 §11's iframe host updates, `rules/DEFERRED.md`; stage 1 built
  2026-10-04: the `sharedElement` attribute, live native flights on iOS/macOS, CSS
  View Transitions on the web's JS target, `apps/shared-elements`). Waits for a named
  consumer: velocity carried into a flight (a release's, an interrupted flight's: Q5),
  drag-to-dismiss handing a hold to the flight back, a name as the zoom's source
  (`navigationSource`), flights on Linux and on the wasm web target, and a prop
  changed on a live node pairing.

- **Web view rows: memory against SwiftUI** (2026-09-28, LLP 1020 §10, LLP 1068 §5.2; moved here 2026-10-23 as the take for LLP 1020.002, the browser shell, `rules/DEFERRED.md`). The feed's web view rows now tie SwiftUI on the iPhone (97.6 against 97.4 fps) and beat it on the iPad (100.5 against 95.8). Footprint is still higher: 70–76 MB against SwiftUI's 38–44 on the iPhone, 61–82 against 43–50 on the iPad. The two oddities are noise. Per-run peaks on the iPad are bimodal (about 62 or 81 MB) with and without near-only leaves, so the medians of three moved with the draw. System Trace's WebContent launch counts vary by ±100 between runs of one build (SwiftUI 290 and 479). A leaf, once made, stays until its row retires two viewports away, so it cannot churn at the quarter-viewport margin. Where the extra ~30 MB lives (the app's WebKit proxies, the text rasters, the pool) is unmeasured.

- **A followed end resolved by the host (deferred, 2026-10-05).** The
  proposal on `ide/doc/followed-end-one-target`
  (`issues/20261005-one-target-for-a-followed-end.md`): the correction names
  the end, and the host fits the extent with its own laid-out rows. LLP 1010
  §6.8's 2026-10-05 note does the cheap half instead: the measured row
  arrives 0.5 to 4 ms after the estimate, so a target before the motion's
  first frame is its one target. Take this up only if a measured report
  round trip is ever longer than a frame. Against it: a wire change on every
  host, and the extent with two sources of truth, so a later runner extent
  that disagrees (f32 rounding, a row whose measurement a cross-size check
  skipped) moves `contentSize` again at the end.

- **Chrome conformance steps for Paste and Match Style and for a clipboard event with nothing focused** (2026-10-21, PR #901; moved here 2026-10-23 as the take for LLP 1133.006's clipboard `write`, `rules/DEFERRED.md`). The driver's `type <id> paste` dispatches a synthetic `ClipboardEvent` at the focus or the target, so it never reaches `<body>`, and it has no Paste and Match Style; both were checked by hand in Chrome over CDP (`Input.dispatchKeyEvent` with `commands: ["paste"]` and `["pasteAndMatchStyle"]`). A driver operation for an edit command, sent as CDP's `commands`, would let `host/web-js/conformance` hold them.

# Graceful-overload consumers (2026-09-16)

LLP 1041 starts `messages-stress` and `completion-storm` as opt-in synthetic
workloads. Next: compare idle/loaded interaction samples on a real 120 Hz device,
then implement bounded completion pumping and admission/fairness where measured.
Keep module placement under LLP 1027.002; automatic windowing/image pressure and
physical-display native sweeps remain follow-ups; the first slice drives AppKit
and the Linux headless CPU renderer without claiming display frame timing.

- Linux textflow follow-up (LLP 1043.000 M3): carry resolved exclusions in immutable content-region worker requests and their accepted artifact identity. Ordinary definite-height text now flows; the opt-in content-region publication still owns its original unflowed geometry. Linux has no native text-selection UI, decoration painter or inline-link hit geometry yet; future consumers must use the accepted paragraph's fragments and original run metadata. Add discretionary-hyphen ink and clamp ellipsis to the flowed path (M3 clips at the band cap); justify currently uses start.

- Apple textflow follow-up (LLP 1043.000 M4): the shared walker now paints native paragraphs on macOS/iOS. A resolved flowed leaf retires an opt-in opaque content-region raster and returns the whole registration to native views, until a new registration. Carry exclusions and fragment geometry through the worker artifact before retaining region rastering for moving shapes; the current fallback trades that worker scheduling for correct fresh ink.
  2026-10-23 (lane q1022/u): Parked on kernel/ExactCore changes outside the assigned scope. No source change. The kernel's RegionTextRequest.with_request passes exclusions: &[] (kernel/src/region.rs); the region collector does the same in region/state.rs. Worker artifacts have no fragment origins or band geometry. Carrying only host flow ops cannot qualify a publication: the kernel must stamp exclusions into the request before the worker compute

- **Android Canvas transitions still expose partially cleared UI regions** (2026-10-17, Pixel 5). The Android host now retries only owed windows, records direct eligibility after rendering, and copies the current SurfaceView image before changing composition. Missing interior regions remain: 67/2,181 analyzed frames on `09f82dc75`, 61/2,106 after the host fixes (35-second seven-phase recordings, first five seconds excluded; this is not a meaningful improvement). The cold baseline also aborted in `vendor/wgpu-hal/src/vulkan/swapchain/native.rs:415`, destroying an acquire semaphore still held by a SurfaceTexture; the warmed baseline completed. GPU lifetime and HWUI buffer ownership remain to investigate with the GPU lane; no GPU files or detach waiting changed here. Rotation and background restoration recover; the native alert dims the world and HUD together. Local evidence: `target/castle-fixhost/{baseline-warm,seven-final}.mp4`, `*-frames-pixels.json`, `*-frames-contact.png`, `before-full-logcat.txt`, `lifecycle-result.json`, and `alert-open.png`. With the module settling a canvas before buffers replace its window (castle-fixgpu2, `2c7f7c97f`), the same recording finds 67/2,079: no change. In it and the host lane's two the bad frames fall throughout the three buffered phases (80% width, opacity 0.5, corner radius; 4 to 18 a 2.2-second phase), not at the switches between SurfaceView and buffers, and the missing regions are rectangular blocks of the page background inside the world, as if the reader drew a buffer whose frame the GPU had not finished or had begun again. A lead: patch 8 enables `VK_EXT_queue_family_foreign`, but nothing releases a buffer's image to `VK_QUEUE_FAMILY_FOREIGN_EXT` after its frame, which Vulkan's rules for external memory ask for before another API reads the image; another is a reader drawing a slot after the ring has freed it. Evidence: `target/castle-fixgpu2/` in that lane's worktree (`seven.mp4`, `seven-frames-pixels.json`, `seven-frames-contact.png`).

- **A Rust module's held request can't cross the module seam yet** (2026-10-23, held requests, theme2's sign-off). `logic/abi/src/lib.rs` refuses a `Request::held()` answer ("a held request cannot cross the Rust module seam yet"), as it refuses a deadline, so a Rust data module loaded as a module (the web build, native module hosts) can't mark a long poll held; an in-process Rust source can. Fix: a held mark in the seam's HTTP result kinds (an additive tag, older hosts refusing it before any effect, as tag 6 did), decoded into `Request::held`, then the source's `Answer::Later(Request::get(url).held())` works across the seam on every host. Tests: the seam round trip, and the held drive (scripts/held-fixture.mjs) with a Rust source.
- **The Android reader's display turn can fire a timer already due at the frozen clock** (2026-10-22, held requests round 2, Grok). `CanvasHost::agent` runs `frame_held` after every request (`host/linux/src/canvas/agent.rs`). With the driver owning the clock, `frame_held` calls `Presenter::advance(now)` when a runner timer, a scroll end or a hatch `after` is due at that now (`host/linux/src/canvas.rs`). So a `state` read there, and `clock held`'s polls, can fire such a timer, which Linux stdio, Apple and the web hosts never do. It's rare: a timer due at the frozen now has usually fired at the clock step that reached it. Fix: no advance on a read-only request.
- **Android's `iframe` ignores `sandbox`** (2026-10-21, LLP 1020.002 §10). `host/android/canvas/src/main/java/dev/exact/canvas/Natives.kt:513-519,693-698` makes a `WebView` with `javaScriptEnabled` and `allowFileAccess` and calls `loadUrl(src)`; nothing reads `sandbox`, so a sandboxed frame runs unsandboxed there, the fail-open class LLP 1020 §3.3 removed on the other hosts. Refuse a sandboxed `iframe` on Android by name, or serve it as Apple's wrapper does.
- **The web's wasm host has no extension modules** (2026-10-24, motion stage 0, LLP 1133.013). Games run on it, and it binds no package implementation and answers no agent `prefer extension` or `prefer permission`; `state.pending` holds no module ticket (`host/web/glue.js`'s `prefer` case). Beacons' tilt therefore works on the web neither for a person nor under the agent, and `game/games/beacons/proof.mjs` skips its tilt leg there, so the leg is unpinned (§10 drive 6). The bridge would mirror `host/apple/src/midi.rs`'s `extension`, with a JS loader for `packages/*/web/index.js` as `host/web-js/modules.js` has; `glue.js` is at its cap, so it goes in a module of its own.
- **Qualify the motion screen-frame browser oracle on hardware** (2026-10-26). Headless Chromium 156.0.8078.4 maps a virtual device gravity `[1, 2, 9]` to `[2, -1, 9]` at an emulated screen angle of 90°. Applying that inverse rotation to Safari on iPhone 16 Pro reverses the physical screen axes. The existing vector matrix remains; compare a real Generic Sensor device and the emulator before using the virtual screen-frame sensor as its oracle.
- **Motion, what the canvas seam leaves** (2026-10-24, LLP 1133.013 §13, "The canvas seam"). The Apple and Android contexts feed a canvas, and Beacons walks by tilt with the real `Motion.kt` bound on an Android emulator. The iPhone drive confirmed reversed roll; D6 now negates screen-frame x, with regression coverage for both roll directions and unchanged pitch. Owed: a physical tilt check of the corrected build (`packages/motion/test/phone-checklist.md`, item 9); ending an input when its canvas unmounts (its samples now go to no canvas, logged once); the web JS target's canvas inputs (no JS-target app has a canvas world); `headphoneMotion` with its usage key; Android's `HIGH_SAMPLING_RATE_SENSORS` derived from a grant line (without it a faster frequency is asked at 200 Hz); and D9's Android options and its status's `android` (null).
- **Wire the module seam drives into a lane** (2026-10-23, LLP 1133.004). `contract/cli/tests/bell/seams.mjs` proves the module seam with a module outside the tree, and `packages/notifications/test/drive.mjs` the notifications package, in a browser and under the agent, but no check or lane runs them; the async lane could, beside `host/web-js/conform.mjs`.
- **A heartbeat in transit can release a signalled wait in the design tool tests** (2026-10-24, dt/dtt round 2, Astra; theme2: left open). After `clock +N real until <signal>`, `clock held changes` returns on the next reply the host lands; a 204 heartbeat the server sent before the event (a write, a frame's registration) but the host landed after the signal releases it, and the event comes on the next ask (at least a second later), so the drive reads state without it. Repro (Astra): the real `readLongPoll` and `heldClock` with the heartbeat's delivery held past the signal: `settled: true`, the event unapplied. Sites: design-tool-poll, -canvas, -scrub and -kinds; each failing expect names it (`HEARTBEAT_RACE`, design-tool-harness.mjs). It can only fail a test, never pass one. Fix: one more `clock held changes` after the signal at each site (one heartbeat each, 3 to 5 s); if it fires in any gate, close it then as a main-red fix.
- **Completion Storm's web smoke fails at navigate-away on Linux** (2026-10-24, q24/count-plural on 450b). `bun apps/completion-storm/smoke.mjs web` against `bun scripts/agent.mjs web --app completion-storm`'s server stops at `smoke.mjs`'s `pendingCount === 0` after `navigate-away`: 128 lanes are still pending (a copy of origin/main b8ebd199fe's smoke, run before any change, fails the same way; a one-lane run leaves 1). Not yet known whether the unmount fails to cancel the held requests or the smoke needs the desktop fixture server (`EXACT_STORM_WEB_URL`) it was written against; the README's web smoke (`apps/completion-storm/README.md`) is the place to start.
- **An iOS textarea's placeholder follows its scroll offset; typed text does not** (2026-10-24, padded-textarea placeholder, Astra). `TextArea.draw` (`host/apple/Sources/ExactKit/IOS/TextAreaIOS.swift`) places the placeholder from `bounds`, whose origin is the text view's `contentOffset`, while typed text starts at the text container's fixed origin; an empty, heavily padded textarea scrolled off zero draws its placeholder where no first line would be. Draw from the container's origin (`textContainerInset` with no `bounds.origin`).
- **An iOS textarea lays out its whole placeholder however little shows** (2026-10-24, same review). The placeholder is drawn with unbounded height and clipped by the view, so a very long one (millions of characters; an AppKit probe took about 225 ms against 15 ms bounded) costs its full layout each draw. Bound the height at the view's height less the top inset plus one line.
- **The web JS target's module substitute lacks three of LLP 1133.030 D12's forms** (2026-10-26; 2026-10-28). `host/web-js/modules.js` mirrors the runner's declared substitute, `update` and a list's `tap` choices included, and holds a send to its `checks`' `one`, `min` and `nonEmpty`, but not a permission's `requires`, an app-scoped list's `due` time, or the check that an instant option is `after` now (its clock is not the epoch's). A module that uses them on the web needs them first, with `contract-difftest` comparing the two.

- **Ink readback RSS peaks** (LLP 1149 S0 C5). Desktop peaks rise during the CPU pixel copy, after device and glyph setup, with or without `ps` sampling. Zeroed output allocation did not remove them. Reuse the presenter's CPU pixmap and the device's staging buffer, then repeat the C4 memory protocol; see `host/ink/MEASUREMENTS.md`.

- **Ink D3D11 readback cost** (LLP 1149 S0 C6). On the NucBox, 1920×1080 C4 scrolling takes 3.920 ms with D3D11 versus 2.718 ms with wgpu DX12; readback, including completion, takes 3.238 ms versus 1.632 ms. Profile staging, completion and copying before optimizing the headless path. DirectComposition avoids that readback in the window. See `host/ink/MEASUREMENTS.md`, “NucBox”.

The minimal frame-health fixture and signed development/core/off builds are prepared locally. Use the phone when available and identify an above-120 Hz display. Run the independent presentation and observer off/on experiments, hidden/stationary/dragging overlay pairs, and energy/thermal checks. Callback/apply proxies and the debug scroll probe currently provide no presentation or battery certification.
- **Apple smoke mismatches before the frame-health rebase (2026-10-28).** On the original 15e54de3e1 base and iPhone Duo/iOS 27.1, both timing arms hold the stationary row at −125 pt, then release to about −77/−79 and settle at −73; two stationary-release assertions fail. On macOS 27.2, the canvas reference differs at 1.63% of pixels beyond the band (mean 1.64). Compare against the branch baseline before assigning a cause; focused frame-health and agent UI tests pass.

- **macOS live trackpad replay (LLP 1079.000, 2026-10-28).** Add a reliable completion acknowledgement for synthetic phased wheels before admitting them under platform timing. Immediate layout reads exposed zero or partial motion; posting the later phases first did not resolve it. The carrier currently refuses with `platform-trackpad-replay-unqualified`; ordinary wheels and frozen-clock functional replay remain available.
- **A minimized frame-health card (LLP 1079.000 §7.2).** The card is opaque and takes the touches within its bounds, so on a phone it covers over a third of the app. A minimized form, resting in its corner as the card does, would leave the app usable while it watches.
- **Frame-health publication from the hook (LLP 1079.000 §5.1).** `FrameHealth.record` and `landed` call `FrameHealthPublication.request`, which at most twice a second schedules a run-loop block and wakes the loop; Core Foundation copies the block, allocates and locks. An expiring agent lease also removes observers there. Move both out of the hook onto an existing pass, leaving it a flag.
- **Late work that lands unchanged leaves no hitch (LLP 1079.000 §1).** A frame whose owner batch changes nothing is never applied, so when it finishes late it counts as a late frame but the detail ring records no apply-deadline event. Record the late frame's start, deadline and landing as an event of its own.

- **NucBox sleep/wake needs a verified wake-timer design** (2026-10-31,
  LLP 1149.001 G7). The unattended October 30 sleep journey never woke;

  plan, retain its due time and wake-source evidence, and provide an
  independent recovery path. A timer handle alone does not prove the machine
  or physical display will wake. Keep this separate from the AMD-hook crash.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: native-field pointer delivery** (Claude review 3,
  `target/review-claude-s1w.md`, `98cff0bdfa`). RichEdit's handled mouse messages
  make winit skip dispatch, so field and ancestor clicks never reach the page.
  Keep the native edit and let the pointer sequence reach the presenter.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: app keys from native fields** (Claude review 10).
  Forward a known editing set to RichEdit; let Escape, function keys and
  app chords outside that set reach winit regardless of RichEdit's answer.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: composition ownership** (Claude review 4). `SES_USECTF`
  enables TSF while the host tracks IMM messages. Drop it and qualify IMM,
  or track composition from the editor before claiming app updates cannot
  overwrite a composition.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: UIA focus and accessible name** (Sol review 7,
  `target/review-sol-s1w.md`, `98cff0bdfa`). `TxSetFocus` focuses only the
  HWND; bridge it to logical field focus. Pass the computed accessible name
  to `EM_SETUIANAME`, separately from the placeholder.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: input event causes** (Sol review 4; Claude review 15).
  Carry `deleteByCut`, `insertFromPaste`, `historyUndo` and
  `deleteContentBackward`, including composition commits, from the message
  that caused the edit. Every native edit currently becomes `insertText`.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: selection direction** (Sol review 5). `EM_EXGETSEL`
  normalizes endpoints; use TOM to retain the active end through programmatic
  backward selection and Shift+Arrow continuation.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: field message and paint churn** (Claude review 15).
  Avoid per-frame `EM_SETUIANAME` and setting `changed` on every routed
  message, including key-up and mouse movement with no edit.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: product qualification** (Sol review 7 and checked
  IME path; Claude reviews 3, 4, 10, 11). Complete Shelf's native UIA tree,
  Japanese IME placement/reconversion and Narrator journeys, then Excord's
  composer, focus traversal and growing/wrapping text. The flag stays off
  until those drives pass; Unicode insertion is not an IME drive.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: startup observer and corrected A/B** (Claude reviews
  1, 7, 8; Sol review 8). The old log and observer bracket different writes:
  2/100 logged entry-arm intervals exceed 50 ms (138.547 and 77.718 ms),
  versus 0/100 after layout, with two-sided Fisher p = 0.4975. Neither tail
  has a stack; one 2.646 ms interval has a late capture. These are not yet
  established as runner-to-layout work. A queued completion suppresses capture.
  Pre-spawn a helper blocked on an event, or capture threads and contexts
  in-process without a VA clone and symbolize offline; the measured VA clone
  pauses threads for about 6.026 ms and process spawning adds latency. Probe
  `LdrLockLoaderLock` with `TRY_ONLY` on the watchdog thread at timeout to
  test loader-lock ownership without snapshotting. Rerun the A/B on corrected
  marks only after that redesign, retaining first-pixel differences between
  arms and marking captured launches as perturbed. No startup fix is earned.
- **Stage 1w chunk B: M9 axis mapping** (Claude review 13; Sol's checked
  DirectWrite path). The candidate maps no axes. Use
  `IDWriteFontResource::CreateFontFace` with design-space values, obtained by
  un-normalizing Parley's coordinates (including `avar` and `fvar`) or carrying
  the requested values. Repeat the audit before admitting variable faces.
